NSE_GR_EXACT_SECTOR_EQUIVALENCE
VERSION = 1.6.0
DATE = 2026-11-13
MODE = B_TIER_DERIVATION
STATUS = ! B_TIER

ROOT:
For every viscosity nu>0, auxiliary constant rho!=0, and reference time t0,
analytic periodic incompressible 3D Navier--Stokes solution germs modulo
pressure gauge are in explicit bijection with a normalized sector of marked
analytic 5D vacuum Einstein metric germs.

The Einstein sector is defined entirely by the marked boundary metric jet,
fixed parameters, and torus differential/Fourier operators. Its definition
contains no NSE solution predicate and no definition as the image of a lift.

Fix:
  nu in (0,infinity), rho in R\{0}, t0 in R.
  T^3 := (R/Z)^3, with normalized spatial measure.
  J_delta := (t0-delta,t0+delta), delta>0.
  Sigma_delta := J_delta x T^3.
  M_epsilon,delta := (-epsilon,epsilon)_r x Sigma_delta.

Coordinates:
  x^0=t; x^i=x_i, i=1,2,3; a,b=0,1,2,3.
  eta_ab=diag(-1,1,1,1).
  partial^a=eta^{ab}partial_b.
  Box_eta=-partial_t^2+Delta_x.

Indices:
  Boundary spacetime indices are raised/lowered with eta at r=0.
  Spatial indices are raised/lowered with delta_ij.
  Repeated indices are summed.

Spatial operations:
  <f>(t):=integral_{T^3}f(t,x)dx.
  (div A)_i:=partial_j A_ij for a spatial symmetric tensor A.
  Z(v)_ij:=partial_i v_j+partial_j v_i.
  TF(A):=A-(tr_x A)I_3/3.
  A:C:=sum_ij A_ij C_ij.

Spatial norms:
  Vector norms are Euclidean. For real or complex spatial matrices,
    |A|_F^2:=sum_ij |A_ij|^2.
  Spatial tensor norms written |A|, ||A||_2, ||grad A||_2, or ||Curl A||_2
  use the componentwise Euclidean/Frobenius norm and normalized torus measure.
  Derivative indices are included in the corresponding finite sum.
  The same convention applies to spatial matrix Fourier coefficients.
  L(t)=||Sym grad V(t)||_{L^infinity,operator} uses the induced Euclidean
  operator norm, explicitly distinguished from the Frobenius norm.
  Lorentzian contractions remain the explicitly displayed eta contractions;
  they are not positive Euclidean norms of spacetime tensors.

Mean-zero inverse Laplacian:
  Delta_0^{-1}f is the unique mean-zero periodic solution of Delta q=f
  when <f>=0.
  For k in Z^3\{0}, its Fourier multiplier is -1/(4*pi^2*|k|^2).
  Its zero Fourier mode is zero.

Leray projection:
  P:=I-grad Delta_0^{-1}div.
  P_hat(k)=I-k tensor k/|k|^2 for k!=0; P_hat(0)=I.
  Spatially constant vector fields are retained.

NSE residual:
  R_nu(u,p):=partial_tu+(u.grad)u+grad p-nu Delta u.
  NSE_nu(u,p) :<=> div u=0 and R_nu(u,p)=0.

Analyticity:
  C^omega means joint real analyticity in all displayed real variables.
  The NSE input is jointly analytic in (t,x), including t=t0.

NSE germs:
  Two analytic periodic pairs (u,p) represent the same pressure-gauge germ
  if, on a common smaller J_delta x T^3,
      u'=u, p'=p+a(t)
  for a real-analytic scalar a(t).
  N^omega_{nu,t0} denotes the set of such germs satisfying NSE_nu.
  The gauge representative <p>=0 is unique.

Metric germs:
  g=dr^2+h_ab(r,t,x)dx^a dx^b is real analytic and Lorentzian on some
  M_epsilon,delta, with h_ab(0,t,x)=eta_ab.
  Normal n=partial_r; K_ab=(1/2)partial_rh_ab|_{r=0}.
  Equality of metric germs means equality after restriction to a common
  smaller marked product collar.
  Coordinates, the t/x splitting, torus marking, and normal orientation
  are fixed. No quotient by arbitrary bulk diffeomorphisms is taken.

Vacuum convention:
  Ric(g)=0; equivalently G(g)=0 in dimension 5.
  Cosmological constant Lambda=0; bulk matter stress tensor=0.
  rho is an auxiliary boundary parameter.

=======================================================================
1. EINSTEIN SECTOR: DEFINITION FROM THE BOUNDARY JET
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP01
  V15_REFINEMENT = FB09, VF05; original sector is precisely the full-stress zero subset.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME21-ME22.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V14_REFINEMENT = HD08, GD07; unchanged canonical constitutive predicate and full-slice operators.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME20.
  V14_LOOKUP = Sections 39-43; exact source hypotheses remain mandatory.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML17-ML19; ME01, ME04, ME05
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP02 actual analytic P and scalar Poisson inverse; NP03 Lorentz/STF tensor definitions.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

For any metric germ with the boundary structure in Section 0, define:

  kappa:=tr_eta K=-K_00+sum_i K_ii,
  beta:=-sum_i K_ii,
  w_i:=K_0i,
  q:=Delta_0^{-1}(beta-<beta>),
  v:=P w.

Define the symmetric trace-free constitutive tensor

  F_nu[K]:=TF(K_spatial+v tensor v-nu Z(v)+Hess_x q).       (1.1)

Every quantity in (1.1) is determined by g and its marked boundary jet.
Neither an auxiliary scalar chosen externally nor an NSE predicate occurs.

Define G^omega_{nu,rho,t0} to be the set of these metric germs satisfying:

  G1. Ric(g)=0.
  G2. beta(t0,x)=rho for every x in T^3.
  G3. partial_t beta(t0,x)=0 for every x in T^3.
  G4. F_nu[K](t,x)=0 as a boundary germ.

The Gaussian form, flat induced metric, analytic regularity, marking, and
normal orientation are part of the ambient metric-germ definition.

Derived normalization, proved in Section 4:
  <beta>(t)=rho,
  q(t0,x)=0,
  q_t(t0,x)=0.

2. EXACT ROOT STATEMENTS
=======================================================================

  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML52.

  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

THEOREM T_EQ: NSE_GR_EXACT_SECTOR_EQUIVALENCE.

For every nu>0, rho!=0, t0 in R, there exist explicit maps

  L_{nu,rho,t0}: N^omega_{nu,t0} -> G^omega_{nu,rho,t0},
  R_{nu,rho,t0}: G^omega_{nu,rho,t0} -> N^omega_{nu,t0}

such that

  R_{nu,rho,t0} o L_{nu,rho,t0}=id,
  L_{nu,rho,t0} o R_{nu,rho,t0}=id.                       (2.1)

Consequently

  N^omega_{nu,t0} ~= G^omega_{nu,rho,t0}.                 (2.2)

The reconstruction is

  R(g)=[v,p_*],
  v=P K_{0 bullet},
  p_*=-K_00-(2rho+|v|^2)/3-q_tt.                        (2.3)

Equivalently,
  R(g)=[v,p_0(v)],
  p_0(v)=-Delta_0^{-1}partial_i partial_j(v_i v_j).       (2.4)

THEOREM T_LIFT_IFF: EXACT LIFT CONDITIONS.

Fix an arbitrary jointly analytic periodic pair (u,p) near
{t0} x T^3, with no NSE hypothesis initially imposed. Define S_rho and
K^0 as in Sections 6 and 8. Then

  NSE_nu(u,p)
    <=>
  there exist positive delta,epsilon and jointly analytic psi,g such that
    Ric(g)=0,
    g=dr^2+h, h|_{r=0}=eta,
    psi(t0,x)=psi_t(t0,x)=0,
    K_ab=K^0_ab[u,p;rho]-partial_a partial_b psi.         (2.5)

For each fixed representative (u,p), the pair of germs (psi,g) in (2.5)
is unique. The metric germ depends only on the pressure-gauge class.

THEOREM T_PROJECT: EXACT PROJECTION CRITERION.

For any C^infinity marked vacuum metric with flat periodic
boundary, define v=P K_{0 bullet}. Then

  exists periodic C^infinity p: NSE_nu(v,p)
    <=>
  C_nu[K]:=P div(K_spatial+v tensor v-nu Z(v))=0.         (2.6)

T_PROJECT requires no analyticity. The analytic existence of L and the
canonical bijection T_EQ use the stronger hypotheses stated above.

  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

LEMMA A1.
Spatial averaging, Delta_0^{-1} on mean-zero fields, P, and finite spatial
or time differentiation preserve jointly analytic periodic germs.

Proof.
Joint real analyticity near {t0} x T^3 and compactness give, after shrinking,
a common complex time neighborhood and a positive complex spatial strip.
On a smaller such neighborhood the spatial Fourier coefficients are
holomorphic in time and bounded by C exp(-a|k|), uniformly in time.
The multipliers of Delta_0^{-1}, P, and their finite spatial derivatives
have at most polynomial growth. They preserve exponential decay after
shrinking the strip. The differentiated series converge normally there.
Spatial averaging selects k=0. Time differentiation is holomorphic
differentiation on a smaller complex time neighborhood. QED.

LEMMA A2.
For periodic vector fields w,
  div(Pw)=0,
  P grad f=0,
  P v=v if div v=0,
  P commutes with partial_t and Delta,
  <Pw>=<w>.

Proof.
For k!=0, P_hat(k) is the orthogonal projection onto k-perpendicular;
P_hat(0)=I. The stated identities follow mode by mode. QED.

4. VACUUM CODAZZI AND INTRINSIC RECONSTRUCTION
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP04
  V15_REFINEMENT = FB05-FB06, PL07; constrained-boundary and linearized reconstruction.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML52; ME21.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V14_REFINEMENT = HD07-HD08; complete pressure/stress residual fibres.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME20.
  V14_LOOKUP = Sections 39-43; exact source hypotheses remain mandatory.
  V13_REFINEMENT = GE07-GE11 and CG01-CG04; derived geometry and canonical pressure.
  V13_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME18.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = AF07-AF10; actual projection and pressure identities.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME16-ME17.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML07-ML12, ML17-ML19; ME01, ME02, ME06, ME07
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP04 boundary residual algebra; NP06 vacuum Codazzi with the declared Lorentzian sign convention.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

Define the boundary tensor

  B_ab:=kappa eta_ab-K_ab.                              (4.1)

Index conversion gives

  B^{00}=beta,
  B^{0i}=w_i,
  B^{ij}=kappa delta_ij-K_ij.                           (4.2)

Vacuum Gauss--Codazzi on the flat timelike boundary gives

  partial_a B^{ab}=0,
  K_ab K^{ab}-kappa^2=0.                               (4.3)

For the 0 component of (4.3),

  beta_t+div w=0.                                      (4.4)

Periodic integration gives

  partial_t<beta>=0.                                   (4.5)

Under beta(t0,x)=rho, (4.5) yields <beta>=rho, and hence

  Delta q=beta-rho,
  div w=-Delta q_t,
  v=P w=w+grad q_t.                                    (4.6)

Under beta_t(t0,x)=0,

  q(t0,x)=0,
  q_t(t0,x)=0.                                         (4.7)

For any scalar q define

  C^{ab}[q]:=partial^a partial^b q-eta^{ab}Box_eta q.     (4.8)

Commutation of flat derivatives gives partial_a C^{ab}[q]=0. Its components
are

  C^{00}=Delta q,
  C^{0i}=-partial_i q_t,
  C^{ij}=q_ij+(q_tt-Delta q)delta_ij.                    (4.9)

Consequently B-C[q] has components

  (B-C[q])^{00}=rho,
  (B-C[q])^{0i}=v_i,
  (B-C[q])^{ij}=M_ij,

where

  M_ij:=B^{ij}-q_ij+(Delta q-q_tt)delta_ij.              (4.10)

Its conservation gives

  div v=0,
  partial_t v_i+partial_j M_ij=0.                       (4.11)

Define

  p_*:=(tr_x M-|v|^2)/3.                               (4.12)

Since kappa=-K_00-beta and beta=rho+Delta q,

  tr_x M
    =3kappa+beta+2Delta q-3q_tt
    =-3K_00-2rho-3q_tt.

Therefore

  p_*=-K_00-(2rho+|v|^2)/3-q_tt,                        (4.13)

as asserted in (2.3).

5. EXACT RESIDUAL AND REVERSE IMPLICATION
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP05
  V15_REFINEMENT = FB06-FB10, VF05-VF06, PL07; exact full-stress and linearized residuals.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML54; ME21.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V14_REFINEMENT = HD05-HD08; scalar, solenoidal and divergence-invisible residual components.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME20.
  V14_LOOKUP = Sections 39-43; exact source hypotheses remain mandatory.
  V13_REFINEMENT = CG01-CG04; orthogonal actual-equation residual.
  V13_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME18.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = AF09-AF10; ST07-ST08 for pressure correction and projected forcing.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML26-ML28; ME16.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML07-ML12, ML17-ML19; ME02, ME06-ME10
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP04 exact pressure and forced residual; NP16 temporal Fourier/RHS adapter if ME10 is used.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

By div v=0, tr_x Z(v)=0. Equations (4.10)--(4.12) imply

  M-v tensor v-p_*I_3+nu Z(v)
    =TF(B_spatial-v tensor v+nu Z(v)-Hess_x q)
    =-F_nu[K].                                         (5.1)

Thus

  M=v tensor v+p_*I_3-nu Z(v)-F_nu[K].                  (5.2)

Substitute (5.2) into (4.11). Since div Z(v)=Delta v,

  div v=0,
  R_nu(v,p_*)=div F_nu[K].                             (5.3)

In particular,

  F_nu[K]=0 => NSE_nu(v,p_*).                           (5.4)

All reconstructed fields are jointly analytic by Lemma A1. Therefore

  R: G^omega_{nu,rho,t0} -> N^omega_{nu,t0},
  R(g)=[v,p_*]

is well-defined.

Equivalent constitutive form.
Under <beta>=rho and div v=0,

  tr_x(K_spatial+v tensor v-nu Z(v)+Hess_x q)
    =-rho+|v|^2.

Hence F_nu[K]=0 is equivalent to the explicit boundary identity

  K_ij=((|v|^2-rho)/3)delta_ij
       -v_i v_j+nu Z(v)_ij-q_ij.                       (5.5)

Pressure-gauge identification.
Taking the divergence of NSE_nu(v,p_*) gives

  Delta p_*=-partial_i partial_j(v_i v_j).

Therefore

  p_*=p_0(v)+a(t),
  p_0(v)=-Delta_0^{-1}partial_i partial_j(v_i v_j),       (5.6)

with a=<p_*> jointly analytic in time.

=======================================================================
6. NSE CONSERVATION TENSOR AND HAMILTONIAN DEFECT
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP06
  V15_REFINEMENT = FB01-FB02, PL03-PL05; actual forced conservation and polarized Hamiltonian.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML54; ME21.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML07-ML13, ML15-ML19; ME01, ME02
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP03 finite tensor/Hamiltonian algebra; NP04 product/divergence calculation in actual fields.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

For an arbitrary analytic periodic pair (u,p), define

  S_rho^{00}=rho,
  S_rho^{0i}=S_rho^{i0}=u_i,
  S_rho^{ij}=A_ij:=u_i u_j+p delta_ij-nu Z(u)_ij.        (6.1)

Direct differentiation gives

  partial_a S_rho^{a0}=div u,
  partial_a S_rho^{ai}
    =R_nu(u,p)_i+u_i div u-nu partial_i div u.           (6.2)

Therefore

  partial_a S_rho^{ab}=0 for all b
    <=> NSE_nu(u,p).                                   (6.3)

For a symmetric contravariant boundary tensor T, define

  H(T):=T_ab T^{ab}-(tr_eta T)^2/3.                     (6.4)

For divergence-free u and D(u):=Z(u)/2,

  tr_eta S_rho=-rho+|u|^2+3p,
  S_rho,ab S_rho^{ab}=rho^2-2|u|^2+|u tensor u+pI-2nu D|^2,

  H(S_rho)
    =(2/3)rho^2+(2/3)rho|u|^2+2rho p
     +(2/3)|u|^4-2|u|^2
     -4nu u_i D_ij u_j+4nu^2|D|^2.                     (6.5)

Even the zero NSE solution u=0,p=0 satisfies H(S_rho)=2rho^2/3!=0.
Thus the scalar constraint is an actual additional equation.

  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML07-ML10, ML13-ML21
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP05 actual scalar analytic CK existence and uniqueness. AnalyticAt.div only proves analytic division after nonvanishing.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

Let

  C^{ab}[psi]=partial^a partial^b psi-eta^{ab}Box_eta psi,
  B^{ab}=S_rho^{ab}+C^{ab}[psi].                        (7.1)

Then

  partial_a C^{ab}[psi]=0,
  partial_a B^{ab}=partial_a S_rho^{ab}.                (7.2)

Write psi_ab=partial_a partial_b psi and z=Box_eta psi. Direct contractions
give

  tr_eta C=-3z,
  C_ab C^{ab}-(tr_eta C)^2/3=psi_ab psi^{ab}-z^2,
  S_ab C^{ab}-(tr_eta S)(tr_eta C)/3=S^{ab}psi_ab.

Consequently

  H(S+C[psi])
    =H(S)+2S^{ab}psi_ab+psi_ab psi^{ab}-(Box_eta psi)^2. (7.3)

Set Q=Hess_x psi, b=grad psi_t, d=Delta psi. Then

  psi_ab psi^{ab}-(Box_eta psi)^2
    =2d psi_tt-2|b|^2+|Q|^2-d^2.

Thus H(B)=0 is exactly

  2(rho+Delta psi)psi_tt
    +H(S_rho)+4u.grad psi_t+2A:Hess_x psi
    -2|grad psi_t|^2+|Hess_x psi|^2-(Delta psi)^2=0.     (7.4)

Equivalently,

  psi_tt=
    [-H(S_rho)-4u.grad psi_t-2A:Hess_x psi
      +2|grad psi_t|^2-|Hess_x psi|^2+(Delta psi)^2]
    /[2(rho+Delta psi)].                               (7.5)

Prescribe the analytic Cauchy data

  psi(t0,x)=0,
  psi_t(t0,x)=0.                                       (7.6)

The highest pure t-derivative coefficient at these data is

  partial H(B)/partial psi_tt=2rho!=0.                  (7.7)

Every derivative on the right of (7.5) has total order at most two and
t-order strictly below two. The right side is analytic in all arguments
near the data (7.6). The second-order Cauchy--Kowalevski theorem therefore
gives a unique analytic local scalar germ.

At each point of {t0} x T^3 the equations and data are compatible under
periodic coordinate transitions. Local uniqueness identifies the germs
on overlaps. A finite spatial cover with compactly contained subcharts
and compactness of T^3 give a common time cylinder J_delta x T^3.

After restriction,

  rho+Delta psi!=0

throughout that cylinder. Thus the solution is actual and analytic;
the assertion is not restricted to formal Taylor coefficients.

No smallness condition on the analytic input (u,p) is used.

=======================================================================
8. COMPLETED GAUSS--CODAZZI DATA
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP08
  V15_REFINEMENT = FB04-FB05, PL04-PL06; independent constrained K and its exact linear constraints.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML54.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V13_REFINEMENT = GE07-GE08; actual Hamiltonian and Codazzi components.
  V13_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML31-ML33.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = CJ01, CJ05-CJ09; finite tensor and initial-jet identities.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML31-ML33.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML07-ML13, ML15-ML19
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP03 trace reversal and Hamiltonian algebra; NP06 Gauss-Codazzi identification.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

Assume now NSE_nu(u,p). With psi from Section 7,

  partial_a B^{ab}=0,
  H(B)=0.                                             (8.1)

Set

  K_ab=(tr_eta B)eta_ab/3-B_ab.                         (8.2)

Taking the trace gives

  kappa=tr_eta K=(tr_eta B)/3,
  B_ab=kappa eta_ab-K_ab.

A direct contraction yields

  K_ab K^{ab}-kappa^2=H(B)=0,                           (8.3)
  partial^a(K_ab-kappa eta_ab)=-partial^aB_ab=0.         (8.4)

These are the vacuum constraints for a flat timelike hypersurface with
spacelike unit normal in a five-dimensional Lorentzian metric [R2,R3].

Define

  K^0_ab[u,p;rho]:=(tr_eta S_rho)eta_ab/3-(S_rho)_ab.

Since tr_eta C=-3Box_eta psi, the trace terms cancel:

  K_ab=K^0_ab[u,p;rho]-psi_ab.                          (8.5)

Explicitly,

  K_00=-(2rho+|u|^2)/3-p-psi_tt,
  K_0i=u_i-psi_ti,
  K_ij=((|u|^2-rho)/3)delta_ij
       -u_i u_j+nu Z(u)_ij-psi_ij.                    (8.6)

In particular,

  beta=rho+Delta psi,
  w=u-grad psi_t,
  P w=u,
  q=psi-<psi>.                                        (8.7)

The zero scalar data (7.6) imply

  beta(t0,x)=rho,
  beta_t(t0,x)=0.

Equation (8.6), together with Hess_x q=Hess_x psi, implies F_nu[K]=0.

=======================================================================
9. ANALYTIC RICCI-FLAT EXTENSION
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP09
  V16_REFINEMENT = CV02,CV04; exact rigid pullback and unique re-completion.
  V16_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML52.
  V16_LOOKUP = Sections 51-55; exact source premises and target adapters retained.
  V15_REFINEMENT = FB05, PL01-PL02, PL04-PL06; exact boundary-vacuum inverse and linearized radial equation.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML54.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V14_REFINEMENT = FC07-FC10; GD01-GD04, GD06; actual radial jet and vacuum collar.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML43-ML48; ME19.
  V14_LOOKUP = Sections 39-43; exact source hypotheses remain mandatory.
  V13_REFINEMENT = GE01-GE12 plus CK01-CK03,CK05-CK12; all geometry and CK steps decomposed.
  V13_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML36,ML40-ML42.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = CJ05-CJ16; radial CK2, inverse jets, zero-jet propagation, compact collar.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML26-ML27, ML29-ML33.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML07-ML21
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP06 Gaussian curvature/Bianchi formulas; NP07 radial analytic existence, subsidiary uniqueness, signature and compact collar.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

Prescribe

  h_ab(0,t,x)=eta_ab,
  partial_rh_ab(0,t,x)=2K_ab(t,x).                      (9.1)

For g=dr^2+h, define off the boundary

  K_ab(r,t,x)=(1/2)partial_rh_ab,
  kappa(r,t,x)=tr_h K.

Use the curvature convention for which

  Ric[g]_ab=Ric[h]_ab-partial_rK_ab-kappa K_ab+2K_ac K^c_b,
  Ric[g]_ra=D_bK^b_a-D_a kappa,
  Ric[g]_rr=-partial_r kappa-K_ab K^{ab}.              (9.2)

Here D and all off-boundary contractions are defined by h.

The equations Ric[g]_ab=0 are equivalent to

  partial_r^2h_ab
    =2Ric[h]_ab-2kappa K_ab+4K_ac K^c_b.                (9.3)

The right side is analytic in h, h^{-1}, first r-derivatives, and tangential
derivatives through order two near the initial data. In particular,
det h(0)=det eta=-1!=0. The right side contains no second r-derivatives.

The second-order analytic Cauchy--Kowalevski theorem gives a unique local
solution with data (9.1) [R1, Section II.A; R2, equations (2)--(8)].

Choose a closed time interval J_bar compactly contained in the scalar
existence interval, with t0 in its interior. Local analytic uniqueness
glues the solutions over J_bar x T^3. Compactness gives epsilon>0 and a
smaller open J containing t0 on which one metric exists on

  (-epsilon,epsilon) x J x T^3.

Continuity preserves nondegeneracy and the Lorentzian signature after
restriction. Thus g has one timelike direction.

Constraint propagation.
Define

  E:=G[g]_rr,
  M_a:=G[g]_ra.

Under Ric[g]_ab=0,

  E=Ric[g]_rr/2,
  G[g]_ab=-E h_ab.

The contracted Bianchi identity gives

  partial_rE=-D_aM^a-2kappa E,
  partial_rM_a=D_aE-kappa M_a.                         (9.4)

The Gaussian scalar identity is

  G[g]_rr=(kappa^2-K_ab K^{ab}-R[h])/2.                (9.5)

At r=0, R[h]=0; (8.3)--(8.4) therefore imply

  E(0,t,x)=0,
  M_a(0,t,x)=0.

System (9.4) is homogeneous, analytic, and solved for the first pure
r-derivatives. Analytic uniqueness implies E=M_a=0. Thus G[g]=0.
In dimension 5,

  tr_g G=(1-5/2)R[g]=-(3/2)R[g],

so G[g]=0 implies Ric[g]=0 exactly.

The resulting metric lies in G^omega_{nu,rho,t0} by Section 8.

10. PRESSURE GAUGE AND WELL-DEFINED LIFT

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP10
  V16_REFINEMENT = CV02-CV04; actual pressure-gauge-compatible transformations.
  V16_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML52,ML59.
  V16_LOOKUP = Sections 51-55; exact source premises and target adapters retained.
  V15_REFINEMENT = FB07, PL02, PL08; full completion fibres and exact parameter/linear gauge action.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML54.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V14_REFINEMENT = GD07-GD09; actual source restriction, gauge cancellation and quotient descent.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML49.
  V14_LOOKUP = Sections 39-43; exact source hypotheses remain mandatory.
  V13_REFINEMENT = CG06-CG09; normalized primitive, exact tensor gauge and metric agreement.
  V13_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML34-ML39.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = CJ04, CJ08, CJ14-CJ15; uniqueness of existing analytic completions.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML29-ML33.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML07-ML11, ML22, ML23
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP08 analytic time primitive and pressure gauge action; NP05/NP07 uniqueness; NP09 representative independence.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

Given an NSE representative (u,p), Sections 7--9 construct a unique
pair of analytic germs (psi,g) with the chosen zero scalar data.

Let p'=p+a(t), with a analytic, and let f satisfy

  f''=-a,
  f(t0)=f'(t0)=0.

Set psi'=psi+f(t). Then

  S_rho[u,p']-S_rho[u,p]
    has only spatial components a(t)delta_ij,
  C[psi']-C[psi]
    has only spatial components f''(t)delta_ij.

Hence

  S_rho[u,p']+C[psi']=S_rho[u,p]+C[psi].                (10.1)

The tensors B,K are unchanged. The scalar psi' satisfies the same zero
Cauchy normalization for the new representative; uniqueness in Section 7
identifies it with the scalar selected by that construction. Radial
uniqueness in Section 9 identifies the metric germs.

Consequently

  L([u,p]):=g

is independent of the pressure representative. Restriction of input
representatives produces the same output germ by both uniqueness steps.
Thus L:N^omega_{nu,t0}->G^omega_{nu,rho,t0} is well-defined.

11. TWO INVERSE IDENTITIES
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP11
  V16_REFINEMENT = CV06,CV08; explicit constant-mean product and source adapter.
  V16_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML24-ML25,ML59.
  V16_LOOKUP = Sections 51-55; exact source premises and target adapters retained.
  V15_REFINEMENT = FB08-FB09, PL07-PL08; forced and linearized two-inverse bodies.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML54.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V14_REFINEMENT = GD07-GD10; complete product-domain inverse identities.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML49.

  V13_REFINEMENT = CG05,CG09-CG10; same root through normalized velocity germs.
  V13_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML34-ML39; ME18.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = CJ04, CJ08, CJ14-CJ15; both inverse-germ comparisons.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML29-ML33.

  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP09 both inverse identities; NP05/NP07 uniqueness; no imported Equiv supplies these bodies.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

PROPOSITION I1. R o L=id.

Proof.
For the lifted B=S_rho[u,p]+C[psi], equations (8.7) give

  q=psi-<psi>,
  v=P(u-grad psi_t)=u.

The reconstructed spatial tensor is

  M=u tensor u+(p+partial_t^2<psi>)I_3-nu Z(u).

Therefore

  p_*=p+partial_t^2<psi>,
  [v,p_*]=[u,p].

Hence R(L([u,p]))=[u,p]. QED.

PROPOSITION I2. L o R=id.

Proof.
Let g belong to G^omega_{nu,rho,t0}. Sections 4--5 reconstruct
(v,p_*) satisfying NSE_nu(v,p_*). Equations (5.1)--(5.2), with F_nu=0,
give the exact decomposition

  B=S_rho[v,p_*]+C[q].                                (11.1)

Vacuum Hamiltonian implies H(B)=0, so q solves precisely the scalar
equation (7.5) for the input (v,p_*).

The normalization conditions G2--G3 imply q(t0)=q_t(t0)=0.
At those initial data the normal coefficient is 2rho!=0. Thus, after a
possible common restriction, scalar analytic uniqueness yields

  q=psi_{v,p_*}.

The metric g and the constructed metric L([v,p_*]) consequently have
identical h|_{r=0}=eta and identical partial_rh|_{r=0}=2K.
Both satisfy the radial analytic system (9.3). Its uniqueness gives

  L(R(g))=g

as marked analytic germs. QED.

THEOREM T_EQ follows from Propositions I1 and I2.

Forward direction of T_LIFT_IFF.
If NSE_nu(u,p), Sections 7--9 give (2.5).

Reverse direction.
Suppose (2.5) holds. Form B_ab=kappa eta_ab-K_ab. Since
K=K^0[u,p;rho]-Hess_{t,x}psi, trace inversion gives

  B=S_rho[u,p]+C[psi].                                (12.1)

Vacuum Codazzi implies partial_aB^{ab}=0. Since div C[psi]=0,
partial_a S_rho^{ab}=0. Equation (6.3) gives NSE_nu(u,p).

Uniqueness.
The vacuum Hamiltonian equation gives H(S_rho+C[psi])=0. Together with
the prescribed zero scalar data, rho!=0, and analytic regularity, this
forces the scalar germ from Section 7. The completed K is therefore
fixed, and radial uniqueness forces the metric germ from Section 9.

Thus the quantified biconditional (2.5) and uniqueness both hold. QED.

13. WEAKER PROJECTION SECTORS AND THEIR EXACT RESIDUALS
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP13
  V16_REFINEMENT = SF03,VI06; exact projected force and independent stress components.
  V16_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML55-ML58; ME23-ME24.
  V16_LOOKUP = Sections 51-55; exact source premises and target adapters retained.
  V15_REFINEMENT = VF05-VF06; three strict admission inclusions with actual vacuum witnesses.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME21-ME22.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V14_REFINEMENT = HD01-HD08; exact four-component distinction between admission predicates.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME20.
  V14_LOOKUP = Sections 39-43; exact source hypotheses remain mandatory.
  V13_REFINEMENT = CG01-CG04 add the exact least-residual pressure normalization.
  V13_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME18.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = ST01-ST07; orthogonal stress distinction between projected and canonical admission.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME16.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML01-ML06, ML17-ML19; ME01, ME02, ME04, ME06-ME10
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP02/NP04 full physical projection and pressure adjustment. P div F=0 does not imply F=0.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

Let g be a C^infinity marked vacuum metric with the same flat
periodic boundary. Set w=K_{0 bullet}, v=P w, and A=K_spatial.

The spatial Codazzi equation is

  partial_t w+grad kappa-div A=0.

After applying P,

  partial_t v=P div A,
  div v=0.                                            (13.1)

Define

  C_nu[K]:=P div(A+v tensor v-nu Z(v)).                 (13.2)

Then

  C_nu[K]
    =partial_t v+P div(v tensor v)-nu Delta v.           (13.3)

If NSE_nu(v,p), projection gives C_nu[K]=0.
Conversely, if C_nu[K]=0, define p_0(v) by (5.6). Then

  div(v tensor v)+grad p_0(v)=P div(v tensor v),

so (13.3) gives NSE_nu(v,p_0(v)). This proves T_PROJECT.

Under the beta normalization and the definitions of Section 4,

  R_nu(v,p_*)=div F_nu[K],
  C_nu[K]=P div F_nu[K].                              (13.4)

The second identity follows because P div annihilates both scalar
identity tensors and Hess_x q.

Exact hierarchy:

  F_nu[K]=0
    => div F_nu[K]=0
    => P div F_nu[K]=0.

  NSE_nu(v,p_*) <=> div F_nu[K]=0.

  exists p: NSE_nu(v,p) <=> P div F_nu[K]=0.

If only P div F_nu[K]=0, an admissible pressure is

  p=p_*-Delta_0^{-1}div div F_nu[K],                    (13.5)

up to an analytic function of time in the analytic setting.

The canonical bijection uses F_nu[K]=0 and the zero scalar Cauchy
normalization. The larger projection sector can contain distinct metric
germs with identical reconstructed NSE pressure-gauge class.

14. EXPLICIT NONEMPTY SECTOR AND STRICTNESS EXAMPLE
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP14
  V15_REFINEMENT = VF06, VF10; actual compatible examples and constrained initial-trace image.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME21-ME22.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = CJ05-CJ09, CJ16; explicit inverse, radial and signature identities.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML31-ML33.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML07-ML19
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP03 finite matrix identities; NP06 coordinate curvature; NP14 explicit examples and collar positivity.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

EXAMPLE E0: CANONICAL ZERO-FLOW METRIC.

Let

  a(r)=1-(2rho/3)r,
  g_0=dr^2-a(r)^{-1}dt^2+a(r)sum_i(dx^i)^2,             (14.1)

on a collar where a(r)>0. Then h(0)=eta and

  K_00|_{r=0}=-rho/3,
  K_0i|_{r=0}=0,
  K_ij|_{r=0}=-(rho/3)delta_ij.

Thus beta=rho, q=0, v=0, and F_nu[K]=0.

Direct Ricci verification.
Put c=-2rho/3, so a=1+cr. Each h(r) has constant tangential coefficients,
so Ric[h]=0. The mixed extrinsic eigenvalues are

  K^0_0=-c/(2a),
  K^i_i=c/(2a), i=1,2,3,
  kappa=c/a,
  K_ab K^{ab}=c^2/a^2.

Hence Ric_rr=-partial_r(c/a)-c^2/a^2=0 and Ric_ra=0.
For the 00 equation, K_00=c/(2a^2), partial_rK_00=-c^2/a^3,
and (9.2) gives c^2/a^3-c^2/(2a^3)-c^2/(2a^3)=0.
For each spatial diagonal equation, K_ii=c/2 and (9.2) gives
-c^2/(2a)+c^2/(2a)=0. All off-diagonal components vanish.
Therefore Ric(g_0)=0.

For u=0,p=0 the scalar completion is

  psi(t,x)=-rho(t-t0)^2/6.

Its boundary K equals that of (14.1), so radial uniqueness gives

  L([0,0])=g_0,
  R(g_0)=[0,-rho/3]=[0,0].

In particular, both sides of T_EQ are nonempty for every nu>0,rho!=0,t0.

EXAMPLE E1: VACUUM AND NORMALIZATION WITHOUT THE CANONICAL SECTOR.

Define

  g_1=dr^2-dt^2+(1-rho r)^2(dx^1)^2+(dx^2)^2+(dx^3)^2, (14.2)

on |rho r|<1. The two-dimensional factor
dr^2+(1-rho r)^2(dx^1)^2 has curvature
-(partial_r^2(1-rho r))/(1-rho r)=0. The remaining product factors are
flat. Thus g_1 is analytic and Ricci-flat.

At r=0,

  K_00=0,
  K_11=-rho,
  K_22=K_33=0,
  K_0i=0.

Consequently

  beta=rho,
  beta_t=0,
  q=v=0,
  F_nu[K]=diag(-2rho/3,rho/3,rho/3)!=0.                (14.3)

Yet div F_nu[K]=0, so

  R(g_1)=[0,-2rho/3]=[0,0]

under the reconstruction formula extended to this larger projection
sector. The marked metric germs g_0 and g_1 are distinct, since their
boundary K tensors differ.

Therefore:

  vacuum + flat boundary + beta normalization
    does not imply F_nu[K]=0;

  the larger div F_nu[K]=0 projection sector is not injectively
  reconstructed by [v,p_*].

The constitutive restriction is mathematically substantive.

15. EXACT ENERGY CONSISTENCY COROLLARY
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP15
  V16_REFINEMENT = SF06,VI05; local reconstructed energy and finite observations.
  V16_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML38-ML39,ML60-ML61; ME12-ME14 with exact scope.
  V16_LOOKUP = Sections 51-55; exact source premises and target adapters retained.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = ST05, ST08; negative-Sobolev forcing norm with zero-mode removal.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME16.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML05, ML11, ML12; ME03, ME12, ME13
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP15 local-interval periodic integration/derivative adapter. Global positive-time predicates cannot be silently restricted from an unprovided global flow.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

For every g in the canonical analytic sector on an existing
time interval, let v=P K_{0 bullet}. Then

  d/dt (||v||_2^2/2)+nu||grad v||_2^2=0.              (15.1)

Proof.
Take the L^2 inner product of the recovered NSE with v. Periodicity and
div v=0 remove the pressure and transport integrals; integration by parts
gives <v,Delta v>=-||grad v||_2^2. QED.

For a general normalized vacuum boundary with constitutive residual,

  d/dt (||v||_2^2/2)+nu||grad v||_2^2
    =-integral_{T^3}F_nu[K]:grad v dx.                 (15.2)

These identities are restricted to the existing time interval. No global
extension follows from (15.1) or (15.2) alone.

=======================================================================
16. PROOF DEPENDENCIES AND OBLIGATION AUDIT
=======================================================================

D1. Analytic Cauchy--Kowalevski existence and uniqueness.
    Highest pure normal derivative is explicitly isolated.
    Scalar normal coefficient: 2(rho+Delta psi), initially 2rho!=0.
    Radial coefficient: identity on the components partial_r^2 h_ab.
    Constraint system: identity on partial_r(E,M).
    Applied to actual analytic solutions, not merely formal series.

D2. Gaussian Gauss--Codazzi and contracted Bianchi identities.
    Fixed convention K=(1/2)partial_r h.
    Formulas (9.2)--(9.5) fix all signs used in the proof.

D3. Periodic Fourier Helmholtz decomposition and mean-zero Poisson inverse.
    P_hat(0)=I; no mean-zero restriction on the NSE velocity is imposed.
    Analyticity preservation is proved in Section 3.

D4. Compactness of T^3, local uniqueness, and finite-cover gluing.
    The time interval is first shrunk, then a compact subinterval is used
    before choosing a common radial collar.

D5. Elementary tensor algebra, differentiation, and periodic integration
    by parts.

All D1--D5 are standard mathematical theorems or identities. Their exact
applicability conditions are checked in the displayed derivation.

UNRESOLVED_ADMISSION_FOR_T_EQ = NONE
UNRESOLVED_ANALYTIC_EXISTENCE_STEP_FOR_T_EQ = NONE

The prescribed analytic domain, boundary constitutive tensor, marking,
dimension, and scalar Cauchy normalization are theorem hypotheses and
definitions. They are not automatically properties of arbitrary Einstein
metrics or arbitrary NSE initial data.

18. EXACT SCOPE BOUNDARY
=======================================================================

ESTABLISHED:
  Analytic periodic NSE_3 pressure-gauge germs
    <==> normalized marked analytic vacuum GR_5 sector germs.

  Spatial domain: full T^3.
  Time/radial domain: data-dependent local collar.
  Einstein equation: exact Ric(g)=0.
  Forward existence: actual analytic solution, by noncharacteristic CK.
  Reverse direction: explicit K-only reconstruction and residual identity.
  Both inverse identities: exact equality of marked germs.
  Small-amplitude requirement: none within the stated analytic class.

NOT ESTABLISHED:
  Equivalence with unrestricted four-dimensional Einstein gravity.
  Equivalence with all five-dimensional Ricci-flat metric germs.
  Global-in-time or global-radial continuation of the constructed metric.
  A collar radius uniform over all NSE data.
  Sobolev or smooth well-posedness of the timelike radial Cauchy problem.
  Global regularity for arbitrary three-dimensional NSE data.
  Analytic extension through a nonanalytic initial trace.
  Arbitrary bulk matter, nonzero cosmological constant, horizon conditions,
  asymptotic conditions, or quotienting by unmarked diffeomorphisms.
  A_TIER or any formal/unconditional closure promotion.

FINAL ROOT:

  ! B_TIER

  forall nu>0, forall rho!=0, forall t0:
    N^omega_{nu,t0} ~= G^omega_{nu,rho,t0},

  with explicit L,R satisfying R o L=id and L o R=id.

19. ROUND I: INITIAL BOUNDARY JET AND REALIZED-TRACE RIGIDITY
=====================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP19
  V16_REFINEMENT = CV05; explicit boost correction to normalized initial K.
  V16_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML17-ML19,ML60.
  V16_LOOKUP = Sections 51-55; exact source premises and target adapters retained.
  V15_REFINEMENT = VF10; ambient initial trace surjectivity and finite-jet canonical/noncanonical separation.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME21-ME22.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V14_REFINEMENT = FC07-FC10; finite local CK jets, separate from realized analytic trace rigidity.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML43-ML48.
  V14_LOOKUP = Sections 39-43; exact source hypotheses remain mandatory.
  V13_REFINEMENT = ME18 realizes spatial derivatives only; AF09 remains the time adapter.
  V13_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME18.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = AF09, AF11, CJ14; differentiated synthesis and analytic trace comparison.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML28-ML30.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML07-ML12, ML17-ML19, ML22, ML23; ME06, ME08, ME10
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP10 initial K jet, projected NSE Taylor recurrence, analytic equality from jets; NP16 time differentiation of the spectral RHS.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

I.1. Exact initial metric-jet formula.

For g in G^omega_{nu,rho,t0}, write u0=v(t0), and define

  A0 := ((|u0|^2-rho)/3) I_3-u0 tensor u0+nu Z(u0).

Then, pointwise on T^3,

  K_0i(t0)=u0_i,
  K_ij(t0)=A0_ij,
  tr_x A0=-rho,
  K_00(t0)=(|A0|^2-2|u0|^2-rho^2)/(2rho).             (I.1)

Proof.
The normalization gives q(t0)=q_t(t0)=0. Hence all spatial derivatives
of these two spatial functions vanish. Equations v=w+grad q_t and
F_nu[K]=0 yield the first two identities. Divergence-free u0 gives
tr Z(u0)=0, hence tr A0=-rho. At t0, put k=K_00. The vacuum Hamiltonian
constraint is

  0=K_ab K^{ab}-kappa^2
   =k^2-2|u0|^2+|A0|^2-(-k-rho)^2
   =|A0|^2-2|u0|^2-rho^2-2rho k.

Since rho!=0, solve for k. This proves (I.1). QED.

The right side uses u0, its first spatial derivatives, nu, rho, and
finite-dimensional matrix algebra. It is independent of pressure gauge.
It prescribes the first radial metric jet on the initial three-torus,
not an arbitrary analytic radial Cauchy datum on an entire time cylinder.

I.2. Canonical second time jet of the scalar and beta.

Let Q_0 f=f-<f> and let

  p0(u0)=-Delta_0^{-1}partial_i partial_j(u0_i u0_j).

The following quantities are also determined by u0:

  q_tt(t0)=-Q_0(K_00(t0)+|u0|^2/3)-p0(u0),            (I.2)

  beta_tt(t0)=-Delta K_00(t0)-(1/3)Delta|u0|^2
              +partial_i partial_j(u0_i u0_j).        (I.3)

Proof.
At t0, the pressure formula gives
q_tt=-K_00-2rho/3-|u0|^2/3-p_*.
Taking mean-zero parts, using <q_tt>=0 and Q_0 p_*=p0(u0), proves
(I.2). Apply Delta and use Delta q=beta-rho to obtain (I.3). QED.

I.3. Projected Taylor recurrence and injective initial trace.

For an analytic NSE germ set U_n=partial_t^n u|_{t=t0}. Then

  U_0=u0,
  U_{n+1}=nu Delta U_n
    -sum_{j=0}^n binom(n,j) P div(U_j tensor U_{n-j}). (I.4)

Proof.
Project NSE to remove pressure:
u_t=nu Delta u-P div(u tensor u).
Apply partial_t^n, commute P and Delta with time differentiation,
and use the finite Leibniz rule. QED.

If two analytic NSE germs have the same u0, induction in (I.4) makes
every U_n equal. Real-analytic uniqueness of the time series at every
spatial point, followed by a finite compact cover of T^3, makes their
velocity germs equal. Their pressure gauges agree by the mean-zero
Poisson formula. Consequently

  ev_{t0}: N^omega_{nu,t0} -> C^omega_sigma(T^3),
  [u,p] |-> u(t0)

is injective. Composing with the established bijection gives

  g1,g2 in G^omega_{nu,rho,t0},
  K(g1)_{0 bullet}(t0)=K(g2)_{0 bullet}(t0)
    => g1=g2 as marked analytic germs.                (I.5)

This determines the canonical metric from one velocity trace within
the realized analytic trace class. It does not assert that every
spatially analytic divergence-free trace is realized.

I.4. Strictness of the realized analytic trace class.

For nu>0 take the spatially analytic periodic shear datum

  u0(x)=e1 sum_{k>=1} exp(-k) cos(2 pi k x2).           (I.6)

It is divergence-free and mean-zero. If a jointly analytic NSE germ
through t0=0 had this trace, (I.4) would force

  U_n(x)=(-4 pi^2 nu)^n e1
         sum_{k>=1} exp(-k) k^(2n) cos(2 pi k x2).

Indeed every U_n remains a shear in the e1 direction depending only
on x2, so every div(U_j tensor U_{n-j}) vanishes. At x=0 and n>=1,

  |U_n(0)|
    >=(4 pi^2 nu)^n exp(-2n)(2n)^(2n).                (I.7)

Time analyticity would give constants C,R>0 with
|U_n(0)|<=C R^(-n)n!. Since n!<=n^n, (I.7) would imply

  C >= (16 pi^2 nu exp(-2) R)^n n^n

for every n, which is impossible. Therefore (I.6) has no jointly
analytic two-sided NSE germ through t=0. The NSE Taylor recurrence
is an exact necessary recurrence, not a convergence theorem for
arbitrary spatially analytic data.

=====================================================================
20. ROUND II: SCALAR-POTENTIAL-FREE ADMISSION AND QUANTITATIVE DEFECT
=====================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP20
  V16_REFINEMENT = SF01-SF04,VI01-VI04; certified approximation and exact admission separated.
  V16_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML55-ML62; ME23-ME24.
  V16_LOOKUP = Sections 51-55; exact source premises and target adapters retained.
  V15_REFINEMENT = VF10; analytic STF sharpness witnesses realized at the ambient reference slice.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME21-ME22.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V14_REFINEMENT = HD01-HD08; complete constitutive residual and its orthogonal kernel.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME20.
  V14_LOOKUP = Sections 39-43; exact source hypotheses remain mandatory.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = ST01-ST07 add an orthogonal stress decomposition; the original Curl proof remains required.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME16.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML01-ML06, ML10, ML17-ML19; ME05, ME06
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP11 row-Curl symbol, sharp STF mode estimate, matrix Parseval, zero mode and scalar-free admission; constants 3/(4*pi^2), 1/(16*pi^4).
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

Let g be a smooth vacuum metric with flat marked boundary and
<beta>=rho. Define v=P K_{0 bullet} and the tensor

  T_nu[K] := K_spatial+v tensor v-nu Z(v)
             -((|v|^2-rho)/3) I_3.                   (II.1)

This expression contains no q or scalar Hessian. Define rowwise Curl by

  (Curl T)_{i ell}=epsilon_{ell j k} partial_j T_{i k}.

The source conventions imply

  F_nu[K]=T_nu[K]+Hess_x q,
  tr F_nu[K]=0,
  Curl F_nu[K]=Curl T_nu[K],
  <F_nu[K]>=<T_nu[K]>.                               (II.2)

II.1. Exact admission equivalence.

  F_nu[K]=0
    <=> Curl T_nu[K]=0 and <T_nu[K]>=0.               (II.3)

Proof via the quantitative inequality below. The forward implication
follows from (II.2). Conversely, vanishing Curl and mean force the
right side of (II.5) to vanish and hence F_nu=0. QED.

This removes the scalar Poisson reconstruction from the constitutive
admission test. The velocity v=P K_{0 bullet} remains the fixed torus
Helmholtz reconstruction; no claim of a purely pointwise K-only test
or of eliminating all torus operators is made.

II.2. Sharp trace-free tensor estimate.

For every smooth symmetric trace-free tensor F on the unit T^3,

  ||grad F||_2^2 <= 3 ||Curl F||_2^2,                 (II.4)

  ||F||_2^2 <= |<F>|^2
                 +(3/(4 pi^2)) ||Curl F||_2^2.       (II.5)

Proof.
For a nonzero Fourier wavevector xi=2 pi k, rowwise curl satisfies

  |(Curl F)^hat(k)|^2
    =|xi|^2 |Fhat(k)|^2-|Fhat(k) xi|^2.

For every complex symmetric trace-free 3x3 matrix A and real unit e,

  |A e|^2 <= (2/3)|A|^2.                             (II.6)

Rotate e to e1. Write the diagonal entries a,d,f with a+d+f=0;
then |d|^2+|f|^2>=|a|^2/2. The off-diagonal entries occur twice in
the Frobenius norm and only once in |A e1|^2. These inequalities
give (II.6). Hence each nonzero Fourier mode satisfies

  |xi|^2 |Fhat(k)|^2 <=3 |(Curl F)^hat(k)|^2.

Sum by Parseval to prove (II.4). For k!=0, |xi|^2>=4 pi^2; the
constant mode contributes |<F>|^2 because the torus has unit volume.
This proves (II.5). QED.

Sharpness in the class of periodic symmetric trace-free tensors follows
from F(x)=sin(2 pi x1) diag(1,-1/2,-1/2). Equality holds in (II.4) and
(II.5). This does not assert sharpness within the narrower class of
residuals realized by vacuum Einstein metrics.

Applying (II.2) gives the computable bound

  ||F_nu[K]||_2^2
    <= |<T_nu[K]>|^2
       +(3/(4 pi^2))||Curl T_nu[K]||_2^2.             (II.7)

II.3. Equivalent scalar-potential-free Laplacian test.

Put A_nu=TF(K_spatial+v tensor v-nu Z(v)), and define

  L_nu[K]=Delta A_nu+TF(Hess_x beta).

Since F_nu=A_nu+TF(Hess q) and Delta q=beta-rho,

  Delta F_nu=L_nu[K],
  <F_nu>=<A_nu>.

Therefore

  F_nu=0 <=> L_nu[K]=0 and <A_nu>=0,                 (II.8)

  ||F_nu||_2^2
    <= |<A_nu>|^2+(1/(16 pi^4))||L_nu[K]||_2^2.      (II.9)

Proof.
The inverse-Laplacian multiplier on every nonzero mode has modulus
at most 1/(4 pi^2). Parseval gives (II.9); it also proves (II.8).
Alternatively, a periodic harmonic tensor is spatially constant. QED.

=====================================================================
21. ROUND III: VISCOSITY RIGIDITY AND BOUNDARY RECOVERY
=====================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP21
  V16_REFINEMENT = VI01-VI08; complete positive-viscosity set and exact Gram criterion.
  V16_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML38-ML39,ML60-ML62; ME12,ME21-ME22.
  V16_LOOKUP = Sections 51-55; exact source premises and target adapters retained.
  V15_REFINEMENT = VF04, VF08-VF09; exact viscosity cancellation, transport and constant intersection.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME21-ME22.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = AF07-AF10 support exact analytic observable maps; original viscosity cancellations retained.
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML26-ML28; ME16.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML05, ML11, ML12, ML17-ML19; ME01, ME03, ME12, ME13
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP12 periodic Hessian/transport cancellation, strain norm identity, viscosity reconstruction and sector intersection; NP15 local-domain adapter.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

III.1. Exact viscosity recovery.

For a canonical metric and a time at which ||Z(v)||_2>0,

  nu = integral K_spatial:Z(v) / ||Z(v)||_2^2
     = integral K_spatial:grad v / ||grad v||_2^2.    (III.1)

Proof.
Because div v=0 and the fields are periodic,

  integral (v tensor v):Z(v)=integral v.grad |v|^2=0,
  integral Hess q:Z(v)=0,
  tr Z(v)=0,
  ||Z(v)||_2^2=2||grad v||_2^2.

For the Hessian pairing, integrate one spatial derivative and use
div v=0. For the last identity, expand the square and integrate the
cross term: integral partial_i v_j partial_j v_i=0.
Pair F_nu=0 with Z(v) and use symmetry of K. QED.

III.2. Pairwise intersection of viscosity sectors.

For fixed rho,t0 and nu1,nu2>0 with nu1!=nu2,

  g in G^omega_{nu1,rho,t0} intersect G^omega_{nu2,rho,t0}
    => v(t,x)=m for one constant vector m.            (III.2)

Proof.
The same metric determines the same beta,w,q,v. Thus

  0=F_nu1[K]-F_nu2[K]=-(nu1-nu2)Z(v).

The preceding norm identity gives grad v=0. Hence v=m(t). Spatial
integration of vacuum Codazzi, or recovered NSE, gives m'(t)=0. QED.

Conversely, for constant u=m, the NSE tensor S_rho, scalar completion,
and radial Einstein construction are independent of nu because
Z(m)=0. Therefore the pairwise intersection is exactly the family
of canonical lifts of constant velocity germs. No nonconstant
canonical velocity germ admits two different positive viscosities.

III.3. Defect-controlled effective viscosity.

For a general normalized smooth vacuum boundary and ||Z(v)||_2>0,
define entirely from K and v

  nu_eff(t)=integral K_spatial:Z(v) / ||Z(v)||_2^2.

For each trial nu>0,

  (nu_eff-nu)||Z(v)||_2^2=integral F_nu[K]:Z(v).

Consequently

  |nu_eff-nu| <= ||F_nu[K]||_2 / ||Z(v)||_2.          (III.3)

Since <Z(v)>=0, the constant part of F_nu does not contribute.
The stronger scalar-potential-free estimate is

  |nu_eff-nu|
    <= (sqrt(3)/(2 pi))
       ||Curl T_nu[K]||_2 / ||Z(v)||_2.               (III.4)

Proof.
Subtract <F_nu> in the pairing, use Cauchy--Schwarz, and apply
(II.5) to F_nu-<F_nu>, followed by (II.2). QED.

The denominator is zero exactly for spatially constant reconstructed
velocities. No viscosity identification is asserted in that case.

=====================================================================
22. ROUND IV: RELATIVE ENERGY ON EXISTING COMMON INTERVALS
=====================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP22
  V16_REFINEMENT = SF06; finite spectral upper bound in the existing forced estimate.
  V16_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML55-ML58; ME23-ME24.
  V16_LOOKUP = Sections 51-55; exact source premises and target adapters retained.
  V15_REFINEMENT = VF07; actual minimum constitutive-defect metric on common time intervals.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME21-ME22.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V14_REFINEMENT = HD03-HD06; the projected-forcing norm remains the exact energetic component.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME20.
  V14_LOOKUP = Sections 39-43; exact source hypotheses remain mandatory.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT = ST05-ST08 replace the coarse forcing bound by the sharper estimate (VII.10).
  ADDITIONAL_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME16.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML05, ML11, ML12; ME12-ME14
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = NP13 two-smooth-flow tensor-forcing energy identity, integrating factor and matched-mean damping; NP15 local domain. ME14 retains its weak/strong and admissible-time premises.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 (primary ML/ME), 31 (additional ML/ME), 25 and 30 (proof contracts).
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target application not compiled.

Let g and g_bar be two smooth normalized vacuum metrics on a common
existing time interval. Fix nu>0. Let their reconstructed fields be

  (v,p,F), (V,P,F_bar),
  R_nu(v,p)=div F,
  R_nu(V,P)=div F_bar.

Set

  z=v-V, y(t)=||z(t)||_2^2,
  D(t)=||F(t)-F_bar(t)||_2,
  L(t)=||Sym grad V(t)||_{L^infinity,operator}.

All quantities are finite and continuous on compact subintervals in
the analytic setting. The statements also hold under the ordinary
integrability hypotheses needed for the displayed smooth energy proof.

IV.1. Exact relative energy identity.

  (1/2)y'(t)+nu||grad z||_2^2
    =-integral z^T(Sym grad V)z
     -integral (F-F_bar):grad z.                     (IV.1)

Proof.
Subtract the equations and write
(v.grad)v-(V.grad)V=(v.grad)z+(z.grad)V.
Take the L^2 pairing with z. The term (v.grad)z vanishes after periodic
integration because div v=0. The pressure term vanishes because
div z=0. Integrate the viscous and tensor-divergence terms by parts.
The antisymmetric part of grad V has zero quadratic pairing with z.
This proves (IV.1). QED.

IV.2. Explicit defect stability.

Young's inequality gives

  y'(t)+nu||grad z||_2^2 <=2L(t)y(t)+D(t)^2/nu.

Thus for s<=t in the existing interval,

  y(t) <= exp(2 integral_s^t L) y(s)
       +(1/nu) integral_s^t
          exp(2 integral_tau^t L) D(tau)^2 d tau.    (IV.2)

Proof.
Use |integral z^T Sym grad V z|<=L y and
D||grad z||<=(nu/2)||grad z||^2+D^2/(2nu) in (IV.1).
Multiply by 2 and apply the scalar integrating-factor argument. QED.

Vacuum Codazzi preserves the mean of each reconstructed velocity.
If <v(s)>=<V(s)>, then <z>=0 throughout. Poincare gives
||grad z||_2^2>=4 pi^2 y, so (IV.2) improves by replacing
exp(2 integral_tau^t L) with

  exp(2 integral_tau^t L-4 pi^2 nu(t-tau)).           (IV.3)

The Curl/mean diagnostic (II.7), applied to the difference of the
two symmetric trace-free residual tensors, can bound D without
computing q or q_bar:

  D^2 <= |<T_nu[K]-T_nu[K_bar]>|^2
          +(3/(4 pi^2))
             ||Curl(T_nu[K]-T_nu[K_bar])||_2^2.      (IV.4)

IV.3. Canonical-sector strengthening.

If both metrics are canonical for nu, then F=F_bar=0. No viscous
term needs to be spent in Young's inequality, and (IV.1) yields

  y(t)<=exp(2 integral_s^t L)y(s).                    (IV.5)

If their means agree, the stronger damping rate is

  y(t)<=exp(2 integral_s^t L-8 pi^2 nu(t-s))y(s).     (IV.6)

In particular, equal velocity traces at s imply equal velocities
on the subsequent common interval. At s=t0, Round I and the exact
analytic germ bijection also identify the normalized marked metric
germs when rho agrees.

These estimates concern reconstructed velocities on already existing
intervals. They assert no Sobolev continuity of the timelike radial
Einstein Cauchy map, no global continuation, and no uniform collar.

METHLIB_ALIGNMENT = verified manifest of regularity-B-assembly/
Singular-Axiomatic-closure, codex/h12-small-unconditional, selects this revision.

Each ML identifier below names an actual declaration in a source file fetched at
TARGET_REVISION. SOURCE_STATEMENT reproduces that file's declaration header and,
for definitions, the indicated defining expression. It is a source excerpt, not
a new Lean file. Implicit variables and typeclasses are specified in CONTEXT.
Namespace-local source identifiers are fully qualified in ENDPOINT.

Source inspection verifies endpoint existence and the displayed hypotheses.
It does not establish that a proposed bridge application elaborates or that the
exact NSE–GR theorem passed a matching external compiler/axiom audit. No result
is promoted to A_TIER, FORMAL_CLOSURE, or UNCONDITIONAL_CLOSURE by this audit.

NORMALIZATION AND REPRESENTATION OBLIGATIONS
------------------------------------------
N01. Torus: UnitAddTorus (Fin 3), period one, normalized Haar measure.
N02. Laplacian: coefficient −4π²|k|²; inverse coefficient −1/(4π²|k|²)
     for k ≠ 0; inverse zero mode = 0; Leray zero mode = identity.
N03. Real fields: either complexify each real component or construct a compatible
     complex vector encoding. Prove conjugate symmetry and descent to real fields.
N04. L² equality, ContinuousMap equality, and pointwise analytic equality are
     distinct representations. Each conversion needs its explicit hypotheses.
N05. Analytic-to-rapid adapter: jointly analytic periodic representatives on a
     neighborhood of {t0}×T³ must yield common local parameter neighborhoods and
     sufficient Fourier bounds for every differentiated multiplier series.
     Exponential spatial decay on a smaller uniform complex strip is sufficient;
     rapid decay alone does not by itself prove preservation of analyticity.
N06. Multi-variable derivative multiplier identities and differentiated-series
     interchange are not supplied by Fourier reconstruction theorems alone.
N07. Finite tensor traces use mixed-index matrices for Lorentzian contractions;
     the source Matrix.trace is an ordinary matrix trace.
N08. Spatial tensor Parseval and Curl estimates use the explicit Frobenius
     norm |A|_F^2=sum_ij |A_ij|^2, including complex Fourier coefficients.
     Supply an explicit finite-sum/Euclidean tensor model or prove the required
     norm identification; no default Lean Matrix norm may be substituted
     without that proof. The L^infinity strain bound uses the operator norm.

SHARED CONTEXTS
---------------
CONTEXT CALC
{𝕜 : Type*} [NontriviallyNormedField 𝕜]
{E F : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace 𝕜 E]
[NormedAddCommGroup F] [NormedSpace 𝕜 F]
{f : E → F} {x : E} {s : Set E}
Bridge specialization: 𝕜 = ℝ; finite-dimensional real chart domains and codomains.
Where CompleteSpace F is stated in a declaration, that hypothesis remains required.

CONTEXT FOURIER
{d : Type*} [Fintype d]
UnitAddCircle = AddCircle (1 : ℝ); UnitAddTorus d = d → UnitAddCircle.
The source file installs a local MeasureSpace UnitAddCircle with measure
AddCircle.haarAddCircle and uses its finite product measure on UnitAddTorus d.
The measure is normalized to total volume 1.
Local notation L²(α) = MeasureTheory.Lp ℂ 2 (volume : Measure α).
Bridge specialization: d = Fin 3, unit-period torus, frequencies 2π k.
Thus Δ has multiplier −4π²|k|² and Δ0⁻¹ has multiplier −1/(4π²|k|²), k ≠ 0.
The zero-frequency multiplier must be P_hat(0)=I. Preserve the source measure arguments.

CONTEXT FOURIER_E
FOURIER plus {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℂ E].
The displayed definitions/theorems do not add an integrability premise.
Consequently the definition alone supplies neither decay nor convergence estimates.

CONTEXT FOURIER_C
FOURIER plus {f : C(UnitAddTorus d, ℂ)}.
C(α,β) denotes ContinuousMap α β; equality/convergence occurs in that space.

CONTEXT ANALYTIC_MUL
CALC for the domain plus {A : Type*} [NormedRing A] [NormedAlgebra 𝕜 A].
The codomain A is the algebra in the displayed declaration.

CONTEXT ANALYTIC_DIV
CALC for the domain plus {𝕝 : Type*} [NormedDivisionRing 𝕝]
[NormedAlgebra 𝕜 𝕝]. The bridge uses 𝕝 = ℝ.
The nonvanishing premise is an explicit mathematical obligation.

CONTEXT ANALYTIC_PI
CALC for the domain plus {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] {e : E}
{Fm : ι → Type*} [∀ i, NormedAddCommGroup (Fm i)]
[∀ i, NormedSpace 𝕜 (Fm i)] {f : Π i, E → Fm i}.

CONTEXT SUM_DERIV
CALC plus {ι : Type*} {u : Finset ι} {A : ι → E → F}
{A' : ι → E →L[𝕜] F}. The derivative sum is finite.

CONTEXT MUL_DERIV
CALC for the domain plus {𝔸' : Type*} [NormedCommRing 𝔸']
[NormedAlgebra 𝕜 𝔸'] {c d : E → 𝔸'} {c' d' : E →L[𝕜] 𝔸'}.
The bridge applies this with 𝔸' = ℝ to individual tensor entries.

CONTEXT TRACE
{m n : Type*} {R α : Type*} [Fintype m] [Fintype n].
Matrix.trace means the ordinary trace of a square matrix. A Lorentzian trace
requires the explicit mixed tensor η⁻¹K; it is not Matrix.trace K on covariant entries.

CONTEXT COMPACT
{X : Type u} {Y : Type v} [TopologicalSpace X] [TopologicalSpace Y]
{s : Set X}. In the bridge, take the compact initial slice or its compact torus factor.

CONTEXT GERM
{α β γ δ : Type*} {l : Filter α} {f g : α → β}.
Filter.Germ l β = Quotient (Filter.germSetoid l β), whose relation is
Filter.EventuallyEq l. The arrow ⇒ in map' is Relator.LiftFun.
The bridge must choose its germ filter and prove representative compatibility.

CONTEXT EQUIV
{α : Sort u} {β : Sort v}. LeftInverse, RightInverse and Bijective are
Function predicates. A structure field proof is still required even though
Equiv includes default tactic expressions for inverse fields.

PRIMARY ENDPOINT LEDGER (25 ENTRIES)
====================================

CONTEXT = FOURIER_E
SOURCE_STATEMENT_BEGIN
def mFourierCoeff (f : UnitAddTorus d → E) (n : d → ℤ) : E := ∫ t, mFourier (-n) t • f t
SOURCE_STATEMENT_END
APPLICABILITY = Concrete torus coefficient endpoint for scalar/vector complex Fourier representations.
REMAINING_OBLIGATION = Real fields require complexification or componentwise scalar embedding, followed by reality/conjugate-symmetry descent. Analytic decay and differentiated-series bounds remain separate.

theorem mFourierCoeff_eq_integral (f : UnitAddTorus d → E) (n : d → ℤ) (a : d → ℝ) :
    mFourierCoeff f n =
    ∫ (x : d → ℝ) in {x : d → ℝ | ∀ i, x i ∈ Ioc (a i) (a i + 1)},
    mFourier (-n) (fun i => x i) • f (fun i => x i) :=
SOURCE_STATEMENT_END
APPLICABILITY = Conversion of torus coefficients to a unit-cube integral; support for coordinatewise periodic integration by parts.
REMAINING_OBLIGATION = Cube/Fubini adapters and coordinatewise derivative multipliers are not included in this statement.

[ML06]
ENDPOINT = fourierCoeffOn_of_hasDerivAt
STATUS = SOURCE_VERIFIED_SUPPORT; BRIDGE_APPLICATION_UNCOMPILED
IMPORT = Mathlib.Analysis.Fourier.AddCircle
REVISION = db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d
SOURCE = https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d/Mathlib/Analysis/Fourier/AddCircle.lean#L596-L599
TXT_PLACEMENT = 3; 6; 13
CONTEXT = No implicit T or Fact (0<T) premise in this displayed declaration. Uses real a,b; complex f,f'; normalized interval Fourier coefficients; volume is Lebesgue measure.
SOURCE_STATEMENT_BEGIN
theorem fourierCoeffOn_of_hasDerivAt {a b : ℝ} (hab : a < b) {f f' : ℝ → ℂ} {n : ℤ} (hn : n ≠ 0)
    (hf : ∀ x, x ∈ [[a, b]] → HasDerivAt f (f' x) x) (hf' : IntervalIntegrable f' volume a b) :
    fourierCoeffOn hab f n = 1 / (-2 * π * I * n) *
      (fourier (-n) (a : AddCircle (b - a)) * (f b - f a) - (b - a) * fourierCoeffOn hab f' n) :=
SOURCE_STATEMENT_END
APPLICABILITY = One-dimensional integration-by-parts identity with explicit boundary term; periodicity removes that term.
REMAINING_OBLIGATION = Apply on coordinate slices, prove interval integrability, use product integration, and handle the zero mode separately. n ≠ 0 is mandatory.

CONTEXT = EQUIV
SOURCE_STATEMENT_BEGIN
structure Equiv (α β : Sort*) where
  /-- The forward map of an equivalence.

  Do NOT use directly. Use the coercion instead. -/
  protected toFun : α → β
  /-- The backward map of an equivalence.

  Do NOT use `e.invFun` directly. Use the coercion of `e.symm` instead. -/
  protected invFun : β → α
  protected left_inv : LeftInverse invFun toFun := by intro; first | rfl | ext <;> rfl
  protected right_inv : RightInverse invFun toFun := by intro; first | rfl | ext <;> rfl
SOURCE_STATEMENT_END
APPLICABILITY = Exact packaging endpoint for lift and decode after both inverse identities are proved.
REMAINING_OBLIGATION = All field types must refer to the exact scoped NSE and GR sector, with completed well-definedness and inverse proofs. No equivalence follows merely from naming Equiv.

EXPLICIT BRIDGE OBLIGATIONS: NO VERIFIED ENDPOINT SUBSTITUTION
==============================================================

These identifiers are audit labels, not Lean declarations. They must not be
inserted into an endpoint manifest as if an imported theorem with that name exists.

GAP-TORUS [sections 3, 4, 5, 7, 11, 13]
  Define the exact analytic periodic function/germ representation; prove analytic
  Fourier decay uniformly on a smaller parameter neighborhood; define Δ0⁻¹ and
  P with the stated zero modes; prove reality, analytic preservation, mean-zero
  inversion, Helmholtz decomposition, derivative commutation, self-adjointness
  where needed, and coefficient uniqueness in the chosen representation.
  Fourier support: ML01–ML06. No source-verified complete analytic Poisson/Leray
  endpoint was located in this bounded audit.

GAP-ALGEBRA [sections 1, 4–8, 13]
  Encode η = diag(−1,1,1,1), index raising/lowering, B, C, K, and TF.
  Derive the exact trace-reversal formulas, the Hamiltonian identity, the affine
  dependence on ψ_tt with coefficient 2(ρ+Δψ), and all finite-dimensional STF
  symbol identities. Matrix/analytic support does not replace these statements.

GAP-CODAZZI [sections 4, 8]
  Derive the marked Gaussian Codazzi constraint with the chosen normal and K
  convention; identify it with conservation of the trace-reversed tensor.
  Derive the exact boundary reconstruction relations with their displayed
  coefficients and signs. No wave equation or evolution equation beyond the
  identities proved in the TXT is assumed by this audit.

GAP-RESIDUAL [section 5]
  From the sector's constraints and normalization derive <β>=ρ, w=v−∇q_t,
  p*=−K00−(2ρ+|v|²)/3−q_tt, and Rν(v,p*)=div Fν[K].
  No generic calculus lemma above states this reconstruction theorem. In particular,
  <β>=ρ does not imply β=ρ; β=ρ+Δq is allowed to vary spatially.

GAP-SCALAR-CK [section 7; uniqueness reused in sections 10, 11]
  For the exact rational scalar second-order PDE, prove analytic existence and
  uniqueness with ψ|t0=0, ψ_t|t0=0, noncharacteristic coefficient 2ρ ≠ 0,
  periodic spatial compatibility, and a uniform local interval over the compact
  torus. ML14 supplies analytic division once nonvanishing is proved; it does
  not establish the Cauchy–Kowalevski theorem.

GAP-RADIAL-CK [section 9; uniqueness reused in section 11]
  Prove the analytic Gaussian-normal Ric_ab=0 Cauchy theorem with h(0)=η and
  ∂r h(0)=2K, the exact noncharacteristic leading matrix, periodic compatibility,
  and uniqueness of the marked local analytic germ. Preserve nondegeneracy and
  Lorentzian signature in the resulting local collar.

GAP-GAUSS-BIANCHI [sections 8, 9]
  Prove Gaussian curvature component formulas with the declared sign convention,
  Gauss/Codazzi identities, the contracted Bianchi identity in that convention,
  and the homogeneous subsidiary propagation system. Derive Ric_rr=Ric_ra=0
  from constraints and Ric_ab=0 by the applicable analytic uniqueness theorem.
  No source-verified complete endpoint was located in the bounded audit.

GAP-GAUGE [section 10]
  Define pressure equivalence p~p+a(t), derive the compensating scalar change,
  prove exact equality of completed K and the marked metric germ, and prove
  representative independence of lift/decode. A generic Germ equality lemma
  cannot supply this invariance.

GAP-GERM [sections 0, 10, 11]
  Choose the solution-germ and metric-germ filters precisely; define quotients
  and solution predicates; prove every operation respects representatives.
  Prove analytic local compatibility and compact torus gluing before packaging
  the two inverse maps with ML24.

GAP-PROJECTION [section 13]
  Prove that P div F=0 is the exact weaker projected residual criterion and
  establish any pressure reconstruction required by its stated hypotheses.
  Do not infer F=0 from P div F=0.

GAP-CURL-STF [strengthened Section 20]
  Prove the row-Curl Fourier symbol, STF modewise coercivity, explicit zero-mode
  requirement, and scalar-to-real-matrix Parseval conversion. ML05 supports
  summation of a proved modewise estimate; it does not state the coefficient
  3/(4π²) or the STF tensor estimate itself.

ADDITIONAL STRENGTHENING OBLIGATIONS
------------------------------------
JET-RIGIDITY: exact projected NSE Taylor-jet recurrence, its analytic uniqueness
consequence, and the stated realized-trace rigidity are new bridge lemmas. No
additional exact Mathlib endpoint for this package was verified in this audit.

HIGHER-PRODUCT-RECURRENCE: binomial product differentiation and coefficient
normalization must be proved for the chosen coordinate derivative convention.
ML11/ML12 provide first-derivative algebra; they do not state the entire recurrence.

DEFECT-STABILITY: any integrating-factor/Gronwall estimate must match the exact
regularity, initial-time, integrability, and sign hypotheses. No such theorem was
selected or source-verified here. Listing a file path as a candidate is not an
endpoint certificate.

AUDIT BOUNDARY
--------------
The bounded source audit located no complete imported statement for the exact
NSE–GR equivalence, the required scalar/radial Cauchy–Kowalevski theorems, the
Gaussian Lorentzian Ricci/Bianchi propagation package, or the analytic torus
Poisson/Leray package. This is not a claim that no related theorem exists anywhere
in Mathlib. It is a declaration that no such endpoint was source-verified here.

No semantic endpoint supplied by Methlib may be substituted solely because its
name resembles one of these obligations. Exact type/domain/regularity/premise
matching and theorem-level axiom audit remain required for external promotion.

AUDIT METHOD
Declaration bodies, explicit premises, immediate imports, contract definitions,
and selected historical evidence files were read. No Lean, Lake, compiler,
proof checker, repository build, workflow dispatch, or repository mutation ran.
SOURCE_VERIFIED_ONLY means the declaration exists with the inspected source
signature. It does not establish A_TIER, FORMAL_CLOSURE, or unconditional closure.
Axiom-freedom of the full transitive closure is not inferred from source search.

A0. CONCRETE SEMANTIC BASE
Module SingularClosure.Contracts uses actual EuclideanSpace R (Fin 3) fields,
actual Frechet derivatives, actual cube Lebesgue integrals, and unit periodicity.
Gradient, divergence, convection and Laplacian are concrete operators.
timeDerivative u t x = derivWithin (fun s => u s x) (Ici 0) t.
GlobalSmoothNSE requires joint C-infinity on t >= 0, periodicity, divergence-free
velocity, the actual equation, initial trace, zero velocity mean and zero pressure
mean. SmoothSigmaZero also requires zero initial velocity mean.
Fefferman_B_0_adjust is a definition of an existence proposition, not its proof.
Source:
https://github.com/randogelist/Methlib/blob/09dfe94a1ef150e21d09df3013fbe09002ef139c/regularity-B-assembly/Singular-Axiomatic-closure/SingularClosure/Contracts.lean

Consequently GlobalSmoothNSE cannot be identified directly with the root's
arbitrary-mean, joint-real-analytic, interior-time germs. Required adapters:
  (a) restriction/time translation for local intervals;
  (b) an actual joint-analyticity proof when importing a merely smooth flow;
  (c) retention of constant vector modes, or an explicitly proved Galilean
      transform for a mean-zero-only existence endpoint;
  (d) pressure-gauge normalization compatible with the marked germ quotient.

TorusSobolev s is the subtype
  {c : FourierCoeff // Mem-lp (sobolevWeightedCoeff s c) 2}.
Its definition imposes neither spectral solenoidality nor zero mean. Those are
separate predicates. This distinction permits arbitrary constant modes in its
Leray operator. Galerkin cutoffModes, by contrast, omits k=0.
Source:
https://github.com/randogelist/Methlib/blob/09dfe94a1ef150e21d09df3013fbe09002ef139c/regularity-B-assembly/Singular-Axiomatic-closure/SingularClosure/TorusSobolev.lean
https://github.com/randogelist/Methlib/blob/09dfe94a1ef150e21d09df3013fbe09002ef139c/regularity-B-assembly/Singular-Axiomatic-closure/SingularClosure/Galerkin.lean

CriticalSobolev q uses integer q indexing the order q/2, with weighted
coefficients criticalHalfWeight(k)^q * c(k,i), criticalHalfWeight(k) =
sqrt(criticalFrequencyWeight(k)), and criticalFrequencyWeight(k) =
sqrt(laplacianFrequency(k)) for k != 0, completed by weight one at k=0.
Thus CriticalSobolev 1 has the H^(1/2) weight; CriticalSigmaZero separately
imposes the zero coefficient at k=0.
https://github.com/randogelist/Methlib/blob/09dfe94a1ef150e21d09df3013fbe09002ef139c/regularity-B-assembly/Singular-Axiomatic-closure/SingularClosure/CriticalSpaces.lean
https://github.com/randogelist/Methlib/blob/09dfe94a1ef150e21d09df3013fbe09002ef139c/regularity-B-assembly/Singular-Axiomatic-closure/SingularClosure/CriticalRegularityContracts.lean

ME01. MODEWISE LERAY ALGEBRA

MODULE = SingularClosure.LerayPressure

SOURCE = https://github.com/randogelist/Methlib/blob/09dfe94a1ef150e21d09df3013fbe09002ef139c/regularity-B-assembly/Singular-Axiomatic-closure/SingularClosure/LerayPressure.lean

MAP = Section 3 / E01; Section 4 / E02; Section 13 projection variants

STATUS = SOURCE_VERIFIED_ONLY; no matching target-root compiler/audit evidence.

DIRECT IMPORTS:
import SingularClosure.GalerkinAlgebra

EXACT SOURCE SIGNATURES; namespace SingularClosure:
theorem coefficientLeray_solenoidal (k : Frequency) (v : Fin 3 → ℂ) :
    (∑ i : Fin 3, (k i : ℂ) * coefficientLeray k v i) = 0

theorem coefficientLeray_eq_of_solenoidal (k : Frequency) (v : Fin 3 → ℂ)
    (hv : (∑ i : Fin 3, (k i : ℂ) * v i) = 0) :
    coefficientLeray k v = v

theorem coefficientLeray_idempotent (k : Frequency) (v : Fin 3 → ℂ) :
    coefficientLeray k (coefficientLeray k v) = coefficientLeray k v

PREMISE AUDIT = No regularity, convergence, mean-zero, or NSE premise. Frequency k and complex vector v are arbitrary.

APPLICATION / ADAPTER = coefficientLeray has the exact zero branch P_0=I in Galerkin.lean. Supports pointwise Fourier multiplier algebra. Physical operators still require convergence, coefficient-identification, and analytic dependence adapters.

BODY DEPENDENCIES OBSERVED = frequency_complex_square_sum_ne_zero; exact finite-dimensional field algebra.

    (i : Fin 3) :

    (∑ i : Fin 3, star (coefficientLeray k v i) *
      (v i - coefficientLeray k v i)) = 0

theorem coefficientLeray_energy_pythagoras (k : Frequency) (v : Fin 3 → ℂ) :
    (∑ i : Fin 3, Complex.normSq (v i)) =
      (∑ i : Fin 3, Complex.normSq (coefficientLeray k v i)) +
      ∑ i : Fin 3, Complex.normSq (v i - coefficientLeray k v i)

theorem coefficientLeray_energy_le (k : Frequency) (v : Fin 3 → ℂ) :
    (∑ i : Fin 3, Complex.normSq (coefficientLeray k v i)) ≤
      ∑ i : Fin 3, Complex.normSq (v i)

APPLICATION / ADAPTER = Modewise orthogonality and contraction are exact. Same-file physicalEnergy_leray_le and integral_norm_sq_leray_le refer to finiteSynthesis N c and require SpectralReality c; they are not automatically arbitrary-field physical L2 theorems. ME04 supplies the infinite Sobolev operator.

def torusSobolevMultiplier (s t : ℕ) (b : Frequency → ℂ)
    (B : ℝ) (hB : 0 ≤ B)
    (hb : ∀ k, torusSobolevWeight k ^ s * ‖b k‖ ≤ B * torusSobolevWeight k ^ t) :
    TorusSobolev t →L[ℂ] TorusSobolev s

theorem torusSobolevMultiplier_coeff (s t : ℕ) (b : Frequency → ℂ)
    (B : ℝ) (hB : 0 ≤ B)
    (hb : ∀ k, torusSobolevWeight k ^ s * ‖b k‖ ≤ B * torusSobolevWeight k ^ t)
    (c : TorusSobolev t) :
    (torusSobolevMultiplier s t b B hB hb c).val = fun k i => b k * c.val k i

def torusSobolevLaplacian (s : ℕ) : TorusSobolev (s + 2) →L[ℂ] TorusSobolev s

theorem torusSobolevLaplacian_coeff (s : ℕ) (c : TorusSobolev (s + 2)) :
    (torusSobolevLaplacian s c).val = fun k i => -(laplacianFrequency k : ℂ) * c.val k i

PREMISE AUDIT = A supplied multiplier b, nonnegative B, and an explicit weighted-symbol bound hb. Laplacian specialization discharges its bound.

APPLICATION / ADAPTER = Can support a new zero-mean Poisson multiplier with b(0)=0 and b(k)=-1/(4*pi^2*|k|^2) for k!=0. The scalar-to-vector embedding, symbol bound, exact inverse on zero-mean sources, mean normalization, derivatives in t, and joint analytic preservation remain new adapter obligations. No ready-made CK solver follows.

    (hb : ∀ i : Fin 3, RapidScalarFourier (fun k => b k i)) :
    ContDiff ℝ ∞ (infiniteTransportPressure b)

theorem infiniteTransportPressure_meanZero (b : FourierCoeff)
    (hb : ∀ i : Fin 3, RapidScalarFourier (fun k => b k i)) :
    PressureMeanZero (infiniteTransportPressure b)

theorem infiniteTransportPressure_recovery (b : FourierCoeff)
    (hb : ∀ i : Fin 3, RapidScalarFourier (fun k => b k i)) (x : Vec) :
    smoothFourierSynthesis b x + gradient (infiniteTransportPressure b) x =
      smoothFourierSynthesis (fun k => coefficientLeray k (b k)) x

theorem infiniteTransportPressure_gradient_coeff (b : FourierCoeff)
    (hb : ∀ i : Fin 3, RapidScalarFourier (fun k => b k i))
    (hreal : SpectralReality b) :

      fun k i => coefficientLeray k (b k) i - b k i

PREMISE AUDIT = Componentwise RapidScalarFourier for b. Literal gradient Fourier identification additionally requires SpectralReality b.

APPLICATION / ADAPTER = The physical pressure is the real Fourier series. The recovery identity includes all frequencies and yields a smooth mean-zero pressure. RapidScalarFourier here is weighted summability for every derivative order, not an exponential-radius estimate or joint-time analyticity statement.

    (hc : ∀ s : ℕ, Memℓp (sobolevWeightedCoeff s c.val) 2)
    (hreal : SpectralReality c.val) (hsol : SpectralSolenoidal c.val) :

      SpatialPeriodic (fullTransportPressure c) ∧

ME09. ACTUAL QUADRATIC PRESSURE COEFFICIENTS

theorem fullTransportPressure_fourierCoeff_quadratic
    (c : TorusSobolev 0)
    (hc : ∀ s : ℕ, Memℓp (sobolevWeightedCoeff s c.val) 2)
    (hreal : SpectralReality c.val) (hsol : SpectralSolenoidal c.val) (k : Frequency) :
    (∫ x in unitCube, star (fourierCharacter k x) * (fullTransportPressure c x : ℂ)) =
      if k = 0 then 0 else
        -(∑ i : Fin 3, ∑ j : Fin 3, (k i : ℂ) * (k j : ℂ) *
            (∑' p : Frequency, c.val p i * c.val (k - p) j)) /
          (∑ i : Fin 3, (k i : ℂ) ^ 2)

PREMISE AUDIT = All-Sobolev, spectral reality, spectral solenoidality premises as in ME08.

APPLICATION / ADAPTER = Literal cube integral of actual pressure equals the normalized double contraction of the genuinely infinite convolution. No GlobalGalerkinStrongLimit premise is needed for this stationary formula. Can support pressure Poisson/sign validation; new analytic-germ adapters remain.

BODY DEPENDENCIES OBSERVED = infinitePressureCoefficient_fullTransport_quadratic; infiniteTransportPressure_coeff; fullTransport_pressure_pairing.

theorem PHYSICAL_NSE_OF_FULL_FOURIER_RHS (ν : ℝ) (c : TorusSobolev 0)
    (hc : ∀ s : ℕ, Memℓp (sobolevWeightedCoeff s c.val) 2)
    (hreal : SpectralReality c.val) (hsol : SpectralSolenoidal c.val)
