ME11. PERIODIC PRESSURE RECOVERY FOR AN EXISTING GLOBAL STRONG LIMIT

theorem GlobalGalerkinStrongLimit.physical_equation
    {ν : ℝ} {a : Field} {ha : SmoothSigmaZero a} (L : GlobalGalerkinStrongLimit ν a ha)
    (t : ℝ) (ht : 0 ≤ t) (x : Vec) :
    timeDerivative L.velocity t x + convection (L.velocity t) x +
      gradient (L.pressure t) x = ν • laplacian (L.velocity t) x

theorem GlobalGalerkinStrongLimit.globalSmoothNSE
    {ν : ℝ} {a : Field} {ha : SmoothSigmaZero a} (L : GlobalGalerkinStrongLimit ν a ha) :
    GlobalSmoothNSE ν a L.velocity L.pressure

theorem PERIODIC_PRESSURE_RECOVERY
    {ν : ℝ} {a : Field} {ha : SmoothSigmaZero a} (L : GlobalGalerkinStrongLimit ν a ha) :
    GlobalSmoothNSE ν a L.velocity L.pressure ∧
      ∀ t : ℝ, 0 ≤ t → ∀ k : Frequency,
        (∫ x in unitCube, star (fourierCharacter k x) * (L.pressure t x : ℂ)) =
          if k = 0 then 0 else
            -(∑ i : Fin 3, ∑ j : Fin 3, (k i : ℂ) * (k j : ℂ) *
              (∑' p : Frequency, (L.state 0 t).val p i * (L.state 0 t).val (k - p) j)) /
                (∑ i : Fin 3, (k i : ℂ) ^ 2)

PREMISE AUDIT = An explicit L:GlobalGalerkinStrongLimit nu a ha, with ha:SmoothSigmaZero a. L is a required witness.

APPLICATION / ADAPTER = Produces concrete GlobalSmoothNSE plus the quadratic pressure formula at every t>=0. This is C-infinity, mean-zero, forward-time scope. It cannot supply arbitrary-mean analytic germs or the existence of L by definition.

BODY DEPENDENCIES OBSERVED = GlobalSmoothBootstrap; PhysicalNSEIdentity; SobolevPressure; PressureQuadraticCoefficient.

SECTION CONTEXT; included in elaborated theorem parameters:
variable (ν : ℝ) (u : Velocity) (p : Pressure)
variable (hu : CriticalPositiveSolenoidalTest u)
variable (hp : ∀ r : ℝ, 0 < r → ContDiff ℝ ∞ (p r))
variable (hpp : ∀ r : ℝ, 0 < r → SpatialPeriodic (p r))

  timeDerivative u r x + convection (u r) x + gradient (p r) x =

EXACT SOURCE SIGNATURES; namespace SingularClosure:
theorem CRITICAL_SMOOTH_NSE_ENERGY_HAS_DERIV (t : ℝ) (ht : 0 < t) :
    HasDerivAt (fun r => physicalEnergy (u r))
      ((-2 * ν) * physicalDissipation (u t)) t

theorem CRITICAL_SMOOTH_NSE_ENERGY_IDENTITY (hν : 0 < ν)
    (s t : ℝ) (hs : 0 < s) (hst : s ≤ t) :
    IntervalIntegrable (fun r => physicalDissipation (u r)) volume s t ∧
      physicalEnergy (u t) + 2 * ν * (∫ r in s..t, physicalDissipation (u r)) =
        physicalEnergy (u s)

PREMISE AUDIT = CriticalPositiveSolenoidalTest is joint C-infinity on t>0 + periodicity + divergence-free. Pressure is C-infinity/periodic at positive times; actual NSE equation holds there. Integrated identity has nu>0 and 0<s<=t.

APPLICATION / ADAPTER = No mean-zero or Galerkin premise. physicalEnergy is the integral of norm squared, so the differential coefficient is -2*nu. A merely local solution does not satisfy a global t>0 predicate automatically: a local-domain version must be derived from the same proof or justified with a suitable domain adapter. It gives neither analytic continuation nor a global existence theorem.

BODY DEPENDENCIES OBSERVED = critical_hasDerivAt_real_cube_integral; physicalEnergy_hasDerivAt; pressure/convection cancellation; periodic Laplacian IBP; intervalIntegral fundamental theorem.

theorem CRITICAL_LH_SMOOTH_RELATIVE_ENERGY_RAW
    (ν : ℝ) (hν : 0 < ν) (a₀ : CriticalPhysicalL2)
    (v u : Velocity) (G : ℝ → Vec → (Vec →L[ℝ] Vec)) (p : Pressure)
    (hv : CriticalLerayHopf ν a₀ v G)
    (hu : CriticalPositiveSolenoidalTest u)
    (hp : ∀ r : ℝ, 0 < r → ContDiff ℝ ∞ (p r))
    (hpp : ∀ r : ℝ, 0 < r → SpatialPeriodic (p r))
    (hNSE : ∀ r : ℝ, 0 < r → ∀ x : Vec,
      timeDerivative u r x + convection (u r) x + gradient (p r) x =
        ν • laplacian (u r) x)
    (s t : ℝ) (hs : 0 < s) (hst : s ≤ t)
    (henergy : physicalEnergy (v t) +
      2 * ν * (∫ r in s..t, criticalGradientEnergy (G r)) ≤ physicalEnergy (v s)) :
    IntervalIntegrable (fun r => criticalGradientEnergy (G r - fderiv ℝ (u r))) volume s t ∧
    IntervalIntegrable (fun r => criticalWeakTransport (v r) (u r) -
      physicalFieldInner (v r) (convection (u r))) volume s t ∧
    physicalEnergy (v t - u t) +
      2 * ν * (∫ r in s..t, criticalGradientEnergy (G r - fderiv ℝ (u r))) ≤
      physicalEnergy (v s - u s) - 2 * (∫ r in s..t,
        criticalWeakTransport (v r) (u r) - physicalFieldInner (v r) (convection (u r)))

PREMISE AUDIT = Actual CriticalLerayHopf weak solution; actual positive-time smooth solenoidal comparator; pressure smoothness/periodicity; comparator satisfies unforced NSE; 0<s<=t; ordinary weak energy inequality henergy at chosen start/end times.

APPLICATION / ADAPTER = The actual weak momentum and physical H1 gradient structure are present in CriticalLerayHopf, rather than an assumed relative-energy property. henergy is a required admissible-time statement. This endpoint cannot be applied unchanged to a comparator with defect div F: the forcing pairing needs a new residual-aware proof.

BODY DEPENDENCIES OBSERVED = CRITICAL_SMOOTH_NSE_ENERGY_IDENTITY; CRITICAL_WEAK_CLASSICAL_CROSS_IDENTITY; physical H1 weak-gradient identity; quadratic energy algebra.

SEMANTIC EXCLUSIONS
1. The inspected modules do not provide a verified endpoint for the root's
   scalar nonlinear CK equation, analytic Gaussian radial extension, analytic
   Bianchi propagation, Lorentzian curvature/Gauss--Codazzi convention package,
   marked metric-germ quotient, pressure-gauge quotient, or two inverse maps.
   These are NEW_FORMAL_OBLIGATIONS; their mathematical derivations may already
   be B_TIER in the main TXT.
2. CriticalSobolevAnalytic.lean concerns complex interpolation / three-lines
   arguments. Its filename does not supply joint analytic NSE regularization
   or Cauchy--Kowalevski.
3. FiniteHessian.lean concerns finite Fourier synthesis. It does not identify
   the full Hessian of arbitrary jointly analytic scalar completion data.
4. No generic wrapper, certificate record, or theorem name licenses substituting
   an arbitrary regularity predicate for an actual PDE solution.
5. No unrestricted NSE global regularity claim follows from the small-data roots.
6. Direct source lexical inspection found no sorry/admit/axiom declaration in
   the selected source files. This is not a theorem-level transitive axiom audit.

NEW FORMAL ADAPTERS REQUIRED BY THIS ROOT
FA01. Actual unit-period analytic-germ field representation, Fourier transform,
      and normalization; equivalence with the marked-domain definitions.
FA02. Mean-zero scalar inverse Laplacian with joint analytic t,x dependence,
      mean normalization, spatial/time derivative commutation, and P_0=I.
FA03. Analytic Helmholtz decomposition and reconstruction in actual fields.
FA04. Lorentzian 5D metric, h/K conventions, vacuum Gauss--Codazzi, contracted
      Bianchi, and the exact signed coordinate formulas used by the TXT.
FA05. Noncharacteristic scalar CK existence/uniqueness, including the nonzero
      coefficient and uniform local periodic gluing.
FA06. Analytic radial CK with Ric_ab=0 and constraint propagation to full Ric=0.
FA07. Pressure-gauge invariance, marked metric-germ equality, both inverse laws.
FA08. Local-slab energy/cancellation adapters; residual-forcing relative energy
      only if a stability corollary is included.

Readiness classification:

  analytic Einstein / scalar completion / germ-equivalence -> FA04-FA07.
This classification records interface applicability, not formal promotion.

Pinned workflow cache policy:
  dependency path = package/.lake/packages;
  dependency key = singular-ax6-deps-${runner.os}-${runner.arch}-
                   hash(lean-toolchain,lake-manifest.json);
  project path = package/.lake/build;
  project key = singular-ax6-build-v2-${runner.os}-${runner.arch}-
                hash(toolchain,manifest)-hash(lakefile,root module,all source);
  restore prefix includes OS, architecture and pinned dependency hash;
  explicit lake exe cache get follows restore;
  version, Mathlib revision and lockfile/source checks follow cache retrieval;
  endpoint-specific compile and exact-type/axiom audits follow.
The YAML disables automatic setup-action caches, then uses these explicit
restore/save steps. The false setup flags do not mean caching is absent.

For the user's later external native Windows run, within the pinned package:
  1. Verify immutable source revision, lean-toolchain, lake-manifest.json,
     native Windows platform, and Lean 4.33.0 toolchain.
  2. Restore only compatible cached dependency/project objects. Do not treat
     Linux artifacts or a 4.34.0-rc1 cache as interchangeable with 4.33.0 Windows.
  3. Run lake exe cache get before the build, and verify Mathlib revision equals
     db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d.
  4. Build the actual selected modules / exact new root and all new adapters.
  5. Record the exact theorem-type check and #print axioms for each final
     imported endpoint plus the exact GR--NSE root; reject sorryAx and every
     axiom outside the expressly approved set.
  6. Retain compiler version, source hashes, lockfile, cache log, build log,
     exact-type results and theorem-level axiom results for the same revision.
  7. Promote only after matching external evidence; cached objects alone do
     not establish either A_TIER or discharge an analytic/semantic premise.

The existing run.cmd additionally runs check_sources.py, checker self-tests,
lake build, build_critical.py, ci/Audit.lean, and ci/check_axioms.py. A future
root-specific audit must add the new GR--NSE declarations; the existing audit
list cannot certify declarations not in that list.

25. PROOF-SPINE INDEX AND EXTERNAL FORMALIZATION CONTRACT

25.1. Evidence vocabulary.

  ! B_TIER:
    the exact mathematical statement has the complete proof in this TXT.

  ML01-ML42 / ME01-ME18:
    exact declarations or declaration groups inspected in pinned source;
    imports, signatures, hypotheses and source URLs are recorded above.

  SOURCE_VERIFIED_SUPPORT / SOURCE_VERIFIED_ONLY:
    source inspection only; no compilation of the new application is implied.

  NP01-NP16:
    new proof-task identifiers defined below; these are not names of imported
    Lean declarations. No axiom or sorry is supplied for them by this TXT.

  A_TIER / FORMAL_CLOSURE / UNCONDITIONAL_CLOSURE:
    not established for the GR--NSE root by this revision.

25.2. New proof contracts.

NP01. Analytic periodic representation.
  V13_REFINEMENT: CG04-CG05; exact pressure-gauge to velocity-germ representation; ME18 arbitrary-mean realization.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: AF01, AF04; common complex domain and actual periodic synthesis.
  Domain: analytic germs along {t0} x T^3, arbitrary vector mean.
  Prove: equivalence with real periodic fields on R^3 on local time intervals;
  coordinate derivatives, products, restrictions, and pressure gauge commute
  with the representation. Choose the exact neighborhood filters and charts.
  Support: ML01-ML04, ML07-ML09, ML20-ML23; ME06.

NP02. Analytic Poisson and Helmholtz operators.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: AF01-AF09; each analytic operator and derivative interface is separately specified.
  Domain: jointly analytic periodic scalar/vector families, with explicit
  zero-mean premise for scalar inversion.
  Prove: Delta q=f, <q>=0, P=I-grad Delta0^-1 div, P_hat(0)=I on the zero
  Fourier mode, derivative commutation, reality, and analytic preservation
  on a smaller common domain. The linear field operator itself has P(0)=0.
  Support: ML01-ML09; ME01-ME09. Integer Sobolev or rapid decay alone is not a
  proof of analytic preservation; retain exponential bounds/common strips.

NP03. Lorentzian finite tensor algebra.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: CJ01-CJ03, CJ05, CJ08-CJ09; finite normal maps and metric jets.
  Domain: Fin 4 spacetime indices, Fin 3 spatial indices; eta=(-1,1,1,1).
  Prove: index raising/lowering, trace reversal, H(S+C), coefficient
  2(rho+Delta psi), K=K0-Hess psi, explicit pressure and initial K00 formulas.
  Support: ML11-ML19. These finite algebra bodies can be proved independently
  of CK existence after their exact tensor signatures have been fixed.

NP04. Actual-field conservation and residual.
  V13_REFINEMENT: CG01-CG04; exact equation residual, pressure minimization and velocity-only equation.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: AF10 and ST07; actual pressure and orthogonal forcing identities.
  Prove: NSE iff div S=0; div C[psi]=0; mean beta conservation; reconstruction;
  R_nu(v,p_*)=div F_nu[K]; canonical zero-defect implication and pressure gauge.
  Support: ML07-ML12; ME01,ME02,ME06-ME10; depends on NP01-NP03 and geometric
  Codazzi from NP06 where vacuum is used.

NP05. Nonlinear scalar analytic Cauchy theorem.
  V13_REFINEMENT: CK01-CK03 and CK05-CK12 provide the constructive existence proof underlying CJ13.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: CJ01-CJ04, CJ13-CJ15; one common CK existence engine and independent jet uniqueness.
  Prove: existence and uniqueness for equation (7.5), zero scalar Cauchy data,
  rho!=0, actual convergence, periodic compatibility and common local interval.
  Support: ML07-ML10,ML13-ML21; NONE is a complete CK endpoint.
  This is a substantive analytic formalization task, not a denominator tactic.

NP06. Gaussian Lorentzian geometry and contracted Bianchi.
  V13_REFINEMENT: GE01-GE12 provide explicit actual-coordinate proofs, including both Bianchi contractions.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: CJ05-CJ06, CJ10; actual inverse, finite Ricci map and signed Bianchi system.
  Prove: actual 5D metric/Ricci/Einstein definitions and equations (9.2),
  Gauss-Codazzi, G_rr formula (9.5), and the homogeneous system (9.4), with
  K=+(1/2)partial_r h and the declared normal orientation.
  Support: finite calculus/algebra ML07-ML19; no complete verified source
  endpoint for this signed geometry package was located in the bounded audit.

NP07. Radial analytic existence and constraint propagation.
  V13_REFINEMENT: GE07-GE11 plus CK12 replace the unsplit geometric and CK mathematical steps.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: CJ05-CJ16; no separate subsidiary existence theorem is required.
  Prove: analytic local h satisfying (9.3) and data (9.1), analytic uniqueness,
  nondegeneracy/signature persistence, compact torus collar, and full Ric(g)=0
  by zero-constraint propagation. Depends on NP05-type CK machinery and NP06.
  Support: ML07-ML21. A mere definition of a vacuum witness does not discharge
  its existence or the full unprescribed Einstein components.

NP08. Pressure-gauge action.
  V13_REFINEMENT: CG06-CG09 and ML34-ML39; exact time primitive, gauge action and complete tensor fibres.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: CJ04, CJ08, CJ14-CJ15; actual analytic uniqueness and common-domain restriction.
  Prove: analytic f''=-a with zero data; S[u,p+a]+C[psi+f]=S[u,p]+C[psi];
  equality of K and metric germs; quotient representative independence.
  Support: ML07-ML12,ML22,ML23; analytic uniqueness from NP05 and NP07.

NP09. Exact germ equivalence.
  V13_REFINEMENT: CG05 and CG09-CG10 give a canonical velocity-only implementation of the same inverse laws.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: CJ04, CJ08, CJ14-CJ15 feed both inverse laws; Section 30 records the assembly.
  Prove: lift/reconstruction well-defined in the actual germ types;
  R o L=id and L o R=id; package the proven functions using Equiv.
  Support: ML22-ML25; requires NP01-NP08. Equiv.bijective is only the final
  structural consequence; it supplies no missing analytic proof.

NP10. Initial-jet and realized-trace rigidity.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: AF09, AF11, CJ14; differentiated Fourier recurrence and analytic equality.
  Prove: formulas (I.1)-(I.3), projected NSE recurrence (I.4), and initial
  velocity trace injectivity for existing analytic germs, including the
  non-realizability counterexample (I.6) at its initial endpoint.
  Support: ML07-ML12,ML17-ML19; ME06,ME08,ME10; NP16 for a spectral proof.
  The statement does not assert convergence of arbitrary NSE initial jets.

NP11. Scalar-potential-free tensor admission.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: ST01-ST07 add the orthogonal stress diagnostic; the original STF-Curl proof is retained.
  Prove: row-Curl/Hessian identity, STF Fourier coercivity, zero-mode removal,
  constants 3/(4*pi^2) and 1/(16*pi^4), and equivalences (II.3),(II.8).
  Support: ML01-ML06,ML10,ML17-ML19; ME05,ME06.
  Required finite symbol inequality: for complex symmetric trace-free A and
  real unit e, |A e|^2 <=(2/3)|A|_F^2. Lift it to actual real tensor fields by
  componentwise Parseval with the unit-period normalization and the explicit
  Frobenius finite-sum norm. Any Lean tensor representation must prove this
  norm identification; no default Matrix norm is silently interchangeable.

NP12. Viscosity observable and rigidity.
  Prove: periodic transport/Hessian cancellations, strain norm identity,
  exact quotient (III.1), sector intersection (III.2), and defect bounds
  (III.3)-(III.4). Support: ML05,ML11,ML12; ME01,ME03,ME12,ME13.
  Dividing by ||Z(v)|| requires the explicit nonconstant-flow premise.

NP13. Tensor-forced relative energy and Gronwall.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: ST05-ST08; exact projected-force norm and factor 1/(2nu) residual bound.
  Prove: (IV.1), the integrating-factor estimate (IV.2), matched-mean rate
  4*pi^2*nu for defective flows, and rate 8*pi^2*nu for canonical flows.
  Support: ML05,ML11,ML12; ME12-ME14; periodic gap and NP15.
  ME14 concerns an actual weak solution and an unforced smooth comparator;
  its type does not contain the new two-smooth-flow tensor forcing.

NP14. Explicit Einstein examples.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: CJ05-CJ09, CJ16 for finite inverse, curvature and signature calculations.
  Prove: positivity of a(r) on a chosen collar, the Ricci calculation for g0,
  flatness of g1, their exact K tensors, and strictness of canonical admission.
  Support: ML07-ML19 and NP06; finite symbolic identities are independent edges.

NP15. Local time-domain and physical-integral adapter.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: ST08 uses Q0 z in the mean-zero homogeneous Sobolev pairing; retain local time domains.
  Prove: restriction/time translation of the required calculus identities to
  an existing local slab; agreement of torus Haar integrals with periodic cube
  integrals; componentwise integration by parts and pressure cancellation.
  Support: ML02,ML05,ML11,ML12; ME06,ME12,ME13.
  A predicate quantified over all t>0 cannot be obtained merely by renaming
  a local solution. Reprove the local identity or use a justified local theorem.

NP16. Spectral time-derivative adapter.
  V13_REFINEMENT: ME18 supplies actual spatial realization; temporal analytic differentiation remains AF09/ML28.
  ATOMIC_REFINEMENT: AF09-AF10; ML28 differentiated limits and ME16 longitudinal pressure sign.
  Prove: for the chosen c(t), physical partial_t u equals synthesis of the
  coefficient derivative. For the unforced equation, the exact Fourier ODE
  identifies that derivative with fullFourierRhs nu c(t).
  Required analytic justification: the time-differentiated synthesis series
  converges locally uniformly on a common smaller domain, established by a
  summable majorant, or an equivalent continuous-linear-map differentiation
  argument. Uniform convergence of the undifferentiated series is insufficient.
  Forced variant: if R_nu(u,p)=div F, retain
    partial_t c(k)=fullFourierRhs nu c(t)(k)
      +coefficientLeray k (FourierCoeff(div F) k).
  Prove the longitudinal pressure correction
    grad(p-p_transport)=(I-P)div F.
  The bare fullFourierRhs evolution applies only to the unforced equation or
  after the projected forcing has been proved zero. The pressure correction
  remains necessary whenever the longitudinal forcing is nonzero.
  Support: ME01,ME02,ME07-ME10 plus the derivative-convergence proof above.
  ME10 is a spatial RHS identity and does not itself assert a time evolution.

25.3. Deterministic proof composition.

Base signatures:
  NP01 representations and actual semantic types;
  NP03 tensor definitions; NP06 geometric definitions/conventions.

Independent algebra/calculus edges:
  conserved tensor; scalar correction; Hamiltonian expansion; trace reversal;
  initial K00 solve; pressure-gauge tensor invariance; STF symbol inequality.

Analytic edges:
  NP02 analytic Fourier/Poisson/Leray;
  NP05 scalar CK;
  NP06 geometric identities;
  NP07 radial CK and propagation;
  local-to-torus compatibility and common collar.

Inverse-map edges:
  NP04 reconstruction -> NP08 gauge compatibility -> NP09 exact inverse laws.

Final root:
  NP01-NP09 -> Equiv actual_NSE_germs actual_GR_sector_germs
  by the direct actual-field proof.
  If ME10 supplies any temporal NSE conclusion inside NP04 or NP10, include
  NP16 as an additional implementation dependency, with its forced variant
  wherever the forcing is nonzero. The direct physical proof of NP13 requires
  no spectral route.

Optional strengthening edges:
  NP10 initial traces;
  NP11 admission diagnostics;
  NP12 viscosity recovery;
  NP13 plus NP15-NP16 defect stability where required;
  NP14 explicit geometric examples.

Scheduling contract for a later A-tier implementation:
  Each independent opaque edge receives its exact endpoint signature.
  Native Lean asynchronous elaboration may schedule independent proof bodies.
  The final chain uses only the exact declared theorem types after those bodies
  pass the external kernel. Scheduling must not change hypotheses, objects,
  axioms, normalizations, or the root conclusion.

25.4. Cache and promotion contract.

  Execution = external only.
  Compiler = Lean 4.33.0 for this immutable source alignment.
  Mathlib = db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d.
  Methlib = 09dfe94a1ef150e21d09df3013fbe09002ef139c.
  Reuse compatible OS/architecture/toolchain/manifest caches before elaboration.
  The exact inspected cache keys and external run sequence are in Section 24.
  Preserve the lockfile; an upgrade is a separate source/toolchain migration.

  Required external evidence for A_TIER of the exact implication:
    compiler version + exact source hashes + exact dependency manifest;
    successful build of the exact new theorem and its imported adapters;
    exact theorem-type check;
    theorem-level axiom closure with no sorryAx or unapproved axiom;
    classification of every retained certificate, witness, checker endpoint,
    admission, semantic, analytic or existence premise.

  A_TIER and FORMAL_CLOSURE apply to the externally checked exact implication
  from its explicitly recorded premises to its conclusion. If named unresolved
  certificate, witness, checker, admission, semantic or analytic premises
  remain, record CONDITIONAL_FORMAL_CLOSURE; do not relabel them as proved.

  For the present root without additional unresolved premises, discharge all
  new formal proof obligations needed to derive it from its declared analytic
  domain hypotheses. The parameters nu>0, rho!=0, periodicity, joint analyticity
  and declared germ domains remain its exact mathematical scope.

  UNCONDITIONAL_CLOSURE additionally requires discharge of every unresolved
  certificate, witness, checker endpoint, admission, semantic and analytic
  premise. Ordinary scope hypotheses remain in the exact root statement.

  Declaring NP05/NP07 or geometric facts as axioms does not establish this
  root unconditionally. A chain parametrized by those statements records
  their unresolved formal status explicitly and cannot receive !!!!.

V.0. Definitions and normalization.

The spatial domain is T^3=(R/Z)^3, with normalized Haar measure. Frequencies
are k in Z^3 and characters exp(2*pi*i*k.x). Write |k|_1=sum_j |k_j| and
|k|_2=(sum_j |k_j|^2)^(1/2). Every coefficient codomain E is a fixed
finite-dimensional complex Hilbert space, equipped with its Euclidean norm;
matrix and higher tensor codomains use their componentwise Frobenius norm.

For sigma>=0, define

  W_sigma(E):={a:Z^3 -> E : ||a||_sigma<infinity},
  ||a||_sigma:=sum_k |a(k)| exp(2*pi*sigma*|k|_1).       (AF.1)

When a is the Fourier sequence of f, use ||f||_sigma for ||a||_sigma.
This is a coefficient l1 norm, not a physical L1 norm.

The map a(k) -> exp(2*pi*sigma*|k|_1)a(k) is an isometric bijection from
W_sigma(E) to the ordinary absolutely summable E-valued sequences. Therefore
W_sigma(E) is a Banach space. If 0<=sigma'<=sigma, then
||a||_sigma'<=||a||_sigma.

All constants below refer to the unit torus. No default Lean Matrix norm may
replace the declared Frobenius norm without a proved identification.

V.1. Common holomorphic tube from the actual analytic germ.

THEOREM AF_COMMON_TUBE.
Let f be jointly real analytic on J_delta x T^3. After restriction there are
R>0, a>0 and a periodic holomorphic extension

  F:D_R(t0) x {z in C^3: max_j |Im z_j|<a} -> E_C

of f. The radii may depend on the given germ. For any smaller closed time
disc and closed spatial tube, F is bounded on one spatial period cell.

Proof.
Lift f periodically to J_delta x R^3. At each real point of the compact
slice {t0} x [0,1]^3, convergent real power series give a holomorphic extension
to a complex polydisc centered at that real point. Choose each real polydisc
inside the original lifted domain. Two such complex polydiscs have convex
intersection. If their complex intersection is nonempty, their real
polydiscs have nonempty open intersection: for every coordinate, two discs
with real centers intersect only if the corresponding real open intervals
intersect. On this real open product the two extensions equal f. Repeated
one-variable identity theorems therefore identify them on the connected
complex intersection. Hence the extensions glue.

Include all integer spatial translates of the selected polydiscs, with
translated local extensions. Their equality on real overlaps follows from
periodicity, so the same argument proves periodic gluing. A finite subcover
of the compact slice gives an open complex neighborhood of that slice.
Compactness supplies positive uniform radii R,a whose product tube, modulo
the spatial lattice, is contained in this neighborhood. Strictly smaller
closed discs/tubes over [0,1]^3 are compact; continuity gives boundedness.
QED.

This proof supplies a common complex domain from the existing analytic germ.
It does not assume common Fourier bounds as an independent analytic premise.

V.2. Quantified exponential Fourier decay.

Suppose F is holomorphic and periodic on a neighborhood of

  closure(D_R(t0)) x {z: max_j |Im z_j|<=a},

and bounded in norm by M there. Define

  c_k(tau)=integral_[0,1]^3 F(tau,x) exp(-2*pi*i*k.x) dx.

Then c_k is holomorphic in tau and

  |c_k(tau)|<=M exp(-2*pi*a*|k|_1), tau in D_R.         (AF.2)

Proof.
Holomorphy of the integral follows by locally uniform differentiation of F
on the compact real cube, or directly by its parameter Cauchy integral
formula. Shift the j-th integration segment to
Im z_j=-a sign(k_j), one coordinate at a time. The vertical edge integrals
cancel by period one and the integral is unchanged by Cauchy's theorem.
On the shifted cube, the exponential factor has modulus
exp(-2*pi*a*|k|_1). The measure of the real cube is one. QED.

If bounds are available only on an open tube, first choose smaller a,R with
closed product contained in that tube; AF_COMMON_TUBE supplies such choices.

For 0<=sigma<a, put

  S(a-sigma):=((1+exp(-2*pi*(a-sigma)))
             /(1-exp(-2*pi*(a-sigma))))^3.

The product of the three elementary geometric series gives

  sup_(tau in D_R) ||c(tau)||_sigma <= M S(a-sigma).    (AF.3)

No unspecified summability or rapid-decay hypothesis remains at this step.

V.3. Synthesis, coefficient identification and real descent.

For a in W_sigma(E), sigma>0, define

  Syn(a)(z)=sum_k a(k) exp(2*pi*i*k.z).

The series converges absolutely and uniformly on every closed tube of width
at most sigma. It is holomorphic in the open tube of width sigma, and

  sup_(max |Im z_j|<=sigma) |Syn(a)(z)| <= ||a||_sigma. (AF.4)

Uniform convergence on the real cube permits integration term by term.
Orthogonality of characters gives FourierCoeff(Syn(a))(k)=a(k), so synthesis
is injective. For the coefficients obtained from F in Subsection V.2, the
existing Fourier reconstruction endpoint ML04 identifies Syn(c(tau)) with
F(tau) on the real torus. Holomorphic identity then gives equality throughout
their common tube. Equivalently one may use ML03 plus continuity to upgrade
an L2 equality, but the direct absolute-summability route is shorter.

For real fields, c_-k(t)=conjugate(c_k(t)) at real t. Conversely this symmetry
makes Syn(c) real on the real torus, by conjugating the absolutely convergent
series and replacing k with -k. For a joint holomorphic extension of a real
field, c_-k(conjugate(tau))=conjugate(c_k(tau)).

V.4. Product/convolution estimate.

Let B:E x F -> G be a complex bilinear map satisfying
|B(e,f)|<=C_B |e| |f|. Define its coefficient convolution

  B_*(a,b)(k)=sum_l B(a(l),b(k-l)).

Then

  ||B_*(a,b)||_sigma <= C_B ||a||_sigma ||b||_sigma,   (AF.5)
  Syn(B_*(a,b))=B(Syn(a),Syn(b)).

Proof.
The triangle inequality |k|_1<=|l|_1+|k-l|_1 implies submultiplicativity of
the exponential weights. Absolute double summation and Tonelli give

 sum_k sum_l |B(a(l),b(k-l))|w_sigma(k)
 <= C_B sum_l |a(l)|w_sigma(l) sum_m |b(m)|w_sigma(m).

Thus all rearrangements are justified and the physical product identity
follows by multiplying the two absolutely convergent Fourier series. QED.

In particular, C_B=1 for scalar multiplication, vector tensor products, and
Frobenius matrix multiplication. Hence

  ||u tensor v||_sigma<=||u||_sigma ||v||_sigma.        (AF.6)

V.5. Explicit spatial radius loss.

Let alpha=(alpha_1,alpha_2,alpha_3) be a multi-index, alpha!=product_j alpha_j!,
|alpha|=sum_j alpha_j. For 0<=sigma'<sigma and d=sigma-sigma',

  ||partial_x^alpha f||_sigma'
    <= alpha! d^(-|alpha|) ||f||_sigma.               (AF.7)

Proof.
The coefficient is product_j (2*pi*i*k_j)^alpha_j c_k. For every nonnegative
x and integer n, x^n<=n! d^(-n)exp(d*x), by one term of the exponential
series. Apply this with x=2*pi*|k_j| for each coordinate, multiply, and sum
the weighted coefficient norms. Normal convergence on smaller tubes
justifies the actual derivative identity. QED.

For the full Euclidean derivative tensor D^m, the same argument with
2*pi*|k|_2<=2*pi*|k|_1 gives

  ||D^m f||_sigma'<=m! d^(-m)||f||_sigma.             (AF.8)

For m>0, the sharper scalar bound sup_(x>=0) x^m exp(-d*x)=(m/(e*d))^m
may replace m!d^(-m), when the corresponding derivative tensor multiplier
has norm bounded by (2*pi*|k|_1)^m. The factorial estimate suffices for the
formal dependency reduction.

V.6. Quantified Poisson and Helmholtz operators without spatial radius loss.

Let Q0 remove the zero Fourier mode, and define G=Delta0^-1 Q0 by

  (Gf)_hat(0)=0,
  (Gf)_hat(k)=-f_hat(k)/(4*pi^2*|k|_2^2), k!=0.

Then for every sigma>=0,

  ||Gf||_sigma <= (1/(4*pi^2)) ||Q0 f||_sigma,         (AF.9)
  ||grad Gf||_sigma <= (1/(2*pi)) ||Q0 f||_sigma,      (AF.10)
  ||Hess Gf||_sigma = ||Q0 f||_sigma.                 (AF.11)

Proof.
For a nonzero lattice frequency, |k|_2>=1. The scalar inverse symbol has
absolute value 1/(4*pi^2*|k|_2^2); the gradient symbol has Euclidean norm
1/(2*pi*|k|_2). The Hessian symbol is k tensor k / |k|_2^2 and its Frobenius
norm is exactly one. Multiply by each scalar coefficient, then sum.
These constants are sharp, for example on cos(2*pi*x_1). QED.

Define P_hat(0)=I and, for k!=0,

  P_hat(k)=I-k tensor k/|k|_2^2.

The nonzero-frequency symbols are orthogonal projections for the Hermitian
Euclidean norm on C^3. Thus

  ||P v||_sigma<=||v||_sigma,
  ||(I-P)v||_sigma<=||v||_sigma.                     (AF.12)

The coefficients give exactly

  Delta Gf=Q0 f, <Gf>=0,
  P=I-grad Delta0^-1 div,
  div(Pv)=0, P^2=P, P grad f=0,
  Pv=v whenever div v=0.                            (AF.13)

Both differential identities and the zero-mode statements are proved at
coefficient level and passed through the injective synthesis. All these
operators commute with spatial differentiation where defined and with
time differentiation under the estimates in Subsection V.7. Their symbols
preserve the conjugate-symmetry condition, so they descend to actual real
analytic periodic fields. The norm-one Leray bound is sharp on any nonzero
spatially constant vector field or transverse single Fourier mode.

V.7. Time derivatives and joint analytic reconstruction.

Let each c_k be holomorphic on D_R(t0), and suppose

  M_sigma:=sup_(tau in D_R) sum_k |c_k(tau)|w_sigma(k)<infinity.

Then for 0<=r<R and every q>=0,

  sup_(tau in D_r) ||partial_tau^q c(tau)||_sigma
    <= q! (R-r)^(-q) M_sigma.                        (AF.14)

Proof.
For fixed tau in D_r, apply the coefficientwise Cauchy formula on a circle
centered at tau of radius b<R-|tau-t0|. Take coefficient norms, multiply by
w_sigma(k), sum, and interchange the nonnegative sum and the circle
integral. The integrand sum is <=M_sigma. This yields q! M_sigma/b^q;
let b increase to R-|tau-t0|. The resulting bound is uniform on D_r. QED.

The map tau -> c(tau) is itself W_sigma-valued holomorphic. Indeed, for a
circle of radius b around tau, the coefficientwise Taylor coefficients
satisfy ||c^(q)(tau)||_sigma<=q! M_sigma/b^q. Consequently their power series
converges absolutely in W_sigma for |h|<b, agrees with c(tau+h) coefficient
by coefficient, and hence agrees in W_sigma. This proves Banach-valued
holomorphy without inferring it from unproved termwise differentiation.

Combining AF.7 and AF.14 gives the explicit mixed bound

  sup_(tau in D_r) ||partial_tau^q partial_x^alpha f(tau)||_sigma'
    <= q! alpha! M_sigma /
       ((R-r)^q (sigma-sigma')^|alpha|).              (AF.15)

Actual differentiation of synthesis follows from locally uniform normal
convergence of the differentiated series. One direct majorant uses the
coefficientwise consequence

  |c_k^(q)(tau)|<=q! M_sigma (R-r)^(-q)w_sigma(k)^(-1)

and a strictly smaller spatial tube. Every fixed polynomial in |k| is
summable against the remaining exponential decay. Thus the time derivative
of synthesis is the synthesis of the coefficient derivative, and the same
holds for all mixed derivatives. This supplies the analytic part of NP16.

In the actual application, AF_COMMON_TUBE and AF.2-AF.3 provide M_sigma.
The common-domain and derivative-majorant premises are therefore derived
from the original analytic germ, not added to the root as new assumptions.

V.8. Analytic pressure and Lipschitz dependence.

Let u in W_sigma(C^3) be divergence free and let F in W_sigma(C^{3x3}),
with conjugate symmetry for real physical fields. At a time of an existing
smooth or analytic solution of the actual equation

  partial_t u+(u.grad)u+grad p-nu Delta u=div F,

its unique mean-zero pressure is

  p0=Delta0^-1 divdiv(F-u tensor u).                  (AF.16)

This formula follows by taking the divergence and using div u=0. The
right side has zero mean because it is a double divergence. For nonzero k,
the multiplier A_hat(k) -> (k tensor k):A_hat(k)/|k|_2^2 has norm one from
the Frobenius tensor space to the scalar space. Therefore

  ||p0||_sigma<=||F||_sigma+||u||_sigma^2.             (AF.17)

For two pairs (u,F),(v,G), with u,v in W_sigma(C^3), F,G in
W_sigma(C^{3x3}), and their corresponding mean-zero pressures,

  ||p0(u,F)-p0(v,G)||_sigma
   <= ||F-G||_sigma
      +(||u||_sigma+||v||_sigma)||u-v||_sigma.         (AF.18)

Proof.
Subtract AF.16 and use
u tensor u-v tensor v=u tensor (u-v)+(u-v) tensor v,
the norm-one pressure multiplier, and AF.6. QED.

For the unforced equation, set F=0. Denote
p_transport=-Delta0^-1 divdiv(u tensor u). Then the forced correction is

  p0-p_transport=Delta0^-1 divdiv F,
  grad(p0-p_transport)=(I-P)div F.                   (AF.19)

This is the same sign as the forced NP16 contract. Subtracting this
correction from a pressure pstar that satisfies R_nu(u,pstar)=div F gives
R_nu(u,pstar-Delta0^-1 divdiv F)=P divF.

The projected NSE right-hand side is

  B_nu(u)=nu Delta u-P div(u tensor u).

For 0<=sigma'<sigma and d=sigma-sigma', AF.5-AF.8 imply

  ||B_nu(u)||_sigma'
   <=(2nu/d^2)||u||_sigma+(1/d)||u||_sigma^2,         (AF.20)

  ||B_nu(u)-B_nu(v)||_sigma'
   <=[2nu/d^2+(||u||_sigma+||v||_sigma)/d]
      ||u-v||_sigma.                                (AF.21)

For the divergence multiplier, |A k|<=|A|_F|k|_2. Leray projection is
contractive, so its single-derivative radius loss is bounded by 1/d.
The Laplacian multiplier is bounded by the second-derivative estimate 2/d^2.

AF.20-AF.21 are estimates on a decreasing analytic scale. They do not give a
same-space Banach ODE theorem, time analyticity for arbitrary analytic
initial traces, or a global existence result. The realized-trace restriction
and the shear counterexample from Section 19 remain unchanged.

V.9. Atomic proof decomposition and existing endpoint placement.

The following AFxx identifiers are proof-task labels only. They are not
names of existing Lean declarations. Each body is mathematically proved in
the numbered sections above; its exact Lean implementation remains new.

AF01 COMMON_TUBE
  INPUT: actual jointly real-analytic periodic representative near the slice.
  OUTPUT: common complex time disc and spatial tube; periodic holomorphic
          extension; boundedness on smaller closed products.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML20,ML21 for compactness; ML07-ML09 for analytic calculus.
  NEW_BODY: complexification, overlap identity, periodic gluing, uniform tube.
  EXISTING_TARGET: NP01 and NP02.

AF02 WEIGHTED_L1_SPACE
  INPUT: finite-dimensional Euclidean/Frobenius codomain, sigma>=0.
  OUTPUT: W_sigma completeness, norm and radius monotonicity.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: no complete endpoint in the primary ML register in Section 23.
  NEW_BODY: isometry with weighted/unweighted absolutely summable sequences.
  EXISTING_TARGET: NP02.

AF03 COEFFICIENT_DECAY
  INPUT: AF01 plus boundedness on a closed product.
  OUTPUT: AF.2 and explicit AF.3.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML01,ML02 supply the actual Fourier coefficient definition
                  and cube integral; no indexed endpoint supplies the contour
                  shift or exponential estimate.
  NEW_BODY: parameter integral, periodic contour shift, geometric series.
  EXISTING_TARGET: NP02 and NP16.

AF04 SYNTHESIS_AND_REALITY
  INPUT: W_sigma sequence or AF03 coefficients.
  OUTPUT: AF.4, actual coefficient identity, reconstruction, real descent.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML01,ML02,ML04; ME06 continuous periodic injectivity.
  NEW_BODY: normal convergence on complex tubes; conjugate symmetry.
  EXISTING_TARGET: NP01 and NP02.

AF05 PRODUCT_CONVOLUTION
  INPUT: bounded complex bilinear B and two W_sigma sequences.
  OUTPUT: AF.5-AF.6 and actual product identity.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML13 covers analytic multiplication only; the absolute
                  convolution/rearrangement and weighted estimate remain new.
  NEW_BODY: weight submultiplicativity and absolutely summable double series.
  EXISTING_TARGET: NP02 and NP04.

AF06 SPATIAL_DERIVATIVE_SCALE
  INPUT: sigma'<sigma, W_sigma sequence.
  OUTPUT: AF.7-AF.8 and coefficient derivative symbols.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML06 one-dimensional derivative coefficient identity;
                  ML07-ML09 analytic derivative regularity.
  NEW_BODY: finite-coordinate iteration, common-domain derivative series,
            elementary exponential/factorial estimate.
  EXISTING_TARGET: NP02.

AF07 POISSON_BOUNDS
  INPUT: scalar W_sigma field, zero-mode extraction.
  OUTPUT: AF.9-AF.11, Delta inverse identity and zero mean.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML01-ML06,ML26-ML27; ME05,ME17 for Sobolev compatibility
                  only, not for the new exponential-weight norm.
  NEW_BODY: analytic weighted norm, Euclidean/Frobenius exact symbol norms,
            actual synthesis and uniqueness.
  EXISTING_TARGET: NP02.

AF08 LERAY_BOUNDS
  INPUT: vector W_sigma field, arbitrary mean.
  OUTPUT: AF.12-AF.13, including P_hat(0)=I.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ME01,ME03,ME04 actual Leray algebra and norm bounds;
                  ML01-ML04 actual torus reconstruction.
  NEW_BODY: W_sigma transfer and analytic descent.
  EXISTING_TARGET: NP02.

AF09 TIME_DERIVATIVE_SCALE
  INPUT: holomorphic coefficients locally bounded in W_sigma, supplied by AF03.
  OUTPUT: AF.14-AF.15; derivative synthesis and Banach-valued holomorphy.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML07-ML09, ML26-ML28; ML28 uses the proved differentiated
                  local-uniform convergence. ME10 supplies only the
                  spatial physical RHS identity after this adapter is proved.
  NEW_BODY: coefficient Cauchy formula, sum/integral interchange, derivative
            majorants and locally uniform differentiated synthesis.
  EXISTING_TARGET: NP16; also NP02 parameter commutation.

AF10 PRESSURE_ESTIMATES
  INPUT: divergence-free actual u and forced tensor F with analytic norms.
  OUTPUT: AF.16-AF.19, including Lipschitz dependence and longitudinal correction.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ME02,ME07-ME09,ME16 pressure recovery and coefficients;
                  the sharper Frobenius norm-one bound is a new symbol proof.
  NEW_BODY: analytic Frobenius pressure-symbol contraction and weighted products.
  EXISTING_TARGET: NP04 and forced NP16.

AF11 NSE_SCALE_RHS
  INPUT: two analytic vector fields on radius sigma and sigma'<sigma.
  OUTPUT: AF.20-AF.21; derivative-loss bounds for the actual projected RHS.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ME10 plus AF04-AF10; ML11,ML12 finite calculus.
  NEW_BODY: combine actual RHS identity with the explicit analytic-scale norms.
  EXISTING_TARGET: NP10 and NP16, without creating an NSE existence theorem.

CHECKPOINT CONNECTIONS:
  CP03: AF01-AF09, replacing the analytic-preservation umbrella with these edges.
  CP05: AF10 and AF09 for actual forced pressure and temporal Fourier use.
  CP07: AF05-AF07 supply analytic tensor/scalar operations only, not CK.

  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML54.

  V14_REFINEMENT = FC07-FC10 and GD01-GD06; finite CK certificates and vertical overlap identity.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML43-ML49; ME19.

  V13_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML36,ML40-ML42.

VI.A. SCALAR NORMAL MAP WITH AN EXPLICIT NONCHARACTERISTIC MARGIN
=======================================================================

Put s=t-t0. Let u,p be any jointly analytic periodic pair; the scalar
existence argument itself does not use the NSE equation. Define the actual
source tensors S_rho and A=S_rho,sp as in Section 6, and write Hscr=H(S_rho).
For a symmetric real 3x3 matrix Q and vector b in R^3, set d=tr Q and

  N(t,x,Q,b)=-Hscr-4 u.b-2 A:Q+2 b.b-Q:Q+d^2,
  Fsc(t,x,Q,b)=N(t,x,Q,b)/(2(rho+d)).                    (CJ.A1)

Here Q:Q and b.b are real polynomial contractions. Their complex analytic
extensions use the same bilinear polynomials, not Hermitian conjugation.
Absolute values and Frobenius norms occur only in estimates.

A1. Explicit margin and analyticity.

For rho!=0 and |tr Q|<=|rho|/2,

  |rho+tr Q|>=|rho|/2,
  |2(rho+tr Q)|>=|rho|,
  |1/(2(rho+tr Q))|<=1/|rho|.                          (CJ.A2)

Proof: the reverse triangle inequality gives
|rho+tr Q|>=|rho|-|tr Q|. The remaining assertions follow. QED.

The same modulus argument is valid for the complexified polynomial
variables. Since |tr Q|<=sqrt(3)|Q|_F, the Frobenius condition

  |Q|_F<=|rho|/(2 sqrt(3))                             (CJ.A3)

is sufficient. On the open set rho+tr Q!=0, Fsc is analytic in all
finite-dimensional arguments: its numerator is polynomial in Q,b with
analytic source coefficients, and its denominator is nonvanishing.

For an actual analytic solution with psi(t0,x)=psi_t(t0,x)=0, its Q=Hess_x
psi vanishes along the compact torus. Continuity and compactness therefore
permit restriction to a common time interval on which (CJ.A2) holds.
This is a local restriction of an existing solution, not an input-smallness
assumption and not an existence proof.

The explicit pointwise normal-map bound is

  |Fsc| <= (|Hscr|+4|u||b|+2|A|_F|Q|_F
               +2|b|^2+|Q|_F^2+|tr Q|^2)/|rho|.      (CJ.A4)

A2. A finite-dimensional Lipschitz bound.

Fix the source point (t,x). Suppose RQ<=|rho|/(2 sqrt(3)), and both
(Q,b) and (Q',b') belong to |Q|_F<=RQ, |b|<=Rb. Define

  BN=|Hscr|+4|u|Rb+2|A|_F RQ+2Rb^2+4RQ^2,
  Lb=4(|u|+Rb)/|rho|,
  LQ=(2|A|_F+8RQ)/|rho|+2 sqrt(3) BN/|rho|^2.

Then

  |Fsc(Q,b)-Fsc(Q',b')|
       <=LQ |Q-Q'|_F+Lb |b-b'|.                      (CJ.A5)

Proof. The domain is convex. The numerator derivatives satisfy

  |D_b N|<=4|u|+4Rb,
  |D_Q N|_F<=2|A|_F+2RQ+2 sqrt(3)|tr Q|
           <=2|A|_F+8RQ,
  |N|<=BN.

Differentiate N/[2(rho+tr Q)]. The denominator bounds (CJ.A2) give
|D_b Fsc|<=Lb and |D_Q Fsc|_F<=LQ. Integrating the derivative along the
segment between the two arguments proves (CJ.A5). QED.

These bounds quantify the algebraic normal map. They are not contraction
bounds for the full PDE in a fixed analytic or Sobolev norm.

SOURCE_SUPPORT = ML11--ML19 for finite calculus/polynomial division;
ML20--ML21 for common compact restrictions.
NEW_FORMAL_BODIES = CJ01--CJ03; parent contract NP03/NP05.

=======================================================================
VI.B. SCALAR CAUCHY JETS: FINITE RECURSION AND ANALYTIC UNIQUENESS
=======================================================================

Let P_n(x)=partial_t^n psi(t0,x), u_n=partial_t^n u(t0,x),
A_n=partial_t^n A(t0,x), and h_n=partial_t^n Hscr(t0,x).
Let C(n,j)=binomial(n,j), P_0=P_1=0. Differentiating the un-divided
Hamiltonian normal equation gives, for n>=0,

  2rho P_(n+2)
    = -h_n
      -2 sum_(j=1)^n C(n,j) (Delta P_j) P_(n-j+2)
      -4 sum_(j=0)^n C(n,j) u_j . grad P_(n-j+1)
      -2 sum_(j=0)^n C(n,j) A_j : Hess P_(n-j)
      +2 sum_(j=0)^n C(n,j)
                 grad P_(j+1) . grad P_(n-j+1)
      -  sum_(j=0)^n C(n,j)
                 Hess P_j : Hess P_(n-j)
      +  sum_(j=0)^n C(n,j)
                 (Delta P_j)(Delta P_(n-j)).           (CJ.B1)

Proof. Apply the n-th Leibniz rule to each product in equation (7.4).
The differentiated coefficient term is

  partial_t^n(2(rho+Delta psi)psi_tt)|t0
    =2rho P_(n+2)
      +2 sum_(j=0)^n C(n,j)(Delta P_j)P_(n-j+2).

The j=0 summand is zero because P_0=0. The remaining terms are exactly
(CJ.B1), with signs obtained by moving all terms other than 2rho P_(n+2)
to the right side. QED.

The right side depends only on P_0,...,P_(n+1) and source jets: for j>=1,
n-j+2<=n+1. Division by the nonzero constant 2rho therefore gives a
well-founded recursion. In particular,

  P_2=-h_0/(2rho),
  P_3=-(h_1+4u_0.grad P_2)/(2rho).                    (CJ.B2)

B1. Uniqueness of the scalar formal jet.

Two solutions with the same source germ and zero Cauchy data have the
same P_n as tangential function germs for every n. This follows by
induction from (CJ.B1).

B2. Uniqueness of existing analytic scalar germs.

At every base point x0, equality of all P_n as functions near x0 gives
partial_x^alpha P_n(x0)=partial_x^alpha P'_n(x0) for all alpha,n.
These are all mixed Taylor derivatives of psi and psi' at (t0,x0).
The convergent real-analytic Taylor expansions are therefore identical
on a common smaller neighborhood. Hence psi=psi' as germs along the
initial torus. Compact shrinking gives a common cylinder when needed.

No convergence of an arbitrarily constructed formal jet is inferred from
(CJ.B1). Actual local existence remains supplied by the analytic Cauchy
existence theorem in Subsection VI.E below. The recurrence proves the uniqueness
part independently of its convergence proof.

SOURCE_SUPPORT = ML07--ML12; ML20--ML23 for compact/germ packaging;
ML26--ML27 for analytic restrictions and composition; ML29 for the
power-series/derivative identity, with a new equality-from-jets adapter.
NEW_FORMAL_BODIES = CJ04, CJ14; parent contract NP05.

=======================================================================
VI.C. RADIAL METRIC AND INVERSE JETS
=======================================================================

Let y=(t,x) be the four tangential coordinates. For an analytic solution
of Ric[g]_ab=0 in the stated Gaussian convention, put

  H_n(y)=partial_r^n h(0,y),
  J_n(y)=partial_r^n(h^{-1})(0,y),
  kappa_n(y)=partial_r^n tr(h^{-1}K)(0,y),
  R_n(y)=partial_r^n Ric[h(r)](0,y),
  H_0=eta, H_1=2K, J_0=eta^{-1}=eta.

Matrix multiplication order is part of every formula below.

C1. Inverse-jet recursion.

For n>=1,

  J_n=-eta^{-1} sum_(j=1)^n C(n,j) H_j J_(n-j).        (CJ.C1)

Proof. Differentiate h h^{-1}=I n times. The j=0 term is eta J_n;
all other terms appear in (CJ.C1). Left multiplication by eta^{-1}
solves for J_n. QED.

In particular J_1=-2 eta^{-1}K eta^{-1}. No commutativity of matrix
multiplication is used.

C2. Curvature and radial-jet recursion.

  kappa_n=(1/2) sum_(j=0)^n C(n,j)
                        tr(J_j H_(n-j+1)).           (CJ.C2)

For n>=0,

  H_(n+2)
    =2R_n-sum_(j=0)^n C(n,j) kappa_j H_(n-j+1)
      +sum_(i+j+k=n) [n!/(i!j!k!)]
                      H_(i+1) J_j H_(k+1).           (CJ.C3)

Proof. Substitute K=h_r/2 into equation (9.3):

  h_rr=2Ric[h]-kappa h_r+h_r h^{-1}h_r.

Apply the n-th Leibniz rule, using the multinomial rule for the triple
product. Differentiating kappa=(1/2)tr(h^{-1}h_r) gives (CJ.C2). QED.

R_n is a finite rational differential expression in H_0,...,H_n and
their tangential derivatives through order two. Indeed, the coordinate
formulas are

  Gamma^a_bc=(1/2)(h^{-1})^{ad}
                       (partial_b h_dc+partial_c h_db-partial_d h_bc),
  Ric[h]_bc=partial_a Gamma^a_bc-partial_c Gamma^a_ba
            +Gamma^a_ad Gamma^d_bc-Gamma^a_cd Gamma^d_ba.

Taking n radial derivatives uses inverse jets J_0,...,J_n, each already
determined by (CJ.C1), and tangential derivatives of H_0,...,H_n only.
Thus (CJ.C3) determines H_(n+2) from H_0,...,H_(n+1). It is an explicit
finite recursion once the actual initial tensor K(y) is specified.

C3. First nontrivial radial jet.

Because h(0,y)=eta is constant in y, Ric[h(0)]=0. With
kappa=tr_eta K,

  H_2=-2kappa K+4K eta^{-1}K.                          (CJ.C4)

This is a consequence of the radial equations alone; no Hamiltonian
or Codazzi assumption is needed for (CJ.C4).

C4. Third radial jet under Codazzi.

Assume the flat Codazzi equation partial_c K^c_b=partial_b kappa.
Then

  H_3=2(Hess_y kappa-Box_eta K)
      +4kappa^2 K-12kappa K eta^{-1}K
      +8K eta^{-1}K eta^{-1}K.                        (CJ.C5)

Here Box_eta K is applied componentwise and Hess_y includes all four
tangential coordinates.

Proof. Since Gamma[h(0)]=0,

  R_1,ab=partial_c partial_a K^c_b
         +partial_c partial_b K^c_a
         -Box_eta K_ab-partial_a partial_b kappa
        =partial_a partial_b kappa-Box_eta K_ab.

Using J_1=-2 eta^{-1}K eta^{-1} and (CJ.C4), equation (CJ.C2) gives

  kappa_1=(1/2)tr(J_1 H_1+eta^{-1}H_2)=-kappa^2.

For n=1, equation (CJ.C3) becomes

  H_3=2R_1-kappa_1H_1-kappa H_2
          +H_2 eta^{-1}H_1+H_1J_1H_1+H_1eta^{-1}H_2.

Substitution and collection yield (CJ.C5). QED.

C5. Existing analytic radial germs are uniquely determined by K.

Suppose h and h' satisfy the same radial normal equation and data
H_0=eta,H_1=2K. Induction using (CJ.C1)--(CJ.C3) yields H_n=H'_n for
all n, as tangential function germs. Mixed tangential differentiation
then identifies every multivariable Taylor coefficient at every base
point. Analyticity gives h=h' as germs. This provides the radial
uniqueness needed for pressure-gauge independence and both inverse laws.

This finite-jet computation neither establishes convergence of a formal
radial series nor yields a uniform radius or a Sobolev-stability estimate.

SOURCE_SUPPORT = ML07--ML19 for differentiation/finite matrix algebra;
ML20--ML23 for compact/germ packaging; ML31--ML33 for the actual inverse
definition and both inverse products under the explicit IsUnit(det h)
condition; ML29 for the analytic-jet adapter.
NEW_FORMAL_BODIES = CJ05--CJ09, CJ14; parent contracts NP03, NP06, NP07,
NP08 and NP09.

=======================================================================
VI.D. BIANCHI PROPAGATION BY ZERO-JET INDUCTION
=======================================================================

D1. Generic homogeneous analytic propagation lemma.

Let W(r,y) be an already-existing jointly analytic R^N-valued field, and
suppose on a neighborhood of {0}xU that

  partial_r W=sum_a A^a(r,y)partial_a W+B(r,y)W,        (CJ.D1)
  W(0,y)=0.

The coefficient matrices A^a and B are analytic. Then W=0 as a germ
along {0}xU.

Proof. Set W_n=partial_r^n W|r=0, A^a_j=partial_r^j A^a|r=0, and
B_j=partial_r^j B|r=0. Differentiation of (CJ.D1) gives

  W_(n+1)=sum_(j=0)^n C(n,j)
             (sum_a A^a_j partial_a W_(n-j)+B_j W_(n-j)). (CJ.D2)

W_0=0 as a function on U. If W_0,...,W_n vanish as functions, their
tangential derivatives also vanish, so (CJ.D2) gives W_(n+1)=0.
Induction proves W_n=0 for every n. At each y0, every mixed derivative
partial_y^alpha W_n(y0) is zero. The convergent analytic Taylor series
at (0,y0) therefore vanishes. QED.

No solution of (CJ.D1) is constructed. The lemma is solely a uniqueness
argument for an existing analytic field.

D2. Application to the Einstein residual.

The actual analytic metric obtained from the radial normal equation
already determines analytic fields E=G_rr and M_a=G_ra. Its tangential
Ricci components vanish. The contracted Bianchi identity gives

  E_r=-D_a M^a-2kappa E,
  (M_a)_r=partial_a E-kappa M_a.

Expanding D_a M^a in coordinates makes this a system of the form
(CJ.D1) for W=(E,M_0,M_1,M_2,M_3), N=5. For example,

  D_a M^a=h^{ab}partial_a M_b-h^{ab}Gamma^c_ab M_c.

All coefficients are analytic because h is analytic and invertible.
The initial Hamiltonian and Codazzi equations give W(0,y)=0. Hence
D1 proves E=M=0. Since Ric_ab=0 and G_ab=-E h_ab, the entire Einstein
tensor vanishes, and trace_g G=-(3/2)R then gives Ric(g)=0.

Formal reduction:
  geometric Bianchi identity + finite Leibniz recursion + analytic jet
  extensionality replace any separate appeal to a subsidiary CK theorem.
  The signed geometric Bianchi identity itself remains an NP06 proof body.

SOURCE_SUPPORT = ML07--ML12, ML26--ML27, ML29;
no imported complete Bianchi endpoint is claimed.
NEW_FORMAL_BODIES = CJ10--CJ12, CJ14; parent contracts NP06/NP07.

=======================================================================
VI.E. ONE GENERIC SECOND-ORDER ANALYTIC EXISTENCE ENGINE
=======================================================================

Definition (reduced second jet).

For U(s,y) in R^N, y in R^d, let

  J^-_2 U=(partial_s^j partial_y^alpha U:
                j+|alpha|<=2, j<=1).

Its entries are U,U_s,U_y,U_yy,U_sy; U_ss is excluded. With symmetric
second tangential derivatives the number of scalar slots is

  N*(2+2d+d(d+1)/2).                                  (CJ.E1)

Analytic Cauchy existence endpoint CK2_EXISTS:

Given d,N>=1, an open finite-dimensional jet domain Omega, an analytic
map F:Omega -> R^N, and analytic Cauchy data a(y),b(y) near y0, assume
(0,y0,J^-_2[a,b](y0)) belongs to Omega. There exists a jointly analytic
local solution U of

  U_ss=F(s,y,J^-_2U),  U(0,y)=a(y), U_s(0,y)=b(y).      (CJ.E2)

The initial reduced jet is (a,b,D_y a,D_y^2 a,D_y b). The assertion is
about a neighborhood of (0,y0), not a globally prescribed slab.

This is the standard fully nonlinear second-order Cauchy--Kowalevski
normal form; R1, Section II.A, equations (31)--(33), explicitly includes
mixed second derivatives. Its actual-convergence assertion is the single
substantive analytic existence body to formalize for both constructions.

Uniqueness can be proved separately: the n-th normal derivative of the
right side involves U_0,...,U_(n+1), since every normal index in J^-_2 is
at most one. The chain rule therefore recursively determines U_(n+2).
Equal data imply equal normal jets as tangential functions; analytic jet
extensionality gives equal germs. This generalizes Subsections VI.B and VI.C.

E1. Scalar specialization.

  Normal variable: s=t-t0.
  Tangential variables: y=x in R^3.
  Number of unknowns: N=1.
  Reduced-jet slots: 14.
  Active highest derivative slots: Q=D_x^2 psi (6), b=D_x psi_t (3).
  Normal map: Fsc from (CJ.A1).
  Initial data: a=b=0.
  Noncharacteristic condition: rho!=0; initially 2(rho+Delta psi)=2rho.

Analytic source coefficients and A1 verify the finite-dimensional
analyticity premise. CK2_EXISTS produces the scalar germ. Subsection VI.B proves
its uniqueness. Compact gluing gives the periodic time cylinder.

E2. Radial specialization.

  Normal variable: s=r.
  Tangential variables: y=(t,x) in R^4.
  Number of unknowns: N=10, the independent entries of a symmetric 4x4 h.
  Reduced-jet slots: 200.
  Active slots: h (10), h_r (10), D_y h (40), D_y^2 h (100).
  Unused mixed slots: D_y h_r (40).
  Normal map: 2Ric[h]-kappa h_r+h_r h^{-1}h_r.
  Initial data: a=eta, b=2K.
  Noncharacteristic condition: already solved for h_rr.
  Analytic inverse condition: det eta=-1!=0.

Represent h by its ten independent entries. The inverse is the rational
matrix map adj(h)/det h on det h!=0. The Christoffel and Ricci expressions
are rational analytic functions of precisely the listed finite jet slots.
Their restriction to symmetric h gives symmetric Ricci, so the ten
independent equations impose all tangential Ricci equations. This finite
symmetry identity remains part of NP06; it is not an assumed new axiom.

CK2_EXISTS therefore produces h. Subsection VI.C proves uniqueness, Subsection VI.F gives
the common invertible Lorentzian collar, and Subsection VI.D propagates constraints.

CK2_EXISTS is a planned mathematical interface, not the identifier of an
existing inspected Lean declaration. Declaring it as a Lean axiom or
retaining it as an unproved premise would produce a conditional formal
chain only. Section 35 constructively derives the generic mathematical
theorem; no matching Lean CK body has been externally compiled/audited.
Section 40 supplies finite-jet certificates and quantified remainders;
Section 41 supplies the exact compact product domains and germ descent.

The normal-order condition is essential. This engine constructs the scalar
completion and radial metric from an already-given analytic NSE germ. It
does not construct a time-analytic NSE germ from arbitrary analytic initial
velocity: NSE is first order in time and second order in space, and hence
does not satisfy the corresponding first-order total-jet CK hypothesis.
The realized-trace restriction of Section 19 therefore remains unchanged.

PRIMARY_SOURCE = https://arxiv.org/pdf/gr-qc/0109076
SOURCE_LOCATION = R1 Section II.A, equations (31)--(33).
SOURCE_SUPPORT = ML07--ML21 for normal-map calculus and compactness.
NEW_FORMAL_BODIES = CJ03, CJ05--CJ07, CJ13--CJ16;
PARENT_CONTRACTS = NP05/NP06/NP07.

=======================================================================
VI.F. COMPACT GLUING AND AN EXPLICIT LORENTZIAN COLLAR MARGIN
=======================================================================

F1. Uniform gluing from local existence and germ uniqueness.

Let C be a compact initial slice. Choose finitely many relatively compact
coordinate sets U_i whose closures lie in larger sets U'_i carrying local
analytic solutions on (-epsilon_i,epsilon_i)xU'_i, with identical prescribed
initial data on overlaps. For each pair i,j, the compact set
cl(U_i) intersect cl(U_j) lies inside U'_i intersect U'_j. Germ uniqueness
at every point of that compact intersection gives an open equality
neighborhood; a finite subcover gives a positive common normal radius
for equality throughout U_i intersect U_j. Taking the minimum of these
finitely many radii and the epsilon_i gives one positive radius. The
local solutions then glue to one analytic solution on the smaller collar.

For the scalar problem C=T^3. For the radial problem first choose a closed
time interval strictly inside the scalar existence interval and take
C=J_bar x T^3; after gluing restrict to an open interval around t0 inside
J_bar. Periodic chart transitions preserve the equations and initial data.

The minimum is positive because the cover and overlap cover are finite.
It depends on the actual analytic data; no data-independent lower bound
is asserted.

F2. Quantified nondegeneracy and signature persistence.

For a real symmetric 4x4 h and 0<=theta<1, suppose

  ||h-eta||_op<=theta.                                (CJ.F1)

Then

  ||h^{-1}||_op<=1/(1-theta),
  |det h|>=(1-theta)^4,
  inertia(h)=(one negative, three positive).           (CJ.F2)

Proof. Write E=h-eta. Since eta is a Euclidean isometry,

  |hz|>=|eta z|-|Ez|>=(1-theta)|z|.

Thus h is invertible and its inverse operator norm is at most 1/(1-theta).
Every singular value is at least 1-theta, so their product gives the
displayed determinant bound.
Alternatively the same determinant bound follows from the block argument
below. Write h in time/spatial blocks as [a,b^T;b,B]. Condition (CJ.F1)
gives a<=-(1-theta) and B>=(1-theta)I_3. Its Schur complement

  a-b^T B^{-1}b<=-(1-theta)

is negative. The elementary block congruence diagonalizes h to this
negative scalar and the positive definite matrix B. Hence the inertia is
(1,3). The determinant is det B times the Schur complement in absolute
value, giving the stated (1-theta)^4 lower bound. QED.

In particular theta=1/2 gives ||h^{-1}||_op<=2 and |det h|>=1/16.
At r=0, h=eta on the compact initial slice. Continuity gives (CJ.F1) with
theta=1/2 on a sufficiently small common collar. Hence g=dr^2+h is
Lorentzian with one negative direction. The nondegeneracy proof can also
be obtained from h=eta(I+eta E) and the Neumann series since
||eta E||_op<=theta<1.

The Frobenius condition |h-eta|_F<=theta is a sufficient stronger condition,
since ||E||_op<=|E|_F. No identification of Frobenius and operator norms
is intended.

SOURCE_SUPPORT = ML17--ML21, ML31--ML33 (finite matrix/compactness support
only; the norm and signature estimates remain new finite-dimensional proofs).
NEW_FORMAL_BODIES = CJ15--CJ16; parent contract NP07.

  V13_REFINEMENT: CK01-CK03 and CK05-CK12 give the complete constructive majorant proof in Section 35.
  Input: the exact open jet-domain and analytic-data hypotheses in Subsection VI.E.
  Output: actual jointly analytic local solution of (CJ.E2).

  Logical warning: formal coefficient recursion alone does not prove CJ13.

CJ14 ANALYTIC_EQUALITY_FROM_MIXED_JETS

  Output: equality on a common neighborhood, hence equality of germs.
  Dependencies: analytic power-series representation and coefficient identity;

  finite-dimensional real codomains discharge that class. Its permutation sum

=======================================================================
29. ROUND VII: ORTHOGONAL STRESS AND SHARPER DEFECT STABILITY
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP29
  V15_REFINEMENT = VF02-VF07; full stress decomposition determines actual vacuum fibres and minima.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME21-ME22.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  V14_REFINEMENT = HD01-HD10; complete the former scalar/transverse kernel and retain G4.
  V14_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME20.
  V14_LOOKUP = Sections 39-43; exact source hypotheses remain mandatory.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML01-ML06, ML17-ML19; ME01-ME06, ME12-ME14.
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = STF stress symbol, orthogonal splitting, negative-Sobolev duality, pressure correction, and refined NP13 energy estimate.
  LOOKUP = Sections 23-24 and 31 for source signatures; Section 30 for dependencies.
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; new application not compiled.

VII.1. Spaces and definitions.

Let F be a real, symmetric, trace-free L^2 spatial tensor on the unit torus.
No mean-zero hypothesis on F is imposed. Fourier coefficients are complex
symmetric matrices, with Frobenius norm and conjugate symmetry. Define

  f:=P div F,
  ||f||_{dot H^{-1}}^2:=sum_{k!=0} |f_hat(k)|^2/(4*pi^2*|k|^2),
  S(f):=Z(Delta0^{-1}f),
  Pi_st F:=S(P div F).                                  (VII.1)

The distribution f is mean-zero and divergence-free. The inverse Laplacian
is componentwise. For general f in dot H^{-1}_{sigma,0}, Delta0^{-1}f is a
mean-zero H^1 vector field, so S(f) is an L^2 tensor. The norm is a genuine
norm on the mean-zero space; no zero-frequency denominator is used.

VII.2. Exact Fourier symbol and orthogonal projection.

For k!=0 set e=k/|k|, P_e=I-e tensor e, A=F_hat(k), b=P_e A e. Then

  f_hat(k)=2*pi*i*|k|*b,
  (Pi_st F)_hat(k)=e tensor b+b tensor e.               (VII.2)

At k=0, (Pi_st F)_hat(0)=0. Since e.b=0, the displayed matrix is symmetric
and trace-free. Furthermore

  P_e (e tensor b+b tensor e)e=b.

Thus Pi_st is idempotent. With the Hermitian Frobenius pairing,

  <A, e tensor c+c tensor e>_F=2<P_e A e,c>
       whenever A^T=A and e.c=0.

Consequently the symbol is self-adjoint, and its extension to the real
L^2 tensor space is an orthogonal projection. In particular,

  F=Pi_st F+H,
  H:=(I-Pi_st)F,
  P div H=0,
  <Pi_st F,H>_{L^2}=0,
  ||F||_2^2=||Pi_st F||_2^2+||H||_2^2.                (VII.3)

Proof of bounded extension.
The finite-dimensional symbol is an orthogonal projector at every nonzero
frequency and zero at frequency zero. Componentwise Parseval gives an L^2
contraction. Partial Fourier sums converge in L^2; distributional divergence
and the H^{-1} multiplier identities pass to the limit. Conjugate symmetry
is preserved because the symbol is real and even in k. QED.

VII.3. Exact negative-Sobolev norm and minimum-norm stress.

Since |e tensor b+b tensor e|_F^2=2|b|^2, Parseval gives

  ||Pi_st F||_2^2=2||P div F||_{dot H^{-1}}^2.

Therefore

  ||P div F||_{dot H^{-1}}^2 <=(1/2)||F||_2^2.         (VII.4)

The constant 1/2 is sharp. For any nonzero smooth, mean-zero, solenoidal a,
take F=Z(a). Then div F=Delta a and Pi_st F=F. For example
  a(x)=e_2 sin(2*pi*x_1)
gives equality in (VII.4).

For any real f in dot H^{-1}_{sigma,0}, direct Fourier calculation gives

  div S(f)=f,
  tr S(f)=0,
  ||S(f)||_2^2=2||f||_{dot H^{-1}}^2.                  (VII.5)

Every real symmetric trace-free L^2 tensor A satisfying P div A=f has
the unique decomposition A=S(f)+H with P div H=0 and orthogonal terms.
Hence

  inf_{A=A^T, tr A=0, P div A=f} ||A||_2^2
    =2||f||_{dot H^{-1}}^2,

and S(f) is the unique minimizer. This is an explicit quadratic minimization
identity; no abstract existence theorem for minimizers is needed.

At each nonzero Fourier mode the projector has complex rank two within the
five-dimensional complex symmetric trace-free matrix space. Its kernel has
dimension three. In a real orthonormal basis with first vector e, it retains
exactly the (1,2),(2,1),(1,3),(3,1) off-diagonal entries. The zero mode is
entirely in the kernel. These are coefficient-space dimensions; reality
couples the k and -k coefficients in real physical fields.

VII.4. Exact relation to the two geometric admission conditions.

For a smooth normalized vacuum boundary, use the actual residual F=F_nu[K].
Set

  phi:=Delta0^{-1}div div F,
  p_corr:=p_*-phi.

The Helmholtz identity gives

  R_nu(v,p_corr)=P div F=f.                            (VII.6)

Consequently

  exists p: NSE_nu(v,p)
    <=> P div F=0
    <=> Pi_st F=0,

whereas canonical admission is

  F=0 <=> Pi_st F=0 and (I-Pi_st)F=0.                 (VII.7)

The kernel component is invisible to the projected velocity equation. Its
divergence is a pressure gradient, and its mean is also unconstrained by
P div F=0. This identifies the precise linear distinction between the
larger projection-admissible sector and the canonical sector. It does not
assert that every abstract kernel tensor is realized by vacuum boundary data.

VII.5. Sharper relative-energy forcing term.

In Section 22 let F_delta=F-F_bar and f_delta=P div F_delta. For
z=v-V, divergence-free, integration by parts and self-adjointness of P give

  -integral F_delta:grad z
    =<f_delta,Q0 z>_{dot H^{-1},dot H^1}
    =-integral Pi_st(F_delta):grad z.                 (VII.8)

Here Q0 z=z-<z> belongs to the mean-zero homogeneous H^1 space.
The physical pairing is unchanged because f_delta has zero mean. By Fourier Cauchy--Schwarz,

  |<f_delta,Q0 z>|<=||f_delta||_{dot H^{-1}}||grad z||_2.

Define

  d(t):=||P div(F-F_bar)||_{dot H^{-1}}
       =||Pi_st(F-F_bar)||_2/sqrt(2).

Then the exact relative-energy identity implies

  y'(t)+nu||grad z||_2^2 <=2L(t)y(t)+d(t)^2/nu.        (VII.9)

Proof.
Use d||grad z||<=(nu/2)||grad z||^2+d^2/(2nu) in
(1/2)y'+nu||grad z||^2<=L y+d||grad z||, then multiply by two. QED.

For s<=t on the common existing interval,

  y(t)<=exp(2 integral_s^t L)y(s)
       +(1/nu)integral_s^t exp(2 integral_tau^t L)d(tau)^2 d tau
    <=exp(2 integral_s^t L)y(s)
       +(1/(2nu))integral_s^t exp(2 integral_tau^t L)
                    ||F(tau)-F_bar(tau)||_2^2 d tau. (VII.10)

If the velocity means agree, both exponential kernels acquire the term
-4*pi^2*nu*(t-tau). If f_delta=0, no Young-inequality loss is required;
the full rate -8*pi^2*nu*(t-s) is available when the means agree, even when
the two nonzero residual tensors differ by a member of ker(P div).

The constant 1/2 in the stress-to-force bound is sharp. No optimality claim
for the full nonlinear Gronwall estimate or for the set of Einstein-realized
residual tensors is made.

VII.6. Atomic proof obligations and source roles.

  ST01: k!=0 -> |k|>0; define e,P_e and verify orthogonality.
        Support ML17-ML19; ME01-ME03; finite Euclidean algebra required.
  ST02: derive the symbol of Z Delta0^-1 P div and its zero mode.
        Support ML01,ML02,ML06; ME01-ME05; multiplier adapter required.
  ST03: prove STF preservation, symbol idempotence and self-adjointness.
        Support ML17-ML19; finite Hermitian tensor algebra required.
  ST04: lift symbol identities by componentwise Parseval to real L^2 fields.
        Support ML03,ML05; ME06 for continuous representatives where used.
  ST05: define mean-zero dot H^{-1}; prove (VII.4)-(VII.5) and sharpness.
        Support ML05; ME04-ME05; the exact negative norm remains a new type.
  ST06: prove the minimum-norm characterization by orthogonal splitting.
        Depends ST03-ST05; no imported minimization theorem is asserted.
  ST07: prove the pressure correction and admission equivalences.
        Support ME01-ME02,ME07-ME10,ME16; depends NP02,NP04 and ST02-ST05.
  ST08: prove H^{-1}/H^1 dual pairing and replace NP13's forcing estimate.
        Support ML05; ME12-ME14 as scoped in their existing register.
        Depends NP15; no weak/strong endpoint is substituted for a new
        two-smooth-flow forced identity.

=======================================================================
30. ATOMIC CHECKPOINT INDEX AND DETERMINISTIC COMPOSITION
=======================================================================

30.1. Exact identifiers and evidence classes.

  ML01-ML33: source-verified Mathlib declaration identifiers.
  ME01-ME17: source-verified Methlib declaration groups.
  NP01-NP16: parent formal proof contracts; Section 25.
  AF01-AF11: analytic Fourier atomic contracts; Section 27.
  CJ01-CJ16: normal-map/jet/geometric atomic contracts; Section 28.
  ST01-ST08: orthogonal stress/energy atomic contracts; Section 29.

An atomic identifier specifies a new mathematical interface and proof body.
It is not an imported declaration, an axiom, a certificate, a prepared Lean
file, or external compiler evidence. Its hypotheses and conclusion are
recorded at its defining subsection. The complete mathematical arguments
are included in those subsections.

30.2. Parent-to-atomic mapping.

PARENT | ATOMIC EDGES | RESULT USED BY THE ROOT OR STRENGTHENING
NP01 | AF01,AF04 | actual analytic periodic representatives and descent
NP02 | AF01-AF09 | common domains, convergence, real analytic Poisson/Leray
NP03 | CJ01-CJ03,CJ05,CJ08-CJ09 | finite tensor normal forms and boundary jets
NP04 | AF10,ST07 | actual residual and exact forced-pressure sign
NP05 | CJ01-CJ04,CJ13-CJ15 | scalar actual existence and independent uniqueness
NP06 | CJ05-CJ06,CJ10 | actual inverse, Ricci and signed Bianchi semantics
NP07 | CJ05-CJ16 | radial existence, unique jets, propagated vacuum and collar
NP08 | CJ04,CJ08,CJ14-CJ15 | pressure-gauge representative independence
NP09 | CJ04,CJ08,CJ14-CJ15 plus original inverse algebra | both exact inverse laws
NP10 | AF09,AF11,CJ14 | realized-trace Taylor recurrence and injectivity
NP11 | ST01-ST07 supplement original Curl symbol body | two admission levels
NP12 | original finite cancellations plus AF07-AF10 where analytic | viscosity
NP13 | ST05-ST08 and NP15 | sharper tensor-forced relative energy
NP14 | CJ05-CJ09,CJ16 plus explicit examples | geometric nonemptiness/strictness
NP15 | original local-integral body; ST08 for mean-zero duality | local-time proof
NP16 | AF09-AF10, ML28, ME10, ME16 | temporal synthesis and forced physical RHS

This mapping does not delete any original NP premise. In particular the
original signed geometric identities, nonlinear inverse-map algebra,
periodic integrations and STF-Curl identity retain their proof obligations.

30.3. Deterministic analytic branch.

  AF01 -> AF03 -> AF04.
  AF02 + AF04 -> AF05 and AF06.
  AF02 + AF04 + finite multiplier symbols -> AF07 and AF08.
  AF03 + AF04 + AF06 -> AF09.
  AF05 + AF07 + AF08 -> AF10.
  AF05 + AF06 + AF08 -> AF11.

The bounded linear multiplier interfaces may use ML26 and ML27 only after
AF02 and the exact symbol/norm proofs have produced the actual maps.
For a temporal Fourier implementation:
  AF09 -> ML28 application -> actual derivative synthesis;
  ME10 identifies the spatial unforced RHS;
  AF10 + ME16 identify the additional longitudinal pressure;
  the exact projected forcing coefficient is retained where nonzero.

30.4. Deterministic geometric branch.

  analytic u,p -> analytic grad_x u,A,H(S_rho).
  CJ01 + CJ02 + these analytic source coefficients -> CJ03.
  CJ05 + actual symmetric-metric Ricci definitions / NP06 geometry
    -> CJ06 -> CJ07.
  CJ03 + CJ13(d=3,N=1) -> scalar analytic existence.
  actual NSE source conservation + scalar completion
    + original trace-reversal/Hamiltonian identities
    -> analytic completed K satisfying Hamiltonian and Codazzi constraints.
  CJ07 + this analytic K + CJ13(d=4,N=10) -> radial analytic existence.
  CJ04 + CJ14 -> scalar germ uniqueness.
  CJ08 + CJ14 -> radial germ uniqueness.
  actual metric + signed NP06 identities -> CJ10.
  CJ10 + Hamiltonian/Codazzi zero initial residual + CJ11 + CJ14
    -> vanishing existing Bianchi residual.
  CJ12 -> full vacuum equation.
  CJ15 + CJ16 -> one periodic marked Lorentzian collar.

The generic CK existence theorem CJ13 is shared between its two instances.
Zero-jet propagation needs no additional PDE existence engine. The already
constructed residual is the object whose vanishing is proved.

30.5. Deterministic final equivalence and optional diagnostics.

  analytic branch + scalar existence + vacuum radial existence
    -> actual lift on representatives;
  actual Codazzi + analytic Poisson/Leray + canonical admission
    -> actual NSE reconstruction;
  pressure-gauge tensor identity + scalar/radial germ uniqueness
    -> representative-independent lift;
  both explicit inverse computations + germ uniqueness
    -> NP09 -> ML24/ML25 -> exact equivalence.

  ST01 -> ST02 -> ST03 -> ST04 -> ST05 -> ST06;
  ST02-ST05 + NP02/NP04 -> ST07;
  ST05 + NP13/NP15 -> ST08 and the sharper relative-energy estimate.

The stress diagnostic branch is a proved strengthening. It is not an extra
premise of the original equivalence. The projected sector remains larger
than the canonical sector.

30.6. External cached validation contract.

  Toolchain: Lean 4.33.0.
  Mathlib pin: db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d.
  Methlib pin: 09dfe94a1ef150e21d09df3013fbe09002ef139c.
  Preserve exact OS, architecture, compiler, dependency and source cache keys.
  Restore compatible dependency/build caches before external elaboration.
  Run the cache/build/audit sequence recorded in Sections 24 and 25 externally.
  Use exact endpoint signatures and independent opaque edge bodies.
  Native asynchronous elaboration may alter scheduling only.
  Compose the final chain deterministically from kernel-checked bodies.
  Check the exact root type and its theorem-level axiom closure.

No local Lean, Lake, compiler, build, cache retrieval or CI run is performed
by this B-tier revision. A prepared signature is not a proved edge. A source
declaration is not evidence that the new application compiles.

30.7. Explicit remaining formal work.

V1.3 REFINEMENT: Sections 34-35 now provide complete mathematical coordinate
and constructive-convergence proofs for NP06 and CJ13. Their exact Lean
bodies remain uncompiled. Section 37 replaces the coarse implementation
dependency nodes by GE01-GE12 and CK01-CK12 without changing their premises.

CJ13 requires an actual convergent analytic existence proof or a matching
proved external library theorem. The coefficient recursions do not discharge
this requirement. NP06 requires actual signed differential geometry, including
contracted Bianchi. AF01-AF11 require the specified spaces and analytic-series
adapters. The germ and physical-field type conversions remain explicit.

The mathematical arguments are B-tier. No unresolved formal body is silently
replaced by a new axiom. A later theorem retaining any such statement as a
named hypothesis must preserve and classify that hypothesis, and cannot be
reported as unconditional closure while it remains unresolved.
