theorem torusSobolev_multiplier_weighted_norm_le (s t : ℕ)
    (b : Frequency → ℂ) (B : ℝ)
    (hb : ∀ k, torusSobolevWeight k ^ s * ‖b k‖ ≤ B * torusSobolevWeight k ^ t)
    (c : FourierCoeff) (ki : FourierIndex) :
    ‖sobolevWeightedCoeff s (fun k i => b k * c k i) ki‖ ≤
      B * ‖sobolevWeightedCoeff t c ki‖ := by

theorem torusSobolevMultiplierLinear_norm_le (s t : ℕ) (b : Frequency → ℂ)
    (B : ℝ) (hB : 0 ≤ B)
    (hb : ∀ k, torusSobolevWeight k ^ s * ‖b k‖ ≤ B * torusSobolevWeight k ^ t)
    (c : TorusSobolev t) :
    ‖torusSobolevMultiplierLinear s t b B hB hb c‖ ≤ B * ‖c‖ := by
SOURCE_STATEMENTS_END
PLACEMENT = NP02 agreement with existing Sobolev multiplier implementations; optional NP16 coefficient realization via a fixed Sobolev target.
REMAINING_OBLIGATION = These are polynomial-weighted l2 Sobolev inequalities. Their s,t variables are natural numbers and the symbol bound hb is a hypothesis. They do not construct the exponential-weight l1 Wiener space, prove a uniform analytic strip, or supply the desired sharp operator constants without a new symbol estimate.
STATUS = SOURCE_VERIFIED_SUPPORT; TARGET_APPLICATION_UNCOMPILED

C. CRITICAL SEMANTIC CHECKS
==========================

1. Matrix.inv is totalized. IsUnit determinant is necessary at every inverse
   identity; no singular metric is admitted by the inverse notation.
2. The analytic map norm need only induce the fixed finite-dimensional topology;
   physical Frobenius/Euclidean norms in tensor estimates still require their
   explicit definitions. Do not equate them to a default Matrix norm.
3. The new W_sigma spaces use exponential weights and l1 summability. Existing
   TorusSobolev operators use natural-number polynomial weights and l2. Norm
   assertions cannot cross that interface without a proved embedding/identity.
4. Analytic uniqueness from jets uses an already convergent analytic series.
   Formal recursive coefficients do not discharge CK existence or convergence.
5. ML28 requires convergence of differentiated approximants. The order of
   derivative, limit, real descent, and physical Fourier identification remains
   an explicit checked chain.
6. The original exact root is retained. Auxiliary choices of analytic radius,
   complex chart, and local collar are existential witnesses derived in the
   proof, not extra restrictions on the analytic germ class.
7. Source-verification of ML26-ML33 / ME16-ME17 is not theorem-level compilation
   or axiom-audit evidence for any new GR/NSE theorem.

======================
The eight additional Mathlib declarations and two Methlib endpoint groups
were read at the exact pinned revisions. The source-file Git blob hashes
were checked against their byte-exact snapshots. Complete signatures,
contexts, imports, source URLs and application limitations are recorded above.
No target theorem was compiled or axiom-audited by this source inspection.

VIII.1. Orthogonal decomposition of the actual equation residual.

Let u(t,x) be a smooth periodic divergence-free vector field on an existing
time interval; let F(t,x) be a smooth periodic tensor and p(t,x) a smooth
periodic scalar. No NSE equation is assumed in this subsection. Set

  pi(u,F):=Delta0^-1 divdiv(F-u tensor u),
  E_nu(u,F):=partial_t u-nu Delta u+P div(u tensor u-F).

The double divergence has zero mean, so pi is defined and <pi>=0.
For analytic u,F, Section 27 proves joint analyticity of pi on a common
smaller domain. The exact identity is

  R_nu(u,p)-div F
    =E_nu(u,F)+grad(p-pi(u,F)).                       (CG.1)

Proof.
Put A=u tensor u-F. Since div u=0, (u.grad)u=div(u tensor u).
The Helmholtz identity gives
  div A=P div A+grad Delta0^-1 divdiv A
       =P div A-grad pi(u,F).
The vector partial_t u-nu Delta u is divergence free, since the displayed
derivatives commute with divergence. Substitution proves (CG.1), and
div E_nu(u,F)=0. QED.

For each time, periodic integration by parts gives the exact Pythagorean
identity

  ||R_nu(u,p)-div F||_2^2
    =||E_nu(u,F)||_2^2+||grad(p-pi(u,F))||_2^2.       (CG.2)

Consequently

  min_{p periodic, <p>=0} ||R_nu(u,p)-div F||_2^2
    =||E_nu(u,F)||_2^2,                              (CG.3)

with unique minimizer p=pi(u,F), in any smooth or analytic class containing
that pressure. Without the zero-mean normalization, the minimizers are
exactly p=pi(u,F)+a(t).

Proof of uniqueness.
Equality in (CG.2) is equivalent to grad(p-pi)=0. A periodic scalar whose
gradient is zero has only its zero Fourier coefficient: for each k!=0,
some component k_i!=0, and (2*pi*i*k_i)(p-pi)_hat(k)=0 forces that
coefficient to vanish. Fourier reconstruction gives p-pi=<p-pi>=<p>.
The zero-mean minimizer is therefore unique. QED.

Poincare supplies the quantitative pressure error estimate

  ||p-<p>-pi(u,F)||_2
    <=(1/(2*pi))||grad(p-pi(u,F))||_2
    <=(1/(2*pi))||R_nu(u,p)-div F||_2.               (CG.4)

This is an orthogonal residual identity for arbitrary smooth solenoidal
trial fields. It does not assume existence of an exact solution nearby.

VIII.2. Velocity-only formulation with an explicit inverse pressure map.

Write pi(u)=pi(u,0)=-Delta0^-1 divdiv(u tensor u). For every jointly
analytic periodic solenoidal u,

  E_nu(u,0)=0
    <=> NSE_nu(u,pi(u))
    <=> exists analytic periodic p: NSE_nu(u,p).       (CG.5)

If the last condition holds, every such pressure is

  p=pi(u)+a(t),  a(t)=<p>(t).                         (CG.6)

Proof.
Set p=pi in (CG.1) and F=0 for the first equivalence. For a general exact
pair, apply P to (CG.1) to obtain E_nu=0, then grad(p-pi)=0. The previous
Fourier argument proves (CG.6). The mean is analytic because it is the
zero Fourier coefficient of the analytic family, as proved in AF03/AF09.
Conversely adding a time-only analytic function does not change grad p.
QED.

Let V^omega_(nu,t0) be the set of jointly analytic periodic velocity germs
along {t0}xT^3 satisfying div u=0 and E_nu(u,0)=0. Equality is restriction
to a common smaller time cylinder, exactly as for the original velocity
representatives. Define

  Forget:N^omega_(nu,t0)->V^omega_(nu,t0), [u,p]|->[u],
  Normalize:V^omega_(nu,t0)->N^omega_(nu,t0),
            [u]|->[u,pi(u)].

The operators defining pi commute with restriction by their coefficient
formulas. Equation (CG.6) therefore proves that both maps are well-defined
and that

  Forget o Normalize=id,
  Normalize o Forget=id.                             (CG.7)

Hence the original pressure-gauge solution space has the exact normal form

  N^omega_(nu,t0) ~= V^omega_(nu,t0).                 (CG.8)

The velocity mean is arbitrary and remains retained by P_hat(0)=I.
No Sobolev or forward-time global solution predicate replaces the analytic
germ predicate in (CG.8).

The normalization on representatives is the idempotent map

  (u,p)|->(u,p-<p>).

On exact NSE pairs its image is precisely (u,pi(u)). This gives a canonical
section of the pressure-gauge quotient, with no choice of pressure witness.

VIII.3. Explicit analytic gauge homotopy.

Let a be an analytic scalar germ at t0. Define, on a complex disc where its
holomorphic extension is defined,

  I2[a](t0+z):=z^2 integral_0^1 (1-s)a(t0+s*z) ds.     (CG.9)

For real t this equals the oriented integral

  I2[a](t)=integral_(t0)^t (t-s)a(s) ds.

The formula is valid on either side of t0. It satisfies

  (I2[a])''=a,
  I2[a](t0)=0,
  (I2[a])'(t0)=0.                                    (CG.10)

Proof of analyticity and the derivative identities.
Write a(t0+z)=sum_(n>=0) a_n z^n on its convergent disc. On each smaller
closed disc the series is absolutely and uniformly convergent, so its
substitution into (CG.9) can be integrated term by term. Since

  integral_0^1 (1-s)s^n ds=1/((n+1)(n+2)),

one obtains the convergent power series

  I2[a](t0+z)=sum_(n>=0) a_n z^(n+2)/((n+1)(n+2)).   (CG.11)

Twice differentiated normal convergence on smaller discs gives (CG.10).
Real coefficients or the usual conjugation symmetry give a real analytic
function on the real interval. The oriented integral formula follows by
the substitution s=t0+theta*(t-t0), including the orientation for t<t0.
QED.

The operator I2 is linear, commutes with germ restriction, and is the unique
twice differentiable solution of (CG.10) with the prescribed zero data.
For uniqueness, a difference b has b''=0, hence b' is constant and b is
affine; the two initial values force b=0. In the analytic class the same
claim follows immediately from the zero Taylor coefficients.

If |a(z)|<=M on the time disc of radius R, then

  |I2[a](t0+z)|<=|z|^2 M/2,
  |(I2[a])'(t0+z)|<=|z| M,
  ||I2[a]||_(infinity,R)<=R^2 M/2.                   (CG.12)

The first bound is immediate from (CG.9). The derivative equals
z integral_0^1 a(t0+s*z) ds, by (CG.11), giving the second. The constant
1/2 in the last bound is sharp for a=1. Boundedness on a disc is obtained
for any analytic germ by shrinking to a closed disc within its domain.

VIII.4. Exact gauge action on completed conserved tensors.

For a fixed rho, define the completion tensor

  B[u,p,psi]=S_rho[u,p]+C[psi].

For each time-only analytic a, set

  G_a(u,p,psi)=(u,p+a,psi-I2[a]).                     (CG.13)

Then

  B[G_a(u,p,psi)]=B[u,p,psi],
  G_a o G_b=G_(a+b), G_0=id, (G_a)^-1=G_(-a).        (CG.14)

Proof.
The pressure change contributes a delta_ij in the spatial block of S and
zero in all other blocks. For the time-only function f=-I2[a],

  C[f]^{00}=Delta_x f=0,
  C[f]^{0i}=-partial_i f_t=0,
  C[f]^{ij}=f_tt delta_ij=-a delta_ij.

The changes cancel exactly. The group laws follow from linearity of I2.
Its zero Cauchy data preserve the scalar normalization. QED.

Trace reversal then gives unchanged K. For the actual constructed marked
metric germs, both metrics solve the radial normal equation with h|0=eta
and h_r|0=2K. Under precisely these hypotheses, CJ08/CJ14 gives equality
of the metric germs. Equality of B or K alone is not asserted to determine
arbitrary marked metrics. This proves the metric gauge action using the
exact identities and the radial uniqueness edge; no change of coordinates
or bulk diffeomorphism quotient occurs.

VIII.5. Complete classification of normalized tensor-completion fibres.

Consider any two jointly analytic periodic triples (u,p,psi) and
(u',p',psi') with the same fixed rho and the zero scalar Cauchy data at t0.
No NSE equation, Hamiltonian equation, or divergence-free hypothesis is
needed for the following algebraic statement:

  B[u',p',psi']=B[u,p,psi]
    <=> exists a(t) analytic:
          u'=u, p'=p+a(t), psi'=psi-I2[a].            (CG.15)

Proof.
The reverse implication is (CG.14). For the forward implication, put
dpsi=psi'-psi. The 00 component is

  0=B'^{00}-B^{00}=Delta_x dpsi.

Periodic harmonic uniqueness implies dpsi=f(t): equivalently every
nonzero spatial Fourier coefficient is killed by the nonzero Laplace
symbol. The mean f is analytic. The 0i components now give

  0=u'_i-u_i-partial_i f_t=u'_i-u_i.

Thus u'=u, including its entire spatial mean. The spatial components
reduce to

  0=(p'-p+f'')delta_ij.

Hence p'-p=a(t)=-f''(t). The normalization gives f(t0)=f'(t0)=0, so
(CG.10) gives f=-I2[a]. QED.

The fixed-rho qualification can also be checked from the tensor: on each
time slice <B^{00}>=rho, since <Delta psi>=0. Equality of two such completed
tensors forces equality of rho before (CG.15) is applied.

The action (CG.13) is free: if G_a fixes a triple, its pressure component
gives a=0. Thus (CG.15) identifies the completion-tensor fibres exactly
with the analytic time-pressure gauge orbits. It introduces no extra
completion freedom and uses no CK existence premise.

VIII.6. Exact velocity-only implementation of the original equivalence.

Define

  L_v([u]):=L([u,pi(u)]),
  R_v([g]):=[P K_(0 bullet)].

Using (CG.7) and the original inverse identities gives

  R_v o L_v=id on V^omega_(nu,t0),
  L_v o R_v=id on G^omega_(nu,rho,t0).                (CG.16)

Thus the exact root factors as

  N^omega_(nu,t0) ~= V^omega_(nu,t0)
                  ~= G^omega_(nu,rho,t0).            (CG.17)

This is an equivalence of representations of the same theorem. The original
maps agree with the normalized implementation because (CG.14) identifies
the completed tensor and radial uniqueness identifies the metric. The root
retains the same pressure-gauge classes, analytic solution germs, parameters,
marking, geometry, normalization and two inverse laws.

IX.0. Actual coordinate definitions and regularity.

Let O be an open coordinate domain with coordinates (r,y^0,y^1,y^2,y^3).
Let h_ab(r,y) be a real symmetric invertible C^3 matrix field, and set

  g_rr=1, g_ra=0, g_ab=h_ab,
  g^{rr}=1, g^{ra}=0, g^{ab}=h^{ab}=(h^{-1})^{ab},
  K_ab=(1/2)partial_r h_ab,
  B^a_b=h^{ac}K_cb,
  kappa=B^a_a,
  Q=K_ab K^{ab}=tr(B^2).                              (GE.1)

The scalar Q is a Lorentzian contraction, not a nonnegative Euclidean norm.
All identities below hold for any nondegenerate signature of h; the intended
sector has signature (-,+,+,+). The root's analyticity hypotheses supply
all required differentiability and permit every later jet argument.

Capital indices range over the full five coordinates; lower-case a,b,c,d
range over the four tangential coordinates. The coordinate definitions are

  Gamma^A_BC=(1/2)g^{AD}
                  (partial_B g_DC+partial_C g_DB-partial_D g_BC),

  R^A_{B I J}=partial_I Gamma^A_JB-partial_J Gamma^A_IB
               +Gamma^A_IC Gamma^C_JB-Gamma^A_JC Gamma^C_IB,

  Ric_BJ=R^A_{B A J},
  R[g]=g^{BJ}Ric_BJ,
  G_BJ=Ric_BJ-(1/2)R[g]g_BJ.                          (GE.2)

These definitions reproduce the Ricci convention in Sections 9 and 28.
The lower-case gamma^a_bc, D, Ric[h], and R[h] are obtained from the same
definitions using the tangential metric h at each fixed r.

IX.1. Inverse, determinant and volume identities.

Differentiating h h^{-1}=I and multiplying in the actual matrix order gives

  partial_r h^{ab}=-2h^{ac}K_cd h^{db}=-2K^{ab},
  partial_r kappa=h^{ab}partial_r K_ab-2Q.             (GE.3)

For every coordinate z^I, differentiation of the determinant's finite
permutation sum gives

  partial_I det h=tr(adjugate(h) partial_I h)
                =(det h)tr(h^{-1}partial_I h).       (GE.4)

The first equality follows by differentiating one factor in each permutation
product and collecting the coefficient of each matrix derivative; the
coefficient is the corresponding cofactor. The second uses the genuine
inverse identity adjugate(h)=(det h)h^{-1}, justified by det h!=0.

Let mu=sqrt(|det h|)>0. The determinant has locally constant sign, so the
ordinary scalar chain rule and (GE.4) give

  partial_r log(mu)=kappa,
  partial_r mu=kappa mu,
  partial_a log(mu)=(1/2)h^{bc}partial_a h_bc
                   =gamma^b_ba.                     (GE.5)

Thus mu is the coordinate density sqrt(|det g|) as well. No positive-
definiteness assumption on h is used in (GE.3)-(GE.5).

IX.2. Complete Christoffel table and differential identities.

Substitution of g_rr=1 and g_ra=0 in (GE.2) yields

  Gamma^r_rr=Gamma^r_ra=Gamma^a_rr=0,
  Gamma^r_ab=-K_ab,
  Gamma^a_rb=Gamma^a_br=B^a_b,
  Gamma^a_bc=gamma^a_bc.                              (GE.6)

For example Gamma^r_ab=-(1/2)partial_r h_ab and
Gamma^a_rb=(1/2)h^{ac}partial_r h_cb. Every remaining component is either
zero from a derivative of 1 or 0, or precisely the tangential formula.
Consequently

  Gamma^I_Ir=kappa,  Gamma^I_Ia=gamma^b_ba.            (GE.7)

The lower Christoffel indices are symmetric. The connection is metric
compatible: in

  nabla_A g_BC=partial_A g_BC
              -Gamma^D_AB g_DC-Gamma^D_AC g_BD,

the two Christoffel terms sum to partial_A g_BC directly by their defining
formula. Differentiating g g^{-1}=I then gives nabla g^{-1}=0.

For a C^1 vector field V and a C^2 scalar phi, the divergence and scalar
second-order operator therefore have the actual coordinate forms

  nabla_I V^I=mu^{-1}[partial_r(mu V^r)+partial_a(mu V^a)],
  Box_g phi=partial_r^2 phi+kappa partial_r phi+Box_h phi. (GE.8)

The first identity follows from (GE.5)-(GE.7). For the second, set
V^I=g^{IJ}partial_J phi. Box_h is the Lorentzian tangential trace of the
Hessian; no ellipticity assertion is attached to this identity.

IX.3. Curvature symmetries from finite connection algebra.

Write (Gamma_I)^A_B=Gamma^A_IB and let

  L_I=partial_I+Gamma_I

act on vector-valued coordinate functions. Expansion and commuting ordinary
coordinate derivatives give

  [L_I,L_J]V=R_IJ V,
  R_IJ=partial_I Gamma_J-partial_J Gamma_I+[Gamma_I,Gamma_J]. (GE.9)

Antisymmetry R_IJ=-R_JI is immediate. Torsion-freeness gives the algebraic
first Bianchi identity

  R^A_{B I J}+R^A_{I J B}+R^A_{J B I}=0.              (GE.10)

For completeness, the derivative terms in this cyclic sum are

  partial_I Gamma^A_JB-partial_J Gamma^A_IB
  +partial_J Gamma^A_BI-partial_B Gamma^A_JI
  +partial_B Gamma^A_IJ-partial_I Gamma^A_BJ,

and cancel in pairs by symmetry of the lower Christoffel indices. The
quadratic terms are

  Gamma^A_IC Gamma^C_JB-Gamma^A_JC Gamma^C_IB
  +Gamma^A_JC Gamma^C_BI-Gamma^A_BC Gamma^C_JI
  +Gamma^A_BC Gamma^C_IJ-Gamma^A_IC Gamma^C_BJ,

which cancel in the same manner. This proves (GE.10) without a curvature
axiom.

Lower the first index: R_ABIJ=g_AC R^C_{B I J}. The connection's extension
to duals and tensor products obeys the finite product rule. Applying its
commutator to the parallel tensor g gives

  0=[nabla_I,nabla_J]g_AB
    =-R^C_{A I J}g_CB-R^C_{B I J}g_AC.

Thus R_ABIJ=-R_BAIJ. The tensor-product commutator formula here follows by
applying (GE.9) to each factor; the curvature acts with the opposite sign
on a dual factor. Equivalently it follows by expanding the two covariant
derivatives of a covariant two-tensor. The lower derivative-index connection
terms cancel in the commutator because Gamma^C_IJ=Gamma^C_JI.

Combining the two skew symmetries with (GE.10) gives pair symmetry. An
explicit algebraic calculation is

  2R_ABCD
    =(R_ACBD+R_BCDA)+(-R_ADBC+R_BDAC)
    =R_CDAB+R_CDAB,

where the first line uses the Bianchi identities with first indices A,B;
the two bracket evaluations use those with first indices C,D. Hence

  R_ABCD=R_CDAB.                                     (GE.11)

Finally

  Ric_BJ=g^{AC}R_CB A J
        =g^{AC}R_A J C B
        =g^{AC}R_C J A B=Ric_JB,

where pair symmetry and the exchange of dummy indices A,C were used.
Thus the actual Ricci tensor and Einstein tensor are symmetric.

IX.4. Differential and contracted Bianchi identities.

The commutator Jacobi identity for the operators L_I is a finite algebraic
identity: expanding the six triple products cancels every term. By (GE.9),

  [L_L,[L_I,L_J]]+[L_I,[L_J,L_L]]+[L_J,[L_L,L_I]]=0

becomes

  (partial_L R_IJ+[Gamma_L,R_IJ])
  +(partial_I R_JL+[Gamma_I,R_JL])
  +(partial_J R_LI+[Gamma_J,R_LI])=0.                 (GE.12)

The full covariant derivative of this endomorphism-valued two-form is

  nabla_L R_IJ=partial_L R_IJ+[Gamma_L,R_IJ]
                  -Gamma^K_LI R_KJ-Gamma^K_LJ R_IK.

The extra lower-index terms cancel in the cyclic sum: for example
-Gamma^K_LI R_KJ cancels -Gamma^K_IL R_JK; the other two pairs cancel
identically. Therefore (GE.12) gives the differential Bianchi identity

  nabla_L R^A_{B I J}+nabla_I R^A_{B J L}
                       +nabla_J R^A_{B L I}=0.       (GE.13)

Metric compatibility permits index lowering and contraction through nabla.
Lowering A and contracting it with L gives

  nabla^A R_ABIJ=nabla_I Ric_BJ-nabla_J Ric_BI.        (GE.14)

Indeed, the other two contracted curvature terms are -Ric_BJ and Ric_BI,
respectively, by the last-index skew symmetry and the definition of Ricci.
Now contract B,J. The left side becomes nabla^A Ric_AI because
g^{BJ}R_ABIJ=Ric_AI, and the right side becomes
nabla_I R[g]-nabla^B Ric_BI. Hence

  2 nabla^A Ric_AI=nabla_I R[g],
  nabla^A G_AI=0.                                    (GE.15)

This derivation uses only the displayed connection, inverse metric, finite
product rules, commuting partial derivatives, and the commutator Jacobi
identity. The contracted Bianchi equation is thereby derived from the
actual coordinate metric rather than introduced as an independent premise.
Its later Lean implementation is still a new formal proof body.

IX.5. Gaussian Ricci blocks from the Christoffel table.

Use the expanded definition

  Ric_BJ=partial_I Gamma^I_JB-partial_J Gamma^I_IB
           +Gamma^I_IC Gamma^C_JB-Gamma^I_JC Gamma^C_IB.

For B=J=r, all terms vanish except -partial_r Gamma^I_Ir and the product
-Gamma^a_rc Gamma^c_ar. Thus

  Ric_rr=-partial_r kappa-Q.                         (GE.16)

For B=r,J=a, the surviving terms are

  partial_b B^b_a-partial_a kappa
     +gamma^b_bc B^c_a-gamma^b_ac B^c_b.

The last expression is the covariant divergence of the mixed tensor B
minus the scalar derivative of kappa. Therefore

  Ric_ra=D_b B^b_a-D_a kappa.                        (GE.17)

For tangential indices a,b, the purely tangential terms assemble into
Ric[h]_ab. The remaining derivative term is -partial_r K_ab. The trace
product contributes -kappa K_ab. The two cross products with exactly one
radial contracted index each contribute K_ac B^c_b. Consequently

  Ric_ab=Ric[h]_ab-partial_r K_ab-kappa K_ab
                         +2K_ac B^c_b.              (GE.18)

These are exactly the signs used in (9.2). Since K=h_r/2, equation
Ric_ab=0 is exactly

  h_rr=2Ric[h]-kappa h_r+h_r h^{-1}h_r,               (GE.19)

with no change to the radial CK normal map.

IX.6. Scalar curvature, Hamiltonian constraint and trace identities.

Contracting (GE.18) with h^{ab} and using (GE.3) gives

  h^{ab}Ric_ab=R[h]-partial_r kappa-kappa^2.

Adding (GE.16) gives

  R[g]=R[h]-2partial_r kappa-kappa^2-Q.               (GE.20)

Hence

  G_rr=(kappa^2-Q-R[h])/2.                           (GE.21)

At the flat marked boundary h(0)=eta, tangential derivatives of h(0)
vanish, so R[h](0)=0. The initial Hamiltonian relation Q-kappa^2=0 is
therefore equivalent to G_rr(0)=0. Equation (GE.17) identifies Codazzi
with the initial condition G_ra(0)=Ric_ra(0)=0.

For any five-dimensional nondegenerate metric,

  g^{AB}G_AB=R[g]-(5/2)R[g]=-(3/2)R[g].             (GE.22)

Thus G=0 implies R[g]=0 and Ric=0; the converse is immediate.
Alternatively, once Ric_ab=0 is already known, G_rr=Ric_rr/2 and
G_ra=Ric_ra, so vanishing of G_rr,G_ra completes the remaining Ricci
components directly. This shorter endpoint has the same root conclusion.

IX.7. Block divergence of an arbitrary symmetric tensor.

Let T be a C^1 symmetric covariant two-tensor. Write

  E=T_rr, M_a=T_ra, S_ab=T_ab,
  M^a=h^{ab}M_b, S^a_b=h^{ac}S_cb.

Use the actual mixed divergence

  nabla_I T^I_J=partial_I T^I_J
                +Gamma^I_IK T^K_J-Gamma^K_IJ T^I_K.

For J=r, substitution of (GE.6)-(GE.7) gives

  nabla_I T^I_r=partial_r E+D_a M^a+kappa E-K^{ab}S_ab. (GE.23)

For J=a, the two cross terms are

  -B^c_a M_c+K_ba M^b=0,

and the remaining terms give

  nabla_I T^I_a=partial_r M_a+D_b S^b_a+kappa M_a.     (GE.24)

The cancellation in (GE.24) uses the covariant normal-tangential component
M_a. Replacing it by M^a without differentiating the inverse metric would
introduce an error. Symmetry of T is used to identify its two mixed blocks.

IX.8. Exact Bianchi system with tangential Ricci defect.

For the actual Einstein tensor, define

  C_ab=Ric_ab, c=h^{ab}C_ab,
  E=G_rr, M_a=G_ra.

Directly from the definitions,

  Ric_rr=2E+c,
  R[g]=2(E+c),
  G_ab=C_ab-(E+c)h_ab.                               (GE.25)

Insert this tangential block into (GE.23)-(GE.24) and use (GE.15):

  E_r=-D_a M^a-2kappa E+K^{ab}C_ab-kappa c,
  (M_a)_r=D_a E-kappa M_a+D_a c-D_b C^b_a.           (GE.26)

This is an exact identity for every C^3 Gaussian metric of the stated
signature and convention. No tangential field equation has yet been imposed.
The two final source terms in each equation express precisely the effect
of a nonzero tangential Ricci defect.

For C_ab=0, equation (GE.26) reduces to

  E_r=-D_a M^a-2kappa E,
  (M_a)_r=D_a E-kappa M_a,                            (GE.27)

which is exactly (9.4), now obtained from the actual coordinate definitions.
The coefficient matrices and zero-order terms are analytic whenever h is
analytic and invertible. CJ11 and CJ14 then prove vanishing from zero initial
constraints. Together with C=0, this proves full Ric(g)=0.

IX.9. Finite-order constraint propagation with a controlled Ricci defect.

Let h be smooth (analytic in the root application) and let N>=0. Suppose

  partial_r^j C_ab|r=0=0 as tangential functions for 0<=j<=N,
  E|r=0=0, M_a|r=0=0.                               (GE.28)

Then

  partial_r^j E|r=0=0,
  partial_r^j M_a|r=0=0,  0<=j<=N+1.                (GE.29)

Proof. Expanding the covariant derivatives in (GE.26) expresses the system
for W=(E,M) as

  W_r=A^b partial_b W+B W+L^b partial_b C+L^0 C,

where the coefficients are smooth functions of h,h^{-1},K and tangential
first derivatives of h. The trace c and raised C^b_a are included in the
last two terms; derivatives of their metric coefficients are zero-order
coefficients multiplying C. The hypotheses imply that every normal jet
through order N of the inhomogeneous term is zero, including its tangential
derivatives. For n=0,...,N, differentiate this equation n times in r.
The Leibniz expansion involves W_0,...,W_n and source jets through n.
Starting from W_0=0, induction gives W_(n+1)=0. QED.

On a compact smaller tangential set, bounded Taylor remainders therefore
give C=O(r^{N+1}) and W=O(r^{N+2}) when the requisite derivatives are
bounded. This is a finite-order statement for an existing metric; it is
not an existence theorem and does not assert any error estimate for a
numerically constructed metric without verification of its hypotheses.

Taking all orders with C=0 and using analyticity recovers the exact
propagation result. The finite-order form makes the constraint behavior
of exact truncated radial jets explicit for subsequent formal interfaces.

IX.10. Atomic contracts and source assignments.

The following assignments use the already-inspected source register.
No complete geometric theorem is attributed to an elementary matrix or
analytic-calculus endpoint. New source additions may refine the support
columns without changing any statement or assumption below.

=======================================================================
X.A. EXACT SECOND-ORDER TO FIRST-ORDER QUASILINEAR REDUCTION
=======================================================================

X.A1. Original second-order normal form.

Let d,N>=1, s in R, y in R^d, U(s,y) in R^N. Let F be real analytic on
an open finite-dimensional domain in the variables

  (s,y,U,V,W,A,B),
  V in R^N,
  W=(W_i)_(1<=i<=d), W_i in R^N,
  A=(A_ij)_(1<=i<=j<=d), A_ij in R^N,
  B=(B_i)_(1<=i<=d), B_i in R^N.

Extend A_ij=A_ji when i>j. The original equation is

  U_ss=F(s,y,U,U_s,D_y U,D_y^2 U,D_y U_s),           (CK.A1)
  U(0,y)=a(y), U_s(0,y)=b(y),

for analytic a,b. The initial point (0,y0,a,b,Da,D^2a,Db) is assumed
to belong to the open domain of F. This is exactly the CJ13 scope.

X.A2. A quasilinear system with the original reduced-jet slot count.

Use Y=(U,V,W,A,B) itself as the unknown. For each component index i,
define the following first-order normal system:

  U_s=V,
  V_s=F(s,y,U,V,W,A,B),
  (W_i)_s=B_i,
  (A_ij)_s=(partial_i B_j+partial_j B_i)/2, i<=j,
  (B_i)_s=F_yi+F_U W_i+F_V B_i
             +sum_j F_Wj A_ij
             +sum_(j<=k) F_Ajk partial_i A_jk
             +sum_j F_Bj partial_i B_j.              (CK.A2)

All derivatives of F in (CK.A2) are finite-dimensional partial derivatives
evaluated at (s,y,U,V,W,A,B). Thus F_U,F_V,F_Wj,F_Ajk,F_Bj are linear maps
R^N -> R^N and F_yi is the explicit derivative in the coordinate y_i.
The notation F_U W_i means application of that linear map, not a scalar
product. No derivative is taken with respect to the normal variable s in
the chain rule for (B_i)_s.

The right side of (CK.A2) is affine in the first tangential derivatives of
Y. Every coefficient is analytic in (s,y,Y), since finite derivatives of
an analytic map are analytic. Hence (CK.A2) has the exact form

  Y_s=sum_i A^i(s,y,Y) partial_i Y+B0(s,y,Y).         (CK.A3)

Its Cauchy data are

  Y(0,y)=(a(y),b(y),Da(y),D^2a(y),Db(y)).             (CK.A4)

The number of scalar unknowns is

  m=N*(2+2d+d(d+1)/2).                               (CK.A5)

The normal coefficient is the identity on R^m. No characteristic or
hyperbolicity assumption is added.

X.A3. Compatibility propagation for the reduced system.

Suppose an actual analytic Y solves (CK.A2)--(CK.A4). Define, for all i,j,

  C_i=W_i-partial_i U,
  D_i=B_i-partial_i V,
  E_ij=A_ij-partial_i W_j.

The defect E is the full d by d array; it is not assumed symmetric.
Its antisymmetric components are included in the compatibility proof.

Direct differentiation, with all coefficients evaluated on the existing
analytic Y, gives

  (C_i)_s=D_i,
  (D_i)_s=F_U C_i+F_V D_i+sum_j F_Wj E_ij,
  (E_ij)_s=(partial_j D_i-partial_i D_j)/2.           (CK.A6)

Proof.
The first equation follows from U_s=V and (W_i)_s=B_i. For the second,
subtract partial_i(V_s)=partial_i F from the displayed (B_i)_s equation.
The explicit F_yi terms and every F_Ajk partial_i A_jk and
F_Bj partial_i B_j term cancel. The remaining terms are precisely those
in the second line of (CK.A6). For the third line,

  (E_ij)_s=(partial_i B_j+partial_j B_i)/2-partial_i B_j
           =(partial_j B_i-partial_i B_j)/2.

Substitute B_i=D_i+partial_i V; mixed tangential derivatives of V commute.
This proves (CK.A6). QED.

The initial data (CK.A4) give C_i=D_i=E_ij=0 at s=0, as functions of y.
System (CK.A6) is a homogeneous analytic first-order normal PDE for the
already-existing defect array. CJ11/CJ14 apply: repeated s differentiation
and Leibniz induction give all normal defect jets zero; tangential
differentiation then gives all mixed jets zero; analytic Taylor expansion
gives C=D=E=0 as germs. No separate defect existence theorem is needed.

Consequently

  V=U_s, W_i=partial_i U, B_i=partial_i U_s,
  A_ij=partial_i partial_j U,

and V_s=F is exactly (CK.A1). Conversely, an actual analytic solution U of
(CK.A1) defines Y by these derivative formulas; the ordinary chain rule
and commutation of mixed derivatives give (CK.A2)--(CK.A4).

Thus the construction is an exact bijection of the respective local
analytic solution germs. The augmented variables introduce no additional
solutions with the prescribed compatible Cauchy data.

X.A4. Alternative redundant prolongation with an ODE-only compatibility law.

An implementation may instead take

  X=(U,V,W_1,...,W_d) in R^M, M=N(d+2),
  P_i in R^M, i=1,...,d.

Define a linear extraction from arbitrary (X,P) to the reduced second jet:

  Q_ij=((P_i)^(W_j)+(P_j)^(W_i))/2,
  R_i=(P_i)^V,

and define

  G(s,y,X,P)=(V, F(s,y,U,V,W,Q,R), R_1,...,R_d).

The preimage of F's open jet domain under this extraction is open and
contains the compatible initial point. Symmetrization is a continuous
linear map, so this is an actual analytic extension off the integrable
jet locus; no analytic extension theorem is assumed.

The prolonged system is

  X_s=G(s,y,X,P),
  (P_i)_s=G_yi+G_X P_i+sum_j G_Pj partial_i P_j,      (CK.A7)
  X(0,y)=(a,b,Da), P_i(0,y)=partial_i(a,b,Da).

It is first-order quasilinear in Y_red=(X,P_1,...,P_d). For the defect

  D_i=P_i-partial_i X,

the chain rule gives the simpler law

  (D_i)_s=G_X D_i, D_i(0,y)=0.                        (CK.A8)

Indeed the sums G_Pj partial_i P_j cancel exactly; replacing them with
sum_j G_Pj partial_j P_i before proving compatibility would change this
identity and is not permitted in this construction.

For each fixed y, (CK.A8) is an ordinary finite-dimensional homogeneous
linear ODE. Its zero solution is unique even without analytic assumptions:
on a compact normal interval let ||G_X||<=K and ||D_i||<=B. The integral
equation iterated n times gives

  |D_i(s,y)|<=B K^n |s|^n/n!, all n.

The right side tends to zero. The same argument applies for negative s
after reversing the normal parameter. Thus P_i=partial_i X. It follows
that W_i-partial_i U has zero normal derivative and zero initial value,
so W=DU; reconstruction of (CK.A1) follows as before.

The redundant scalar-unknown count is

  m_red=N(d+2)(d+1).

Its excess over (CK.A5) is N*d(d+1)/2: Nd repeated first derivatives and
N*d(d-1)/2 antisymmetric second-derivative slots. These are constrained by
compatibility, not free data.

MODEL | d | N | REDUCED QL UNKNOWNS | REDUNDANT QL UNKNOWNS
scalar completion | 3 | 1 | 14 | 20
radial symmetric metric | 4 | 10 | 200 | 300

The reduced system is used below because it preserves the original
reduced-jet dimension. The redundant system supplies an independent
compatibility route whose defect equation is only an ODE.

=======================================================================
X.B. CONSTRUCTIVE FIRST-ORDER QUASILINEAR ANALYTIC EXISTENCE
=======================================================================

X.B1. Exact theorem.

Let d,m>=1. Let A^i(s,y,Y) be real-analytic m by m matrices and let
B(s,y,Y) be a real-analytic m-vector on an open neighborhood of
(0,0,phi(0)). Let phi(y) be real analytic near zero. Then the equation

  Y_s=sum_(i=1)^d A^i(s,y,Y) partial_i Y+B(s,y,Y),
  Y(0,y)=phi(y),                                      (CK.B1)

has an actual jointly real-analytic local solution. Existing analytic
solutions with these data agree as germs. The proof below gives a
data-dependent explicit convergence domain after coefficient bounds
have been selected.

X.B2. Shift to zero initial data.

Put v=Y-phi(y). Then

  v_s=sum_i Atilde^i(s,y,v) partial_i v+Btilde(s,y,v),
  v(0,y)=0,                                          (CK.B2)

where

  Atilde^i(s,y,v)=A^i(s,y,v+phi(y)),
  Btilde(s,y,v)=B(s,y,v+phi(y))
                +sum_i Atilde^i(s,y,v)partial_i phi(y).

All these functions are analytic. Therefore finite-dimensional
complexification and strict shrinking provide R>0,C>0 such that every
entry of every Atilde^i and Btilde is holomorphic on a neighborhood of
the closed coordinate polydisc of radius R in (s,y,v) and is bounded
there by C. A finite maximum gives one common C. Increasing C if
necessary makes it strictly positive even when all coefficients vanish.

Multivariable Cauchy coefficient estimates then imply

  |a_(n,alpha,beta)|<=C R^(-n-|alpha|-|beta|)          (CK.B3)

for every Taylor coefficient of those entries. This uses ordinary
power-series coefficients, so no extra factorial occurs in (CK.B3).

X.B3. Formal coefficient order and one rational majorant.

For multivariable formal series P,Q, write P prec Q when every absolute
coefficient of P is bounded by the corresponding nonnegative coefficient
of Q. Products, sums, tangential differentiation and substitution of
zero-constant series preserve the appropriate coefficient inequalities.
These statements follow term by term from finite coefficient convolution.

The bound (CK.B3) yields, componentwise,

  Atilde^i_ab, Btilde_a
      prec C/[1-(s+sum_i y_i+sum_a v_a)/R].           (CK.B4)

Indeed the right coefficient of total degree q is C R^(-q) multiplied
by a multinomial coefficient at least one. This majorizes (CK.B3).

Set

  L=d*m, z=sum_i y_i.

Because d,m>=1, L>=m and L>=1. If each component v_a is majorized by
w(s,z), then each partial_i v_a is majorized by w_z(s,z). There are dm=L
terms in each sum_i sum_b Atilde^i_ab partial_i v_b. Thus a sufficient
scalar majorant equation is

  w_s=C/[1-(s+z+Lw)/R]*(1+L w_z),
  w(0,z)=0.                                          (CK.B5)

The replacement m w by L w in the denominator enlarges every nonnegative
coefficient. It is needed to align the majorant transport coefficient
with the total derivative-term count.

X.B4. Explicit analytic majorant by a uniform contraction.

Define the positive constants

  W=R/(4L),
  zeta=R/4,
  tau=min(R/8, R/(32 C L)).                           (CK.B6)

For complex |s|<=tau, |z|<=zeta and |w|<=W, put q=R-z-Lw and

  T_(s,z)(w)=C R sum_(n>=1) (s/q)^n/n.                (CK.B7)

This is the convergent logarithm series -CR log(1-s/q); the power-series
definition fixes its branch without a logarithm ambiguity. On this set,

  |q|>=R/2,
  |s/q|<=1/4,
  |T_(s,z)(w)|<=4C|s|<=W/2.                          (CK.B8)

The derivative is

  partial_w T_(s,z)(w)=C R L s/[q(q-s)],

and the bounds |q|>=R/2, |q-s|>=R/4 give

  |partial_w T_(s,z)(w)|<=8 C L tau/R<=1/4.           (CK.B9)

The straight segment between two points in the closed w-disc stays in
that disc. Integration of the complex derivative along the segment
therefore proves a uniform Lipschitz constant 1/4.

Starting at w_0(s,z)=0, define

  w_(n+1)(s,z)=T_(s,z)(w_n(s,z)).

Induction gives |w_n|<=W/2 for n>=1, and

  sup|w_(n+1)-w_n|<=(W/2)*4^(-n),
  sup|w-w_n|<=(2W/3)*4^(-n).                         (CK.B10)

Thus the iterates converge uniformly on the closed parameter polydisc
to a unique fixed point in the closed w-disc. Each iterate is holomorphic
on the open parameter polydisc: the rational denominator stays nonzero,
the logarithm series converges normally, and composition preserves
holomorphy. The limit is jointly holomorphic there. Here the joint assertion
has an explicit two-variable proof; a one-variable uniform-limit theorem
alone is not a direct joint-analyticity endpoint. Choose a smaller closed
polydisc around any interior parameter point and two enclosing circles
of radii r_s,r_z still inside the construction domain. Apply the iterated
Cauchy formula to each w_n and pass the uniform limit through both circle
integrals. Expansion of the two Cauchy kernels gives coefficients

  c_ab=(2*pi*i)^(-2) integral integral
          w(xi,eta) xi^(-a-1) eta^(-b-1) d eta d xi,

after translating that interior point to the origin. The norm bound gives

  |c_ab|<=(W/2) r_s^(-a) r_z^(-b).

The corresponding double geometric majorant converges absolutely on
every smaller polydisc. The iterated Cauchy formula then identifies its
sum with w. Thus w has a joint convergent complex power series at each
interior point. No analytic implicit-function theorem or analytic ODE
existence theorem is needed in this construction.

At s=0 the map T is zero, so w(0,z)=0. The fixed-point identity and the
exponential/logarithm power-series identity imply

  s=(R-z-Lw)*(1-exp(-w/(C R))).                       (CK.B11)

X.B5. Verification of the scalar PDE and nonnegative coefficients.

Let E=exp(-w/(C R)), q=R-z-Lw, and

  D=-L(1-E)+q E/(C R).

On the entire construction domain,

  D=(q-s)/(C R)*(1-partial_w T_(s,z)(w))!=0,

because |q-s|>=R/4 and |partial_w T|<=1/4. Differentiating (CK.B11) gives

  1=D w_s,
  1-E=D w_z,
  q-s=qE.

Consequently

  C/[1-(s+z+Lw)/R]*(1+Lw_z)
    =CR/(qE) * [D+L(1-E)]/D
    =CR/(qE) * [qE/(CR)]/D
    =1/D=w_s.

This proves (CK.B5) throughout the stated domain. An equivalent direct
verification avoids the exponential identity: the derivatives of (CK.B7)
satisfy T_s=CR/(q-s), T_z=CR*s/[q(q-s)] and T_w=L*T_z. Differentiating
w=T(s,z,w) gives w_s=T_s/(1-T_w), w_z=T_z/(1-T_w), and therefore
w_s=CR/(q-s)*(1+L*w_z), which is (CK.B5).

Every Taylor coefficient of w at (0,0) is nonnegative. One proof is
induction on the normal degree in (CK.B5): w_0(z)=0; the denominator
expansion has nonnegative coefficients; and the coefficient determining
(n+1)w_(n+1)(z) contains only w_0,...,w_n and their z derivatives.
Thus nonnegative coefficients propagate. Equivalently, every iterative
map (CK.B7), expanded at the origin, has nonnegative coefficients, and
uniform convergence on smaller polydiscs passes this property to w.

X.B6. Formal vector recursion and coefficient domination.

Construct formal v(s,y)=sum_(n>=1) v_n(y)s^n, where every v_n is a formal
series in y. The equation (CK.B2) determines

  (n+1)v_(n+1)
    =coefficient_(s^n)[sum_i Atilde^i(s,y,v)partial_i v
                         +Btilde(s,y,v)].             (CK.B12)

The right side uses only v_0,...,v_n, with v_0=0, so this is a
well-founded recursion in n. Composition is formally well defined:
v is divisible by s and therefore has zero total constant coefficient.
For each fixed total multi-index, the coefficient convolutions are finite.
The tangential coefficients need not be truncated; the induction takes
place in the ring of tangential formal series.

Induction using (CK.B4), the product/substitution inequalities, L>=m,
and the L derivative terms gives

  v_a(s,y) prec w(s,y_1+...+y_d), all a.              (CK.B13)

The derivative step is exact. If alpha is a tangential multi-index of
degree r, then the coefficient of y^alpha in w(s,sum y) is
binomial(r;alpha) times the coefficient of z^r in w. Differentiation
with respect to y_i multiplies by alpha_i+1 and converts the
multinomial coefficient of alpha+e_i to that of w_z(s,sum y). Thus
partial_i v_a prec w_z(s,sum y) introduces no additional dimension factor.
The only dimension factor is the explicit count L=dm above.

X.B7. Actual convergence, PDE identity and initial condition.

Since w is holomorphic for |s|<tau, |z|<zeta and has nonnegative
coefficients, (CK.B13) implies absolute convergence of v whenever

  |s|<tau, sum_i |y_i|<zeta,                          (CK.B14)

and

  |v_a(s,y)|<=w(|s|,sum_i |y_i|)<=W/2=R/(8L).        (CK.B15)

On every strictly smaller product domain, all fixed finite-order
differentiated series converge uniformly: polynomial factors in normal
and tangential degrees are absorbed by the strict radius margin.
Therefore v is jointly holomorphic and its actual derivatives are the
termwise derivatives of the constructed series.

The composition domains are also justified. On (CK.B14),

  |s|+sum_i |y_i|+sum_a |v_a|
    <R/8+R/4+mR/(8L)<=R/2.

Thus the shifted analytic coefficient functions are evaluated strictly
inside their original polydisc, and their composed Taylor series converge
absolutely. Substitution, differentiation and coefficient comparison
are valid operations on the actual analytic functions. Every coefficient
of the residual in (CK.B2) vanishes by (CK.B12); its convergent series
therefore represents the zero function. Also v(0,y)=0. Hence
Y=phi(y)+v(s,y) solves (CK.B1) on a neighborhood of the initial point.

All real coefficient data yield real formal coefficients, so conjugation
preserves the constructed series and its restriction to real s,y is a
jointly real-analytic solution. The complex domain is a proof witness,
not an additional assumption on the original analytic real data.

X.B8. Analytic uniqueness.

Any existing analytic solution of (CK.B2) has v(0,y)=0 and its Taylor
coefficients obey the same finite normal recursion (CK.B12). Induction
identifies every normal coefficient as a tangential function germ, and
hence every mixed Taylor coefficient. The convergent analytic series are
equal on a common smaller neighborhood. Thus the germ is unique.

This concludes a complete mathematical proof of the quasilinear CK
theorem (CK.B1) from finite-dimensional complexification, Cauchy coefficient
estimates, elementary uniformly contracting iteration, uniform holomorphic
limits, and justified power-series algebra. It does not infer convergence
from formal recursion alone.

=======================================================================
X.C. CONSEQUENCES FOR THE EXACT NSE--GR CONSTRUCTION
=======================================================================

X.C1. Generic CJ13 endpoint.

Apply the theorem of Subsection X.B to the reduced system (CK.A2). Its coefficients
are analytic, and its initial data (CK.A4) are analytic. The initial jet
belongs to the open coefficient domain by the CJ13 hypothesis. Parts
A2--A3 therefore yield an actual analytic solution of the original
second-order normal form, with the prescribed two Cauchy traces. The
existing analytic jet uniqueness proof gives the same germ uniqueness.

This proves the mathematical CJ13 endpoint constructively. Its Lean
implementation remains uncompiled and un-audited. No new axiom or theorem
name is imported by this reduction.

X.C2. Scalar and radial counts and conservative convergence scales.

For scalar completion, d=3,N=1 gives m=14 and L=42. After the analytic
coefficient bound (C,R) is chosen, the quasilinear majorant permits

  tau=min(R/8,R/(1344 C)), zeta=R/4.

For the ten-component radial symmetric metric, d=4,N=10 gives m=200 and
L=800, hence

  tau=min(R/8,R/(25600 C)), zeta=R/4.

These are conservative data-dependent scales in normalized local
coordinates of the zero-data-shifted quasilinear system. They are not
uniform lower bounds over all analytic NSE data. They are not radii
expressed solely in a Sobolev norm. The coefficient constants include the
actual source fields, initial analytic data, and domain margins.

For the scalar completion the data a=b=0 make all augmented initial
coordinates zero. For the radial problem a=eta and b=2K, so the reduced
data are

  (U,V,W,A,B)|s=0=(eta,2K,0,0,2D_y K).

The derivative D_y K is analytic because K is analytic. The zero-data
shift may use further tangential derivatives of these known analytic
data in its coefficients; no new regularity premise is introduced.

X.C3. Existing source assignments.

  Analytic finite sums/products/division: ML13--ML16.
  Analytic coordinate restrictions and composition: ML26--ML27.
  Analytic derivatives of finite normal maps: ML07--ML09.
  Derivative/product identities: ML10--ML12.
  Differentiated-series verification when using partial sums: ML28.
  Taylor coefficient/derivative adapter and analytic equality: ML29--ML30.
  Compact periodic gluing after local construction: ML20--ML23.
  Actual radial inverse coefficients and nondegeneracy: ML31--ML33.

The v1.3.0 source addendum additionally supplies ML40,
TendstoLocallyUniformlyOn.differentiableOn, from
Mathlib.Analysis.Complex.LocallyUniformLimit. Its domain variable is one
complex variable and its range is a complete complex normed space. It may
be applied on individual parameter slices; the joint two-parameter claim
still uses the explicit iterated Cauchy/power-series adapter proved in Subsection X.B4.
ML36 converts an actual complex differentiability result to analyticity
with its displayed source hypotheses. Neither endpoint supplies an
arbitrary multivariable normal-convergence assertion by itself.

The Cauchy coefficient bound, double-Cauchy adapter and logarithm/exponential
power-series interfaces retain their exact implementation obligations.
No additional uninspected declaration is asserted. Methlib pressure/Leray/
energy endpoints do not prove this CK convergence argument.

X.C4. Preserved scope.

The CK engine is applied to scalar completion and transverse metric
construction for an already-given analytic NSE germ. It does not produce
a time-analytic NSE germ from arbitrary analytic initial velocity. A
first-normal-order equation with second tangential derivatives, such as
NSE in time, does not become a closed first-order quasilinear system by
this finite second-normal-order construction: differentiating its
spatial jet equations continues to produce higher spatial orders.

The canonical geometric sector, arbitrary velocity mean, pressure gauge,
marked coordinates, local domain quantifiers and five-dimensional vacuum
conclusion all remain unchanged.

=======================================================================
X.D. ATOMIC PROOF CONTRACTS
=======================================================================

CK01 REDUCED_JET_COORDINATES
  Input: d,N>=1 and the exact open reduced-jet domain of F.
  Output: finite symmetric A coordinates, full index reconstruction, analytic
  coordinate maps and counts N*(2+2d+d(d+1)/2).
  Support: ML15--ML16, ML26--ML27; finite indexing is a new body.

CK02 REDUCED_QUASILINEAR_SYSTEM
  Input: analytic F and its actual finite-dimensional derivatives.
  Output: (CK.A2) in the exact quasilinear form (CK.A3), with analytic
  coefficients and data (CK.A4).
  Support: ML07--ML16, ML26--ML27; no existence theorem used.

CK03 REDUCED_COMPATIBILITY_PROPAGATION
  Input: an existing analytic solution of (CK.A2)--(CK.A4).
  Output: (CK.A6), zero defects, and a solution of the original CK2 equation.
  Support: CJ11/CJ14 and ML10--ML12, ML29; actual analytic zero-jet argument.

CK04 REDUNDANT_PROLONGATION_AND_ODE_DEFECT
  Input: analytic F, arbitrary (X,P) in the pulled-back open domain.
  Output: analytic symmetrized G, quasilinear system (CK.A7), ODE defect
  (CK.A8), and exact reconstruction with m_red=N(d+2)(d+1).
  Support: ML07--ML16, ML26--ML27; finite Volterra iteration proof included.

CK05 ZERO_INITIAL_DATA_SHIFT
  Input: analytic quasilinear coefficients and analytic phi.
  Output: (CK.B2), with analytic shifted coefficients and zero Cauchy data.
  Support: ML07--ML16, ML26--ML27.

CK06 COMMON_COEFFICIENT_MAJORANT
  Input: CK05's actual finite-dimensional analytic coefficients.
  Output: C,R>0, coefficient estimate (CK.B3), and rational majorant (CK.B4).
  Body: finite complexification, closed-polydisc shrinking, Cauchy estimates.
  ML41 reconstructs from circle integrals; ML42 bounds the corresponding
  one-variable Cauchy coefficients. Finite coordinate iteration and the
  actual Taylor-coefficient identification are explicit adapters. The
  constants are derived witnesses, not additional root assumptions.

CK07 UNIFORM_HOLOMORPHIC_SCALAR_CONTRACTION
  Input: C,R>0 and L>=1.
  Output: (CK.B6)--(CK.B11), uniform factor 1/4 convergence and actual
  holomorphic fixed point. The logarithm is defined by its convergent series.
  Body: rational bounds, convergent logarithm series, explicit iteration,
  uniform Cauchy limits and the explicit double-circle power-series adapter.
  ML40/ML36 supply only their correctly scoped one-variable analytic support.
  ML41/ML42 support both circle integrations and coefficient bounds; the
  joint two-variable series remains the explicit proved adapter.
  No analytic IFT or analytic ODE existence theorem is used.

CK08 MAJORANT_PDE_AND_POSITIVITY
  Input: CK07's analytic fixed point.
  Output: (CK.B5) and nonnegative Taylor coefficients.
  Body: implicit differentiation, algebra, normal coefficient induction.
  Support: ML11--ML16, ML28--ML29 and exact complex-series adapters.

CK09 FORMAL_VECTOR_RECURSION_AND_DOMINATION
  Input: CK06 and CK08, L=dm.
  Output: unique formal (CK.B12) and the coefficient bound (CK.B13).
  Body: multivariable coefficient products, zero-constant substitution,
  derivative multinomial identity and normal-degree induction.

CK10 ACTUAL_CONVERGENCE_AND_PDE
  Input: CK09, with CK07's explicit convergence domain.
  Output: convergent real-analytic solution of (CK.B1), exact equation and
  initial condition; bounds (CK.B14)--(CK.B15).
  Body: absolute domination, differentiated convergence on smaller domains,
  analytic substitution and zero residual coefficients.
  Support: ML28--ML29 after the actual convergence hypotheses are proved.

CK11 ANALYTIC_UNIQUENESS
  Input: existing analytic solutions of (CK.B1) with the same initial data.
  Output: equality of every coefficient by (CK.B12), hence equality of germs.
  Support: CJ14, ML29--ML30; no convergence conclusion from formal jets alone.

CK12 EXACT_CK2_INSTANTIATION
  Input: CK01--CK03 and CK05--CK11, plus the original CJ13 hypotheses.
  Output: actual CK2 existence and uniqueness; scalar/radial instances and
  their exact germ correspondence. CK04 is an optional alternative adapter.
  Support: CJ03/CJ07 for the specific scalar/radial maps, CJ15 for compact
  gluing; all original Hamiltonian/Codazzi and geometric obligations remain.

MATHEMATICAL_CK_CONVERGENCE = DERIVED_BY_EXPLICIT_MAJORANT_CONTRACTION.
FORMAL_CK_CONVERGENCE = NOT_COMPILED_OR_AXIOM_AUDITED.
SOURCE_ENDPOINT_PROMOTION = NONE.

[ML42]
ENDPOINT = norm_cauchyPowerSeries_le
IMPORT = Mathlib.MeasureTheory.Integral.CircleIntegral
REVISION = db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d
BLOB_SHA = 03eddc0ee2aa1aec79060846bda5eeff20383e65
SOURCE = https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/CircleIntegral.lean#L623-L636
CONTEXT_BEGIN
{E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℂ E].
Both the definition and theorem are in the root namespace; Complex is opened.
No CompleteSpace E, differentiability, continuity, CircleIntegrable, or positive-radius premise occurs in this theorem's signature.
CONTEXT_END
SOURCE_DEFINITION_BEGIN
def cauchyPowerSeries (f : ℂ → E) (c : ℂ) (R : ℝ) : FormalMultilinearSeries ℂ ℂ E := fun n =>
  ContinuousMultilinearMap.mkPiRing ℂ _ <|
    (2 * π * I : ℂ)⁻¹ • ∮ z in C(c, R), (z - c)⁻¹ ^ n • (z - c)⁻¹ • f z
SOURCE_DEFINITION_END
SOURCE_SIGNATURE_BEGIN
theorem norm_cauchyPowerSeries_le (f : ℂ → E) (c : ℂ) (R : ℝ) (n : ℕ) :
    ‖cauchyPowerSeries f c R n‖ ≤
      ((2 * π)⁻¹ * ∫ θ : ℝ in 0..2 * π, ‖f (circleMap c R θ)‖) * |R|⁻¹ ^ n
SOURCE_SIGNATURE_END
PLACEMENT = CK06: boundary norm bound M and R>0 give the coefficient bound M/R^n after bounding the displayed normalized interval integral. CK07: apply the estimate successively to the two normalized Cauchy coefficient constructions to obtain |c_ab| <= M*r_s^(-a)*r_z^(-b), including M=W/2 for the scalar-majorant limit.
REMAINING_OBLIGATION = The theorem bounds the operator norm of a formal multilinear coefficient. Identify the scalar coefficient by evaluating on the all-ones tuple and unfolding cauchyPowerSeries; then establish the integral-average bound from boundary continuity/integrability and the uniform pointwise bound. For multiple variables, iterate the actual circle-coefficient construction and supply all joint-domain and sum/integral identifications. No multivariable result is imported by naming this one-variable coefficient estimate.
STATUS = SOURCE_VERIFIED_SUPPORT; TARGET_APPLICATION_UNCOMPILED

C. EXACT PLACEMENT AND COMPOSITION
=======================================================================

CP10 / NP08:
  ML34 -> ML35 -> normalized first primitive b with b'=a,b(t0)=0.
  Repeat ML34 -> ML35 on b -> c with c'=b,c(t0)=0.
  The derivative premise implies complex differentiability on the disc;
  ML36 then supplies analyticity. Thus c''=a,c(t0)=c'(t0)=0.
  ML37 twice gives uniqueness of this normalized second primitive.
  ML38/ML39 support the real-axis integral representation on either side of t0.
  NEW: analytic real-axis descent, c=I2a, sharp R^2/2 norm bound, additive
       composition, and the actual tensor identity for (p,psi)->(p+a,psi-I2a).

CP03 / AF04 / AF06:
  Original analytic Fourier bounds -> componentwise RapidScalarFourier.
  ME18 supplies actual physical synthesis, derivatives and coefficient inverse.
  ME06 supplies equality of continuous periodic physical representatives.
  NEW: common-domain analytic identification and real/vector type conversions.

CP05 / NP04 / velocity-only normal form:
  ME01-ME03: exact Leray algebra and longitudinal sign.
  ME07-ME09: actual pressure, its mean zero, and quadratic Fourier coefficients.
  ME18: actual velocity realization and spatial derivative identities.
  AF07/AF10: analytic Poisson and canonical pressure bounds.
  NEW: pressure uniqueness from zero spatial gradient plus mean zero, pressure
       gauge quotient normalization, and the exact velocity-only inverse maps.

CP19 / NP16:
  ME18 is spatial only. AF09 and ML28 remain necessary when time derivatives
  of the physical synthesis are inferred from coefficient evolution. ME10
  identifies the spatial RHS only after these temporal premises are supplied.

CP09 / NP06:
  Retain ML31-ML33 for actual matrix inverse identities and ML11-ML12 for
  finite differentiation. No additional determinant/inverse derivative endpoint
  is selected by this bounded audit. The signed Christoffel/Ricci/Bianchi
  formulas require the new exact coordinate proofs, regardless of matrix
  inverse source support.

CK scalar-majorant existence:
  Picard contraction on the specified common complex parameter polydisc gives
  uniform convergence of holomorphic iterates. ML40 and ML36 apply on scalar
  slices; the double-Cauchy reconstruction establishes joint analytic power
  series. Keep that reconstruction and coefficientwise domination explicit.
  The one-variable analytic local inverse was also located at the pin, but
  is not indexed: it does not supply the needed parameter-analytic implicit
  theorem directly, and the contraction route removes that dependency.
  ML41 supplies normalized Cauchy reconstruction for each scalar slice.
  ML42 supplies actual formal-coefficient norm bounds from normalized boundary
  norm integrals. CK06/CK07 remain new multi-index and double-integral proof
  bodies, now supported by these exact one-variable declarations.

FINAL_AUDIT_BOUND:
  Only the already audited CauchyIntegral file and its single direct import
  CircleIntegral were inspected for this final extension. No further source
  families, global CK theorem, formal execution, or additional IDs are claimed.

CRITICAL_SCOPE:
  Primitive existence on an open complex disc does not extend the NSE class.
  Local integral identities do not assert global time regularity.
  Actual spatial synthesis is not an imported time-evolution theorem.
  Source verification does not promote any new endpoint or root to A_TIER.

37.1. Version 1.3.0 refinement counts and roles.

V1_4_REFINEMENT = Section 43 extends this composition with HD, FC and GD;
the exact root and all earlier proved bodies are retained.

  Existing atoms: AF01-AF11, CJ01-CJ16, ST01-ST08 = 35.
  New atoms: CG01-CG10, GE01-GE12, CK01-CK12 = 34.
  Total atomic contracts = 69; parent contracts NP01-NP16 remain intact.
  Source declarations = ML01-ML42; source groups = ME01-ME18.

Every named atom has its exact mathematical hypotheses, conclusion and proof
in the associated section. These names are planning identifiers; no claim
that matching Lean declarations already exist is made.

37.2. Canonical pressure and gauge branch.

  AF01-AF10 + actual solenoidal u -> CG01.
  CG01 + actual equation definitions + periodic integration -> CG02.
  CG02 + gradient-kernel/mean-zero Poincare -> CG03.
  CG01-CG03 + analytic means -> CG04 -> CG05.
  analytic time gauge + ML34/ML35 twice + ML36 -> CG06 existence.
  ML37 twice + ML38/ML39 + explicit kernel identities -> full CG06.
  CG06 + actual S,C definitions -> CG07.
  CG07 + periodic harmonic uniqueness -> CG08.
  CG07 + scalar/radial uniqueness on the stated data -> CG09.
  CG05 + CG09 + the original actual inverse computations -> CG10.

The quotient normalization may be implemented first, so the core lift is
defined on velocity germs with the explicit pressure pi(u). CG09 proves
that this implementation is exactly the original lift on pressure classes.
Neither an arbitrary pressure witness nor a different NSE predicate replaces
the original theorem.

37.3. Actual-coordinate geometric branch.

  actual invertible h + ML31-ML33 + finite derivatives -> GE01.
  GE01 + the displayed metric/connection definitions -> GE02.
  GE02 -> GE03 -> GE04; GE03 -> GE05.
  GE02 + GE04 + GE05 -> GE06 (contracted Bianchi).
  GE02 -> GE07; GE01 + GE07 -> GE08.
  GE02 -> GE09; GE06 + GE09 -> GE10.
  actual analytic radial solution + initial Hamiltonian/Codazzi
    + GE07-GE10 + CJ11 + CJ14 -> GE11 -> Ric(g)=0.
  GE10 + finite vanishing Ricci-source jets -> GE12.

All curvature objects are computed from the actual metric. The finite
commutator and contraction proofs do not assume a Bianchi axiom. Their Lean
bodies still require external elaboration and theorem-level axiom auditing.

37.4. Constructive analytic existence branch.

  exact CJ13 normal map + analytic data -> CK01 -> CK02.
  CK02's actual analytic coefficients/data -> CK05.
  CK05 + finite complexification + ML41/ML42 adapters -> CK06.
  C,R>0 and L=dm -> CK07 (uniform scalar contraction).
  CK07 + the derived scalar PDE -> CK08 (nonnegative coefficients).
  CK06 + CK08 -> CK09 (finite normal recursion and domination).
  CK07 + CK09 + smaller-domain derivative/composition bounds -> CK10.
  normal recurrence + existing analytic series -> CK11.
  CK10 + CK03's zero compatibility defects -> CK12 -> CJ13.

CK03 uses CJ11/CJ14 only for the already-existing analytic defect field.
Those zero-jet and Taylor-identity proofs require no CK existence theorem,
so this dependency is acyclic. CK04 is an optional independent adapter
with a linear ODE compatibility equation; it is not a second required
analytic existence engine.

The convergence proof uses the actual rational/logarithm-series map
  T_(s,z)(w)=C R sum_(n>=1)(s/(R-z-Lw))^n/n,
with uniform contraction factor 1/4 on the displayed polydisc. The finite
coefficient bounds C,R are derived from the given analytic maps. No analytic
IFT, analytic ODE existence, formal-series convergence admission or uniform
Sobolev collar is introduced.

37.5. Full root composition with the exact hypotheses.

  analytic NSE representative
    -> canonical analytic pressure and velocity germ (CG01-CG05);
  analytic scalar normal map + constructive CJ13
    -> normalized actual scalar completion;
  actual NSE conservation + scalar completion + trace reversal
    -> analytic K satisfying initial Hamiltonian and Codazzi;
  actual radial normal map + constructive CJ13
    -> analytic symmetric h with h0=eta, h1=2K and Ric_ab=0;
  GE11 + compact gluing + Lorentzian margin
    -> actual marked analytic vacuum metric germ;
  reconstruction + exact completion gauge fibres + both inverse computations
    -> the original N^omega_(nu,t0) ~= G^omega_(nu,rho,t0).

The analytic trace restriction, arbitrary velocity mean, fixed rho!=0,
positive viscosity, marked torus, pressure gauge and local quantifiers are
preserved at every edge. EqOn/germ/Equiv endpoints package already-proved
identities and supply no analytic existence or geometric proof themselves.

37.6. External cached validation and evidence.

  Compiler alignment: Lean 4.33.0.
  Mathlib: db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d.
  Methlib: 09dfe94a1ef150e21d09df3013fbe09002ef139c.

  elaboration where safe -> deterministic final chain.
  Scheduling cannot change any object, premise, axiom or conclusion.
  Promotion requires matching external compilation, exact root-type checking

into complete proofs. Their formal implementation, all semantic adapters,
and the exact root still lack matching external Lean compilation/audit.
Every retained unresolved premise in a later conditional formalization must
