XI.1. Spaces, distributions, and the four projections.

Let STF_3(C):={A in C^(3x3): A^T=A and tr A=0}, with Hermitian
Frobenius product <A,B>_F=sum_ij A_ij conjugate(B_ij). The product is
linear in its first argument. Real fields have conjugate-symmetric Fourier
coefficients. Spatial measure on T^3 is normalized.

Let H be the real Hilbert space L^2(T^3;STF_3(R)). No mean-zero condition
is imposed on H. For any mean-zero vector distribution a, set

  ||a||_{dot H^-1}^2
    :=sum_(k!=0) |a_hat(k)|^2/(4*pi^2*|k|^2).

The resulting space dot H^-1_0 is defined by finiteness of this sum;
dot H^-1_(sigma,0) is its divergence-free subspace. Denote by G the
mean-zero inverse Laplacian, acting componentwise when necessary.

For F in H define, initially through Fourier multipliers,

  M(F):=<F>,
  f(F):=P div F,
  phi(F):=G div div F,
  Pi_0 F:=the constant field M(F),
  Pi_v F:=Z G f(F),
  Pi_s F:=(3/2) TF Hess G phi(F)
          =(3/2) TF Hess G^2 div div F,
  Pi_T F:=F-Pi_0 F-Pi_v F-Pi_s F.                    (XI.1)

All divergences are distributions. The formulas below prove that f(F)
belongs to dot H^-1_(sigma,0), phi(F) belongs to L^2_0, and every Pi maps
H boundedly into H. Thus these definitions require no pointwise derivative
of an arbitrary L^2 tensor. In the smooth or analytic case they agree with
the usual differential operators.

The subscript T denotes the mean-zero transverse trace-free remainder:
both div(Pi_T F)=0 and <Pi_T F>=0 are derived below. It does not denote a
new spacetime tensor or a quotient of the marked metric sector.

XI.2. Exact nonzero-frequency algebra.

Fix k!=0 and set

  lambda:=2*pi*|k|>0,
  e:=k/|k|,
  P_e:=I-e tensor e,
  A:=F_hat(k),
  alpha:=e^T A e,
  b:=P_e A e,
  Q_e:=e tensor e-I/3.

Then e.b=0, A e=alpha e+b, tr Q_e=0, Q_e e=(2/3)e, and
|Q_e|_F^2=2/3. Define

  V_e(A):=e tensor b+b tensor e,
  C_e(A):=(3/2)alpha Q_e,
  T_e(A):=A-V_e(A)-C_e(A).                             (XI.2)

V_e(A) and C_e(A) are symmetric and trace-free, with

  V_e(A)e=b,
  C_e(A)e=alpha e,
  T_e(A)e=0,
  tr T_e(A)=0.                                        (XI.3)

For every c perpendicular to e and every STF matrix D with D e=0,

  <A,e tensor c+c tensor e>_F=2<b,c>,
  <A,Q_e>_F=alpha,
  <D,Q_e>_F=0,
  <D,e tensor c+c tensor e>_F=0.

The first identity follows by expanding the two finite sums and using
A^T=A. The second uses tr A=0. The remaining identities use D e=0 and
tr D=0. Hence the three summands in (XI.2) are pairwise orthogonal and

  |A|_F^2=2|b|^2+(3/2)|alpha|^2+|T_e(A)|_F^2.         (XI.4)

Each of V_e,C_e,T_e is the orthogonal projection onto its displayed range:

  range V_e={e tensor b+b tensor e: e.b=0},
  range C_e=span_C{Q_e},
  range T_e={D in STF_3(C): D e=0}.

The range dimensions are respectively 2,1,2. For the last dimension choose
a real orthonormal basis with first vector e. A matrix D e=0 has zero first
row and column; its remaining symmetric 2x2 block has trace zero and two
independent complex entries. Thus the nonzero-mode decomposition has
dimensions 2+1+2=5. Reality couples k with -k and is not an additional
independent doubling of physical modes.

At k=0 the entire five-dimensional STF coefficient is Pi_0 F; the symbols
of Pi_v,Pi_s,Pi_T are zero. These zero-mode definitions are essential.

XI.3. Differential symbols and pressure sign.

At k!=0, the Fourier symbols of divergence, gradient and G are respectively
i*lambda contraction with e, i*lambda e, and -lambda^-2. Consequently

  f(F)_hat(k)=i*lambda*b,
  phi(F)_hat(k)=alpha,
  (Pi_v F)_hat(k)=V_e(A),
  (Pi_s F)_hat(k)=C_e(A),
  (Pi_T F)_hat(k)=T_e(A).                              (XI.5)

For the scalar formula, div div F has coefficient -lambda^2 alpha;
one application of G gives alpha, and TF Hess G phi has coefficient
alpha Q_e. Therefore the sign and the factor 3/2 in (XI.1) are fixed by
the chosen Laplacian convention, not selectable normalizations.

Taking one divergence yields the exact distribution identities

  div Pi_v F=f(F),
  div Pi_s F=grad phi(F),
  div Pi_T F=0,
  div F=f(F)+grad phi(F).                             (XI.6)

For example, in physical notation,

  div(TF Hess G phi)
    =grad Delta G phi-(1/3)grad Delta G phi
    =(2/3)grad phi,

which independently verifies the scalar factor. All nonconstant pieces
have zero mean. The four projection symbols are real and even in k, so
the four projections preserve real fields. The scalar extraction phi has
a real even symbol. The symbol of f=P div is imaginary and odd; its
conjugation compatibility also preserves real fields.

XI.4. L^2 decomposition and exact norm identities.

At each nonzero frequency the three projections are mutually annihilating
self-adjoint idempotents; at zero frequency Pi_0 is the identity. Apply
componentwise Parseval, first to finite Fourier sums and then to their L^2
limits. This produces bounded real orthogonal projections on H satisfying

  Pi_a Pi_b=0 for a!=b,
  Pi_a^2=Pi_a,
  Pi_0+Pi_v+Pi_s+Pi_T=I,
  ||Pi_a F||_2<=||F||_2.                              (XI.7)

The four components are pairwise orthogonal, and

  ||F||_2^2
    =|<F>|_F^2+||Pi_v F||_2^2+||Pi_s F||_2^2+||Pi_T F||_2^2
    =|<F>|_F^2+2||f(F)||_{dot H^-1}^2
       +(3/2)||phi(F)||_2^2+||Pi_T F||_2^2.             (XI.8)

The first finite-dimensional identity in (XI.4) simultaneously proves
the required boundedness of f(F) and phi(F), before taking the L^2 limit.
Distributional multiplier identities pass coefficientwise to the limit.
No derivative bound in L^2 is inferred from F in L^2 alone.

For the complete divergence,

  ||div F||_{dot H^-1}^2
    =||f(F)||_{dot H^-1}^2+||phi(F)||_2^2
    =(1/2)||Pi_v F||_2^2+(2/3)||Pi_s F||_2^2.          (XI.9)

The first equality also expresses the orthogonality of the solenoidal and
longitudinal parts of div F in dot H^-1_0. In particular,

  ||P div F||_{dot H^-1}^2 <=(1/2)||F||_2^2,
  ||G div div F||_2^2 <=(2/3)||F||_2^2,
  ||div F||_{dot H^-1}^2 <=(2/3)||F||_2^2.             (XI.10)

The constants are sharp in the full real STF tensor space. The first is
attained by any nonzero Pi_v-range tensor. The latter two are attained by
any nonzero Pi_s-range tensor. Explicit single-frequency witnesses are

  V(x)=cos(2*pi*x_1)*(e_1 tensor e_2+e_2 tensor e_1),
  C(x)=cos(2*pi*x_1)*diag(1,-1/2,-1/2),
  T(x)=cos(2*pi*x_1)*diag(0,1,-1),
  M(x)=diag(1,-1,0).                                  (XI.11)

These belong respectively to the four projection ranges. They also show
that each projection has operator norm exactly one. The equality statement
for the full-divergence bound requires Pi_0 F=Pi_v F=Pi_T F=0; the scalar
range is the only nonzero equality range. Sharpness here is an algebraic
tensor-space statement. The later VF10 proof in Section 46 realizes
these analytic witnesses as initial constitutive tensors of actual
normalized ambient vacuum metrics. That extension uses its exact
compatible construction and does not change canonical admission.

XI.5. Full inverse parametrization and exact fibres.

Let M be a constant real STF matrix, f any real member of
dot H^-1_(sigma,0), phi any real member of L^2_0, and T any member of
H with <T>=0 and div T=0. Define

  S(f):=Z G f,
  C(phi):=(3/2)TF Hess G phi,
  F[M,f,phi,T]:=M+S(f)+C(phi)+T.                       (XI.12)

G f is a mean-zero H^1 vector field and G phi a mean-zero H^2 scalar,
as follows immediately from their Fourier norms. Thus both derivatives in
(XI.12) define L^2 tensors. S(f) is STF because div f=0; C(phi) is STF
by construction. The direct symbol calculations give

  <F[M,f,phi,T]>=M,
  P div F[M,f,phi,T]=f,
  G div div F[M,f,phi,T]=phi,
  Pi_T F[M,f,phi,T]=T.                                (XI.13)

Conversely (XI.1) reconstructs every F in H from these four data. Therefore
F -> (<F>,f(F),phi(F),Pi_T F) is a bijective real linear map onto the
displayed product space. It is an isometry when that product carries norm
squared |M|_F^2+2||f||_{dot H^-1}^2+(3/2)||phi||_2^2+||T||_2^2.

Consequences, with equality in the distribution sense where required:

  ker(P div)=range Pi_0 direct_sum range Pi_s direct_sum range Pi_T,
  ker(div)=range Pi_0 direct_sum range Pi_T,
  ker(G div div)=range Pi_0 direct_sum range Pi_v direct_sum range Pi_T.
                                                               (XI.14)

The sums are orthogonal. In particular, the kernel of P div has three
nonzero-mode STF dimensions: one scalar-pressure direction and two
divergence-free tensor directions. The constant mode is an additional
independent five-dimensional summand.

XI.6. Minimum-norm tensor for a complete prescribed divergence.

Let g be any real mean-zero member of dot H^-1_0. Set

  f_g:=P g,
  phi_g:=G div g,
  F_min(g):=S(f_g)+C(phi_g).                            (XI.15)

At k!=0 write g_hat(k)=P_e g_hat(k)+e(e.g_hat(k)). The quantity
phi_g_hat(k)=-(i/lambda)e.g_hat(k) belongs to l^2, and
grad phi_g has coefficient e(e.g_hat(k)). Thus

  g=f_g+grad phi_g,
  div F_min(g)=g.

Every A in H with div A=g has the unique form

  A=F_min(g)+M+T,

with M constant STF and T mean-zero STF with div T=0. Orthogonality gives

  ||A||_2^2
    =2||P g||_{dot H^-1}^2+(3/2)||G div g||_2^2
       +|M|_F^2+||T||_2^2.                            (XI.16)

Hence F_min(g) is the unique minimum-norm STF tensor with divergence g,
and the minimum squared norm is the first two terms in (XI.16). This is
an explicit inverse and quadratic identity. No minimizer-existence axiom
or abstract PDE solvability premise is needed. In particular, divergence
maps H onto the full mean-zero vector space dot H^-1_0.

The analogous projected-force minimizer remains S(f), as in ST06.
Prescribing complete divergence fixes both the Pi_v and Pi_s components;
prescribing only projected divergence fixes only Pi_v.

Let H_T:={T in H: <T>=0 and div T=0}. The same orthogonal decomposition
also supplies actual attained distances, with the L^2 metric:

  dist(F,ker(P div))^2=2||f(F)||_{dot H^-1}^2,
  dist(F,ker(div))^2
    =2||f(F)||_{dot H^-1}^2+(3/2)||phi(F)||_2^2,
  dist(F,H_T)^2
    =|<F>|_F^2+2||f(F)||_{dot H^-1}^2+(3/2)||phi(F)||_2^2.
                                                               (XI.16a)

The unique nearest tensors are respectively F-Pi_v F, Pi_0 F+Pi_T F,
and Pi_T F. For example, every H in ker(div) differs from Pi_0 F+Pi_T F
by a vector in that kernel, orthogonal to Pi_v F+Pi_s F. Expanding its
squared distance proves the second formula and uniqueness. The other two
follow by the identical finite orthogonal expansion. The distance to the
canonical stress set {0} is exactly ||F||_2, with the four terms (XI.8).
These are tensor-space distances; no distance or nearest-point theorem
for constrained vacuum metric germs is asserted.

XI.7. Exact pressure-stress fibres at a fixed velocity.

Let u be a fixed smooth periodic vector field and let (p,F),(p',F') be
smooth periodic pressure/STF-stress pairs. Put delta F=F'-F. Then

  R_nu(u,p')-div F'=R_nu(u,p)-div F
    <=> grad(p'-p)=div delta F
    <=> there are unique M,phi,T and a spatially constant scalar a with
          delta F=M+C(phi)+T,
          p'-p=phi+a,
          <phi>=0, <T>=0, div T=0.                   (XI.17)

Here M=<delta F>, phi=G div div(delta F), T=Pi_T(delta F), and
a=<p'-p>. Necessity follows by applying P to the gradient identity,
then applying the unique decomposition (XI.12), and finally using the
periodic gradient kernel. Sufficiency follows from div C(phi)=grad phi,
div M=div T=0. No NSE existence premise is used.

For time-dependent smooth or jointly analytic fields this identity is
applied at every t on the common interval. The bounded multiplier maps
preserve the corresponding joint analytic germs, so M,phi,T,a have the
same time regularity. This is a fibre classification of the auxiliary
pressure/STF-stress equation at fixed u. It does not enlarge the original
NSE pressure gauge: when F=F'=0, the identity reduces to p'-p=a(t).

XI.8. Three exact boundary admission predicates.

For an actual smooth normalized vacuum boundary in the original marked
scope, put F=F_nu[K], v=P w, and use the already-derived identity
R_nu(v,p_*)=div F. Define phi=G div div F and T=Pi_T F. Then

  F=0
    <=> <F>=0, f(F)=0, phi=0, T=0;                    (XI.18)

  R_nu(v,p_*)=0
    <=> div F=0
    <=> f(F)=0 and phi=0;                             (XI.19)

  exists p with NSE_nu(v,p)
    <=> f(F)=0.                                      (XI.20)

The divergence-free condition on v is already part of the reconstruction.
The pressure realizing (XI.20) is explicitly

  p_corr=p_*-phi,
  R_nu(v,p_corr)=f(F).                                (XI.21)

For (XI.19), apply the orthogonal negative-Sobolev identity (XI.9);
or apply P and G div to the equation div F=0. Since <phi>=0,
grad phi=0 implies phi=0. For analytic fields, the L^2 or Fourier
identities imply the actual pointwise identities by continuity/Fourier
injectivity; the germ is then restricted to a common interval as usual.

Thus Pi_s is precisely the pressure-changing part of the constitutive
residual; Pi_0 and Pi_T are invisible even to its full divergence; Pi_v
is precisely its projected forcing part. The canonical sector continues
to require all four components to vanish. None of the weaker predicates
replaces G4. The abstract witnesses (XI.11) separate the tensor predicates;
they do not establish realizability by distinct vacuum metric germs.

XI.9. Analytic Fourier preservation and sharp weighted bounds.

Use the weighted Fourier norm from Section 27,

  ||F||_sigma=sum_k |F_hat(k)|_F exp(2*pi*sigma*|k|_1),

and, for a mean-zero vector distribution a, define

  ||a||_{W_sigma^-1}
    :=sum_(k!=0) lambda^-1 |a_hat(k)| exp(2*pi*sigma*|k|_1).

All four Pi symbols are pointwise orthogonal projections; hence for
each sigma>=0 and F with finite displayed norm,

  ||Pi_a F||_sigma<=||F||_sigma.                       (XI.22)

For a=v,s,T the stronger right side ||Q0 F||_sigma is valid, because
their zero mode vanishes. The exact coefficientwise equalities give

  ||Pi_v F||_sigma=sqrt(2)||f(F)||_{W_sigma^-1},
  ||Pi_s F||_sigma=sqrt(3/2)||phi(F)||_sigma.           (XI.23)

Consequently,

  ||f(F)||_{W_sigma^-1}<=1/sqrt(2)||Q0 F||_sigma,
  ||phi(F)||_sigma<=sqrt(2/3)||Q0 F||_sigma,
  ||div F||_{W_sigma^-1}<=sqrt(2/3)||Q0 F||_sigma.      (XI.24)

The constants are sharp by the same real single-frequency tensors as in
(XI.11). These are weighted l^1 estimates; no squared Pythagorean identity
for the global W_sigma norm is asserted. Orthogonality holds coefficient
by coefficient and for Hilbert Fourier norms, not for an l^1 sum after
arbitrary coefficients have been summed.

Every Pi and phi is a bounded linear multiplier on the relevant W_sigma
space. Their symbols are time-independent and commute with Fourier
truncation, torus translations, and time differentiation when the latter
is defined. AF09 supplies the actual locally uniform differentiated
synthesis. ML26/ML27 may then transfer Banach-valued analyticity. Thus a
jointly analytic periodic tensor germ has jointly analytic components on
each common smaller domain supplied by AF01-AF09. No spatial derivative
is claimed bounded W_sigma -> W_sigma unless its negative weight in
(XI.23)-(XI.24) or the radius loss of AF06 is retained.

XI.10. Atomic proof contracts and source placement.

HD01 STF_MODE_SPLITTING
  INPUT: e in R^3, |e|=1, A in STF_3(C).
  OUTPUT: (XI.2)-(XI.4), including exact scalar factor, orthogonality,
          projection ranges, and dimensions 2+1+2.
  PROOF: XI.2 finite sums and a real orthonormal basis of e-perp.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML17-ML19 for matrix traces; ME01-ME03 for the
                  already-pinned vector Leray algebra; ME20 gives literal
                  complex linearity and vector reality compatibility.
  NEW_BODY: The STF Hermitian matrix splitting and scalar/TT projectors.

HD02 EXACT_FOUR_COMPONENT_SYMBOLS
  INPUT: smooth periodic real STF tensor F; G,P,Z and differential
         conventions exactly as in Sections 0 and 27.
  OUTPUT: (XI.5), zero modes, conjugate symmetry, (XI.6).
  PROOF: XI.3 direct multipliers; vector symbol already ST02.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML01-ML06; ME01-ME05,ME16,ME20.
  NEW_BODY: Scalar-pressure symbol, zero-mode conventions and TT symbol.

HD03 L2_ORTHOGONAL_FOUR_COMPONENT_EXTENSION
  INPUT: F in the actual real Hilbert space H.
  OUTPUT: bounded orthogonal projectors (XI.7), identities (XI.8)-(XI.9),
          exact distributional divergence and extraction domains.
  PROOF: XI.4 finite Fourier sums, Parseval, and coefficientwise limits.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML03,ML05; ME04-ME06; AF02/AF04; ST04/ST05.
  NEW_BODY: Real STF L^2 types, full mean/scalar/TT extension and limits.

HD04 SHARP_FULL_DIVERGENCE_AND_PRESSURE_BOUNDS
  INPUT: F in H.
  OUTPUT: (XI.10), all equality cases claimed in XI.4, witnesses (XI.11).
  PROOF: XI.8-XI.9 coefficients and explicit real modes.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML05; finite inequalities; HD01-HD03.
  NEW_BODY: Sharp constants 2/3 and the exact scalar equality range.

HD05 INVERTIBLE_STRESS_DATA_PARAMETRIZATION
  INPUT: M STF constant; f in dot H^-1_(sigma,0); phi in real L^2_0;
         T in H with zero mean and zero divergence.
  OUTPUT: (XI.12)-(XI.14), both inverse laws and weighted Hilbert isometry.
  PROOF: XI.5 Fourier inverse formulas, HD02-HD03.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML03,ML05,ML24-ML25 if packaging the equivalence;
                  ME04-ME06,ME16; exact type adapters remain required.
  NEW_BODY: The product-space inverse map and all component kernels.

HD06 MINIMUM_STF_STRESS_FOR_COMPLETE_DIVERGENCE
  INPUT: any real g in dot H^-1_0.
  OUTPUT: explicit F_min(g), div F_min=g, full affine fibre, unique
          minimum norm and exact minimum value (XI.15)-(XI.16); the
          attained orthogonal distances and minimizers (XI.16a).
  PROOF: XI.6; HD05 and orthogonality.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ME01-ME05,ME16; ML05; ST06 as the projected subcase.
  NEW_BODY: Complete-divergence rather than projected-force inverse.

HD07 EXACT_PRESSURE_STRESS_PAIR_FIBRES
  INPUT: fixed smooth periodic u; two smooth real pressure/STF-stress pairs.
  OUTPUT: the full iff statement (XI.17) with unique M,phi,T,a.
  PROOF: XI.7; HD05 and the periodic gradient kernel.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ME06,ME16; CG01-CG03; NP02/NP04.
  NEW_BODY: Auxiliary stress-pair fibre; original pressure gauge unchanged.

HD08 THREE_BOUNDARY_ADMISSION_LEVELS
  INPUT: an actual normalized vacuum boundary satisfying the original
         regularity and marking hypotheses; its actual F_nu[K],v,p_*.
  OUTPUT: (XI.18)-(XI.21), with G4 preserved as full F=0.
  PROOF: XI.8; the actual residual identity from NP04; HD03-HD05.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ME06-ME10,ME16; ST07; NP02/NP04/NP11.
  NEW_BODY: Exact scalar and TT obligations separating the three predicates.

HD09 WEIGHTED_ANALYTIC_PROJECTION_BOUNDS
  INPUT: actual W_sigma STF Fourier data with sigma>=0.
  OUTPUT: (XI.22)-(XI.24), reality and sharpness, no false l^1 Pythagoras.
  PROOF: XI.9 coefficientwise projection bounds and exact symbol norms.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML01-ML06; ME01-ME05,ME16; AF02-AF08.
  NEW_BODY: W_sigma^-1 type and scalar/TT continuous linear maps.
  SCOPE_NOTE: ME17 polynomial Sobolev multipliers are not an exponential
              l^1 endpoint; no replacement of AF02-AF08 is permitted.

HD10 ANALYTIC_GERM_COMPONENT_REALIZATION
  INPUT: a jointly analytic periodic STF tensor germ on the common domain
         of the original bridge, with actual Fourier synthesis adapters.
  OUTPUT: jointly analytic real M,Pi_v F,Pi_s F,Pi_T F,phi; time-derivative
          commutation on smaller common domains; pointwise identities from
          the coefficient and L^2 identities.
  PROOF: XI.9 plus AF01-AF09, bounded multipliers and real descent.
  SOURCE_SUPPORT: ML26-ML28; ME06,ME18,ME20 with their actual hypotheses.
  NEW_BODY: Analytic time/space adapters. ME18 proves smooth realization;
            it does not independently establish joint analyticity.

DEPENDENCY_SPINE:
  HD01 -> HD02 -> HD03 -> HD04;
  HD01-HD03 -> HD05 -> HD06;
  HD05 + exact residual/gradient semantics -> HD07 -> HD08;
  HD01-HD02 + AF02-AF08 -> HD09;

ROOT_EFFECT:
  The original canonical admission F_nu[K]=0 and the exact NSE<->GR maps
  are unchanged. HD08 refines the canonical constitutive predicate into
  four independently specified vanishing bodies. HD07 identifies precisely
  which stress components alter pressure and which are invisible to the
  full divergence. The new diagnostic branch adds no root premise.

=======================================================================
40. ROUND XII: FINITE CK CERTIFICATES AND QUANTITATIVE JET CONTROL
=======================================================================

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP40
  V16_REFINEMENT = SF05; separate observed time-frequency approximation certificate.
  V16_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML41-ML42,ML55-ML58.
  V16_LOOKUP = Sections 51-55; exact source premises and target adapters retained.
  V15_REFINEMENT = VF10; actual vacuum counterexamples to fixed finite-jet canonical admission.
  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ME21-ME22.
  V15_LOOKUP = Sections 45-49; all exact source hypotheses retained.
  SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML43-ML48; ML07-ML16, ML28-ML29, ML31-ML33, ML41-ML42.
  REQUIRED_BRIDGE_BODIES = FC01-FC10 below; CK05-CK12 remain the actual
    existence and uniqueness proofs, not assumptions replaced by a checker.
  LOOKUP = Section 42 for exact new source signatures; Sections 23,31,36
    for previous source signatures; Section 43 for deterministic assembly.
  EVIDENCE = source-verified support; target applications not compiled.

This round derives finite certificates for the actual analytic solutions
constructed in Section 35. Finite residual tests identify a specified
finite jet. Convergent-tail bounds then quantify approximation on a stated
smaller domain. A finite jet never asserts equality of entire analytic germs.

=======================================================================
XII.A. CERTIFIED SCALAR CONTRACTION AND FINITE RATIONAL ITERATION
=======================================================================

A1. Exact objects and metric restriction.

Retain CK.B6--CK.B9:

  C,R>0, L>=1, W=R/(4L), M=W/2,
  zeta=R/4, tau=min(R/8,R/(32CL)),
  D={a in C: |a|<=W},
  q(a)=R-z-La,
  T_(s,z)(a)=CR sum_(j>=1)(s/q(a))^j/j,
  |s|<=tau, |z|<=zeta.

Then D is a nonempty complete metric space with its induced distance,
T maps D into {|a|<=M}, and T is (1/4)-Lipschitz on D. The actual fixed
point w(s,z) constructed in Section 35 belongs to {|a|<=M}.
No Lipschitz assertion on the whole complex plane is required.

A2. Exact residual and perturbation certificates.

For every a in D,

  |a-w|<=|a-T(a)|+|T(a)-T(w)|
       <=|a-T(a)|+(1/4)|a-w|.

Rearrangement gives the a posteriori estimate

  |a-w| <= (4/3)|a-T(a)|.                            (FC.A1)

It follows that a D-valued exact fixed point is unique. If a second
self-map Ttilde:D->D has an actual fixed point wtilde in D and
sup_D |T-Ttilde|<=epsilon, then

  |w-wtilde| <= (4/3)epsilon.                        (FC.A2)

Indeed, insert a=wtilde into (FC.A1) and use Ttilde(wtilde)=wtilde.
Existence of wtilde is a separate proved premise. The bound does not
imply stability of arbitrary nonlinear PDE coefficients or of GR germs
in a Sobolev topology. ML43--ML47 have precisely these restricted metric
roles; their complete source signatures are indexed in Section 42.

The inequalities are pointwise in (s,z). Uniform residual bounds yield
uniform value bounds by taking suprema. Derivative bounds additionally
require actual holomorphy and a strict parameter-domain margin.

A3. A finite rational map with a proved map-error bound.

For integers J>=1, set

  T^[J]_(s,z)(a)=CR sum_(j=1)^J (s/(R-z-La))^j/j.

This is a finite rational expression. Write alpha=2tau/R, so
0<alpha<=1/4. On the entire parameter polydisc and D,

  |s/q(a)|<=alpha,
  |T(a)-T^[J](a)| <= epsilon_J,
  epsilon_J=CR*alpha^(J+1)/((J+1)(1-alpha)).           (FC.A3)

Proof. The omitted j-th term has norm at most CR alpha^j/j. For j>=J+1,
1/j<=1/(J+1), and the positive geometric tail equals
alpha^(J+1)/(1-alpha). ML48 supplies the exact geometric sum; absolute
convergence justifies the comparison. The rational denominator is bounded
away from zero by |q(a)|>=R/2.

The same positive-term majorants used for T prove

  |T^[J](a)|<=4C|s|<=M,
  |partial_a T^[J](a)|
    <= CRL |s|/(|q(a)|(|q(a)|-|s|))
    <= 8CLtau/R <= 1/4.                              (FC.A4)

The last bound uses |q(a)|>=R/2 and |q(a)|-|s|>=R/4.
Convex-segment integration therefore makes T^[J]:D->D a contraction.
Its fixed point w^[J] exists and is unique; (FC.A2)--(FC.A3) give

  |w-w^[J]| <= (4/3)epsilon_J.                       (FC.A5)

For finite rational iterates a_0=0, a_(n+1)=T^[J](a_n),

  |a_(n+1)-a_n|<=M*4^(-n),
  |a_N-w^[J]|<=(4M/3)*4^(-N),
  |a_N-w|<=(4M/3)*4^(-N)+(4/3)epsilon_J.             (FC.A6)

The first inequality is an induction from |a_1|<=M. Summing its positive
geometric tail proves the second; (FC.A5) proves the third. Every iterate
is an actual rational function with a certified nonvanishing denominator.
This supplies a finite evaluation certificate for the scalar majorant.
It is not a numerical execution or a certificate that a_N solves CK.B5.

=======================================================================
XII.B. ABSOLUTE COEFFICIENT BUDGETS AND SHARP GEOMETRIC TAILS
=======================================================================

B1. Boundary coefficient budget.

Write the scalar majorant as

  w(s,z)=sum_(n,k>=0) c_(n,k)s^n z^k,
  c_(n,k)>=0, c_(0,k)=0.

For 0<r<1, the convergent series at (r*tau,r*zeta) has nonnegative
terms and sum at most M. For each finite set of pairs (n,k), let r
increase to 1 in the corresponding finite subsum. Its limiting value
is at most M. Taking the supremum over finite subsets gives

  sum_(n,k>=0) c_(n,k)tau^n zeta^k <= M.             (FC.B1)

This finite-subsum proof requires no unproved boundary analyticity.

Let v_a(s,y)=sum_(n>=1,beta) v_(a,n,beta)s^n y^beta be any component
of the zero-data shifted quasilinear solution, with y in C^d. CK.B13 is
the coefficient bound

  |v_(a,n,beta)|
       <= c_(n,|beta|) * |beta|!/(beta_1!...beta_d!). (FC.B2)

For nonnegative r_i with sum_i r_i<=zeta, the multinomial identity and
(FC.B1) imply

  sum_(n,beta) |v_(a,n,beta)|tau^n product_i r_i^beta_i <= M. (FC.B3)

In particular, every finite Taylor partial sum has absolute value at
most M on |s|<=tau, sum_i|y_i|<=zeta. These component bounds use the
max norm on C^m; no dimension factor is hidden in M.

B2. Total-degree and rectangular truncations.

Let P_Nv be the total Taylor polynomial retaining n+|beta|<=N. On

  D_theta={|s|<=theta*tau, sum_i|y_i|<=theta*zeta},
  0<=theta<1,

each omitted monomial receives a factor at most theta^(N+1). Hence

  ||v-P_Nv||_(infinity,D_theta) <= M theta^(N+1).    (FC.B4)

For theta=0 the omitted terms vanish at the origin, so the same formula
holds. This estimate uses one absolute coefficient budget, rather than
an extra geometric-series factor for each coordinate.

More generally let P_(N,Q)v retain n<=N and |beta|<=Q. On
|s|<=theta_s*tau, sum_i|y_i|<=theta_y*zeta, with 0<=theta_s,theta_y<1,

  ||v-P_(N,Q)v||_infinity
       <= M max(theta_s^(N+1),theta_y^(Q+1)).         (FC.B5)

Every excluded term satisfies at least one of the two strict degree
bounds. Its factor theta_s^n theta_y^|beta| is bounded by the displayed
maximum. Summing the excluded part of (FC.B3) proves (FC.B5), with no
double counting. A normal-degree-only truncation has error at most
M theta_s^(N+1), even with untruncated tangential coefficients.

B3. Differentiated tails with explicit margins.

Fix 0<=theta<eta<1, an integer r>=0, and a tangential multi-index beta.
At any point of D_theta, the coordinate polydisc with radii

  delta_s=(eta-theta)tau,
  delta_y=(eta-theta)zeta/d

lies in D_eta. Iterating the one-variable Cauchy derivative formula for
the actual holomorphic function v-P_Nv gives

  ||partial_s^r partial_y^beta(v-P_Nv)||_(infinity,D_theta)
   <= r! beta! M eta^(N+1)
          / [((eta-theta)tau)^r
             ((eta-theta)zeta/d)^|beta|].             (FC.B6)

Here beta!=product_i beta_i!. The enclosing coordinate circles lie
strictly inside the existence domain because eta<1. ML41--ML42 provide
the scalar circle interfaces; joint holomorphy and the coordinate
iteration are the explicit CK/FC adapters.

For a single derivative in one tangential coordinate, no d loss is
necessary: vary only that coordinate, using radius (eta-theta)zeta.
Thus one may take

  epsilon_0=M theta^(N+1),
  epsilon_s=M eta^(N+1)/((eta-theta)tau),
  epsilon_i=M eta^(N+1)/((eta-theta)zeta)              (FC.B7)

as uniform errors for the value, normal derivative and each individual
tangential derivative. For fixed theta<eta<1 and any fixed derivative
order, these errors tend geometrically to zero as N tends to infinity.

=======================================================================
XII.C. FINITE RESIDUAL CHECKERS FOR THE ACTUAL TAYLOR JET
=======================================================================

C1. Polynomial jet notation and quasilinear residual.

For an analytic vector function f near the origin, j^N f denotes its
ordinary Taylor polynomial of total degree at most N, with factorials
included in the coefficient convention. All comparisons of j^N objects
are finite coefficient equalities. Write

  R(P)=P_s-sum_i Atilde^i(s,y,P)partial_i P
                       -Btilde(s,y,P).

For N>=1 and any polynomial P(s,y) of total degree<=N with P(0,y)=0,

  j^(N-1)R(P)=0  <=>  P=j^N v.                     (FC.C1)

The coefficient maps are evaluated near (0,0,0), where they are analytic;
P(0,0)=0 ensures a neighborhood on which the residual is defined.

Proof, forward direction. Expand P=sum_(n=0)^N p_n(y)s^n, with p_0=0
and degree(p_n)<=N-n. The coefficient of s^n y^beta in R(P), for
n+|beta|<=N-1, is

  (n+1)[y^beta]p_(n+1)
    -[s^n y^beta](sum_i Atilde^i(s,y,P)partial_i P+Btilde(s,y,P)).

The second term uses only normal coefficients p_0,...,p_n. A tangential
derivative can request one higher y degree, but its requested total
degree in P is still at most N. Induct first on n, comparing all allowed
tangential degrees. The zero-residual equations force p_(n+1) to equal
the corresponding exact solution coefficients from CK.B12. This proves
P=j^N v.

Reverse direction. The error v-P has total order at least N+1. Its first
derivatives have order at least N. Analytic composition gives
Atilde(s,y,v)-Atilde(s,y,P) and Btilde(s,y,v)-Btilde(s,y,P) order at
least N+1, by expanding in the state variables or by the segment integral
of the actual analytic state derivative. Subtracting R(v)=0 leaves
R(P) of order at least N. Therefore j^(N-1)R(P)=0.

All the coefficient equalities in (FC.C1) are finite. For an index of
total degree at most N-1, coefficient multiplication and substitution
request only finitely many coefficients of Atilde, Btilde and P. The
normal induction decreases n even when a tangential derivative preserves
the total degree. Thus the certificate has an acyclic finite dependency
order; there is no circular unknown coefficient on its right side.

C2. A proved finite jet plus a tail bound.

If the left side of (FC.C1) and the displayed polynomial/initial-data
conditions have been proved, then every value and derivative estimate
(FC.B4)--(FC.B7) applies to that P. This composes a finite exact residual
certificate with the constructive analytic existence theorem and its
derived constants. The certificate does not replace the existence proof.
Unverified output from a future coefficient checker would remain an
unresolved checker premise, not an A-tier theorem body.

C3. A quantitative residual bound on a smaller domain.

For P=j^N v, put e=v-P. For each coefficient matrix let the norm be its
operator norm induced by the vector max norm. Suppose on the relevant
coefficient domain

  ||Atilde^i(s,y,P)||_op<=A_i,
  ||Atilde^i(s,y,v)-Atilde^i(s,y,P)||_op<=L_i||e||_infinity,
  ||Btilde(s,y,v)-Btilde(s,y,P)||_infinity<=L_B||e||_infinity,
  ||partial_i v||_infinity<=D_i.

The exact residual difference is

  R(P)=-e_s+sum_i Atilde^i(P)partial_i e
            +sum_i(Atilde^i(v)-Atilde^i(P))partial_i v
            +Btilde(v)-Btilde(P).

Consequently

  ||R(P)||_(infinity,D_theta)
       <= epsilon_s+sum_i A_i epsilon_i
                      +(L_B+sum_i L_i D_i)epsilon_0. (FC.C2)

All constants can be taken directly from CK06, rather than assumed.
Each shifted coefficient entry Atilde^i_ab or Btilde_a has norm at most C on its closed
coordinate polydisc of radius R. By (FC.B3), both v and P have each
state coordinate bounded by M=R/(8L)<=R/8; their segment does also.
A state-coordinate Cauchy circle of radius R/2 around any segment point
stays strictly in the original coefficient domain. Hence each state
partial derivative has norm at most 2C/R. For m state coordinates,

  A_i=mC, L_i=2m^2 C/R, L_B=2mC/R                   (FC.C3)

are valid bounds in the indicated max/operator norms. A one-coordinate
Cauchy estimate on v, followed by a limit from smaller circles, gives

  D_i=M/((1-theta)zeta).                             (FC.C4)

Together, (FC.B7) and (FC.C2)--(FC.C4) give a completely explicit finite
polynomial residual bound. No converse claim from a small residual alone
is made. The estimate follows from the proved coefficient certificate
and the actual CK solution.

=======================================================================
XII.D. SECOND-ORDER JET LOCALITY AND DIRECT SCALAR/RADIAL CERTIFICATES
=======================================================================

D1. Finite dependency of a CK2 normal jet.

Let U solve the actual second-order normal equation

  U_ss=F(s,y,U,U_s,U_y,U_yy,U_sy),
  U(0,y)=a(y), U_s(0,y)=b(y),                        (FC.D1)

with the same analytic open-domain hypotheses as CJ13. Denote
H_k(y)=partial_s^k U(0,y). For k>=2 and a tangential multi-index beta,
the value partial_y^beta H_k(0) is determined by the following finite data:

  j_y^(k+|beta|) a,
  j_y^(k-1+|beta|) b,
  all partial derivatives of F of order<=k-2+|beta|
    at the common initial point (0,0,a,b,Da,D^2a,Db). (FC.D2)

H_0 and H_1 use only the corresponding derivatives of a and b. For a
fixed normal map F, the third item is fixed once its initial point is
fixed. These are sufficient bounds; special equations may use fewer jets.

Proof. H_2=F(0,y,a,b,Da,D^2a,Db), so ordinary repeated chain/product
rules give the claim for k=2 and every beta. For k>2, differentiate
(FC.D1) k-2 times in s. Each term is a finite product of an F derivative
of order at most k-2 and normal derivatives of its reduced-jet entries.
Such entries have the form partial_y^gamma H_j with
j<k and j+|gamma|<=k. After |beta| additional tangential derivatives,
each requested lower normal jet has j+|gamma|<=k+|beta|. Induction on
the normal index j, applied for all tangential orders, bounds its a and
b requests by k+|beta| and k-1+|beta|. Derivatives of the displayed F
coefficients contribute order at most k-2+|beta|. Finite Leibniz and
chain-rule partitions preserve these inequalities. This proves (FC.D2).

Thus for N>=2, identical a jets through N, b jets through N-1, and F
jets through N-2 at the identical initial jet imply identical mixed U
jets of total degree<=N. The conclusion is a finite local jet statement,
not an equality of analytic germs from finitely many derivatives.

D2. Exact finite certificate in the original second-order variables.

Let P be an N-degree polynomial in (s,y), N>=2, satisfying

  j_y^N P(0,y)=j_y^N a(y),
  j_y^(N-1)P_s(0,y)=j_y^(N-1)b(y).                  (FC.D3)

Then its initial reduced jet equals that of the exact solution, so the
analytic expression F(s,y,J^-_2P) is defined near the origin. One has

  j^(N-2)(P_ss-F(s,y,J^-_2P))=0
       <=> P=j^N U.                                 (FC.D4)

Proof. For each normal n=0,...,N-2 and tangential degree |beta|<=N-2-n,
the residual coefficient determines (n+2)(n+1)[s^(n+2)y^beta]P. Its
right side involves normal indices at most n+1. Tangential derivatives
request total polynomial degrees at most N. Starting from (FC.D3),
normal induction gives the unique coefficients of U. Conversely, an
error of total order N+1 has all reduced second-jet errors of order at
least N-1. Composition with the analytic F preserves that order, while
the P_ss error also has order at least N-1. Hence the residual vanishes
through total degree N-2.

The inverse of the positive integer (n+2)(n+1) exists in R and C. The
finite coefficient identities therefore have no division-by-zero branch.
This certificate works directly with 1 scalar or 10 symmetric metric
components; it does not require storing every prolonged variable in the
finite polynomial checker.

D3. Finite transfer through the actual matrix inverse and curvature.

Let h and htilde be analytic matrix-valued functions with the same
invertible value at the base point. If j^N h=j^N htilde, then

  j^N(h^(-1))=j^N(htilde^(-1)).                       (FC.D5)

One proof is the exact identity

  h^(-1)-htilde^(-1)=h^(-1)(htilde-h)htilde^(-1).

Both inverses are analytic on a smaller neighborhood; the middle factor
has total order N+1, so the product has that order. For a finite direct
coefficient computation, writing h=sum_alpha H_alpha z^alpha and
h^(-1)=sum_alpha J_alpha z^alpha gives

  J_0=H_0^(-1),
  J_alpha=-H_0^(-1) sum_(0<beta<=alpha) H_beta J_(alpha-beta),
  alpha!=0.                                         (FC.D6)

Only strictly lower total inverse degrees occur. Matrix multiplication
keeps the displayed order; no commutative matrix simplification is used.
ML31--ML33 supply the actual adjugate/determinant inverse identities after
invertibility has been proved. They do not supply finite-jet transfer alone.

For N>=2, equality of metric jets through N implies equality of the
associated coordinate Ricci jets through N-2. The Christoffel formula
uses the inverse and one metric derivative; the Ricci formula uses one
Christoffel derivative and a quadratic Christoffel product. Product and
derivative jet transfer together with (FC.D5) therefore prove the claim.
The statement applies to the actual Gaussian metric diag(1,h), with the
curvature convention already derived in Section 34.

D4. Exact placement in the two completion engines.

Scalar engine: s=t-t0, d=3, U=psi, a=b=0 and F=Fsc. The initial
coefficient 2rho is nonzero, so the scalar rational map is analytic on
the same open jet neighborhood used by CJ03. A polynomial certificate
(FC.D3)--(FC.D4) proves exactly j^N psi. The required finite Fsc jet
contains finite jets of the actual analytic source fields u,p; obtaining
canonical p=G divdiv(-u tensor u) remains a full-torus operation.
Finite pointwise velocity jets alone do not determine that pressure.

Radial engine: s=r, d=4, U=h in its 10 symmetric coordinates,
a=eta, b=2K and F=F_G. A radial polynomial P here consists of 10
symmetric real coefficient polynomials, hence is real on real arguments.
For a given N>=2, the input consists of the
specified finite tangential K jet through N-1 and the finite source
coefficient identities of F_G at eta. The constant value eta is
invertible, so every finite polynomial with these Cauchy data defines
a local Lorentzian Gaussian metric after a neighborhood restriction.
Equations (FC.D3)--(FC.D4) identify its entire metric jet through N with
the already-constructed radial solution.

If K is the actual completed boundary datum of the root construction,
the exact radial solution is vacuum by GE11. Consequently a polynomial
P satisfying this radial certificate obeys

  j^(N-2) Ric(dr^2+P_ab dy^a dy^b)=0                 (FC.D7)

at the base point, by D3. This is a certified finite vacuum jet. It is
not a claim that the polynomial metric has identically zero Ricci or
belongs to the complete geometric sector on a collar.

The constants and remainder bounds of XII.B apply to the reduced
quasilinear solution and hence to every selected original U component
after its known analytic initial-data shift. To use a direct original
U Taylor polynomial, its known initial-data Taylor remainder must also
be included; it is bounded by that initial datum's own analytic Cauchy
budget. This additional remainder is zero for the scalar psi data and
for the radial h component's constant eta data. Other prolonged radial
coordinates, such as V(0,y)=2K(y), retain their actual data remainders.

=======================================================================
XII.E. ATOMIC PROOF CONTRACTS AND EVIDENCE BOUNDARY
=======================================================================

FC01 RESTRICTED_CONTRACTION_RESIDUAL_AND_PERTURBATION
  Input: C,R>0,L>=1, the exact CK parameter domain and a candidate a in D;
  for map perturbation, a second D-self-map with a proved D-fixed point.
  Output: (FC.A1)--(FC.A2), exact fixed-point uniqueness and uniform value
  versions. Body: triangle inequality and 1/4 contraction on the actual D.
  Support: ML43--ML47; closed-disc/self-map/metric adapters explicit in A1.

FC02 FINITE_RATIONAL_MAJORANT_ITERATION
  Input: FC01, J>=1, N>=0 and the actual finite rational map T^[J].
  Output: (FC.A3)--(FC.A6), denominator margin, finite-iterate error and
  fixed-point comparison. Body: positive tail, derivative estimate,
  complete-disc iteration and triangle inequality.
  Support: ML43--ML48; no local numerical or Lean run asserted.

FC03 ABSOLUTE_COEFFICIENT_BUDGET_AND_TAIL
  Input: CK08 positivity, CK09 coefficient domination and CK10's domain.
  Output: (FC.B1)--(FC.B5), including rectangular and total-degree tails.
  Body: finite nonnegative subsums, monotone radius limit, multinomial
  identity and a single coefficient-budget estimate.
  Support: ML41--ML42 and ML48 where geometric summation is used.

FC04 DIFFERENTIATED_CERTIFIED_TAILS
  Input: FC03, 0<=theta<eta<1 and finite derivative order (r,beta).
  Output: (FC.B6)--(FC.B7), with stated coordinate radii and factorials.
  Body: iterated actual Cauchy formula on an enclosing polydisc.
  Support: ML41--ML42; holomorphy supplied by CK10, not by the bound alone.

FC05 FINITE_QUASILINEAR_JET_CERTIFICATE
  Input: CK.B2, N>=1, degree(P)<=N and P(0,y)=0.
  Output: exact biconditional (FC.C1) and finite normal-degree checker order.
  Body: triangular normal recursion, finite analytic coefficient algebra,
  and reverse error-order calculation. Support: ML07--ML16, ML29.

FC06 QUANTITATIVE_POLYNOMIAL_RESIDUAL
  Input: FC05's proved certificate and CK06's actual coefficient constants.
  Output: (FC.C2)--(FC.C4), using FC.B7 and explicit norms.
  Body: exact residual difference, Cauchy derivative bounds and finite
  component sums. Support: ML11--ML16, ML41--ML42.

FC07 SECOND_ORDER_FINITE_JET_DEPENDENCY
  Input: an actual analytic CJ13 solution and the indicated finite data.
  Output: (FC.D2), equality transfer for mixed jets of total degree<=N.
  Body: induction on normal order with all tangential orders, actual
  finite chain/product partitions and jet-degree accounting.
  Support: ML07--ML16, ML29; no arbitrary-germ equality from finite jets.

FC08 DIRECT_SECOND_ORDER_JET_CERTIFICATE
  Input: CJ13's actual solution, N>=2 and polynomial Cauchy data (FC.D3).
  Output: exact biconditional (FC.D4), in the original dependent variables.
  Body: finite triangular recurrence and the reduced-jet error order.
  Support: FC07, ML07--ML16, ML29.

FC09 ACTUAL_INVERSE_AND_CURVATURE_JET_TRANSFER
  Input: invertible common metric value and equality of jets through N.
  Output: (FC.D5)--(FC.D6), and Ricci jet equality through N-2 for N>=2.
  Body: exact noncommutative inverse identity, coefficient recurrence and
  the actual coordinate Christoffel/Ricci definitions.
  Support: ML31--ML33, GE01--GE07 and finite derivative/product adapters.

FC10 ROOT_COMPLETION_AND_VACUUM_JET_CERTIFICATES
  Input: the actual source data of the unchanged root; scalar/radial
  polynomial certificates; GE11's actual vacuum conclusion.
  Output: exact scalar and radial finite jets, (FC.D7), and the correctly
  shifted component remainder bounds of D4.
  Body: FC08--FC09 plus CK12, original Cauchy data and actual source
  pressure construction. No additional root premise is introduced.

The finite coefficient identities remain exact equalities of the supplied
real or complex data. No decidability assumption for arbitrary real
coefficients, numerical tolerance or floating-point equality is introduced.

FINITE_CHECKER_SEMANTICS = MATHEMATICALLY_DERIVED.
FINITE_CHECKER_IMPLEMENTATION = NOT_CREATED_OR_EXECUTED.
EXTERNAL_CACHE_REUSE = REQUIRED_FOR_LATER_MATCHING_LEAN_RUNS.
LOCAL_LEAN_EXECUTION = NONE.
A_TIER_PROMOTION = NONE.

41. ROUND XIII: VERTICAL UNIQUENESS, COMPACT COLLARS AND EXACT GERM DESCENT

LEAN_CHECKPOINT CP41

  V16_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML03-ML04,ML26-ML28.

  V15_SOURCE_ENDPOINTS = ML50-ML54; ME21.

    immutable pins; no matching external Lean compilation or axiom audit.

This round refines CJ15 and the domain/quotient edges of the existing root.
The scalar equation, radial equation, normal orientation, torus marking,
analytic category, pressure gauge, parameters and inverse maps are unchanged.

=======================================================================
XIII.A. ONE NORMAL INTERVAL SUFFICES FOR EVERY SPATIAL OVERLAP
=======================================================================

A1. Vertical analytic continuation.

Let a,b>0, let U,V be open subsets of an initial-coordinate space, and let

  f:(-a,a) x U -> R^N,
  g:(-b,b) x V -> R^N

be jointly real analytic. Suppose that for every y in U intersect V the
two germs at (0,y) agree. Then

  f(s,y)=g(s,y)
    for |s|<min(a,b), y in U intersect V.              (GD.A1)

Proof. Fix y in U intersect V. The maps s |-> f(s,y) and s |-> g(s,y)
are actual real-analytic functions on the connected open interval
I=(-min(a,b),min(a,b)). Equality of the germs at (0,y) supplies equality
on an open normal interval about 0 after fixing this y. The real-analytic
identity principle on I gives equality for every s in I. The argument
applies separately to every y; no connectedness of U intersect V is used.
For a finite vector or tensor target, either apply the vector identity
principle or apply its scalar form componentwise. QED.

The source interface ML30 applies with U=I, base point z0=0 and actual
eventual equality in a neighborhood of 0. Restriction to a normal slice
is an analytic composition. ML30 does not obtain that initial eventual
equality from formal coefficients; CJ14/ML29 supplies that separate step.

A2. Equal analytic Cauchy data imply the overlap premise of A1.

Suppose f and g solve the same second-normal-order equation

  U_ss=F(s,y,U,U_s,D_y U,D_y^2 U,D_y U_s)

near {0} x (U intersect V), with the same analytic source coefficients,
the same two Cauchy traces and the prescribed open jet-domain condition.
For each y0 in U intersect V, equal trace functions give equal tangential
derivatives of every order at (0,y0). Repeated s differentiation of the
normal equation determines every subsequent pure normal derivative from
earlier normal derivatives and their tangential derivatives. Induction
therefore gives equality of all mixed derivatives at (0,y0). Existing
convergent analytic Taylor series and CJ14 give equality on a neighborhood
of (0,y0). Applying A1 gives (GD.A1) on the entire common product domain.

For scalar completion this is exactly CJ04. For radial metric completion
it is exactly CJ08. The recurrence, source agreement and analytic-series
identity are used only near the initial hypersurface. The continuation
step then compares already-existing analytic functions on a connected
normal interval; it introduces no second existence theorem.

Consequently, compact spatial overlaps, connected overlap components,
and additional pair-dependent shrinkings are unnecessary for product
domains centered on the same normal slice. A finite common radius still
remains necessary when initially different product radii are present.

=======================================================================
XIII.B. FINITE PRODUCT GLUING WITH AN EXPLICIT DOMAIN QUANTIFIER
=======================================================================

B1. Compact-base product gluing.

Let X be an initial-coordinate manifold and C subset X be nonempty and
compact. All solution values are expressed in one fixed finite-dimensional
real target space, using the marked scalar or tensor component trivialization;
overlap equality below means literal equality in that target. Suppose that
every y in C has an open neighborhood U_y in X and
a number e_y>0 on which a local analytic solution exists on

  (-e_y,e_y) x U_y.

Suppose that on overlaps the actual equations, initial traces and target
coordinate changes agree, and that A2 applies after expressing the two
solutions in the same coordinates. Choose a finite subcover

  C subset U_1 union ... union U_m, m>=1,

and set

  e=min(e_1,...,e_m)>0, O=U_1 union ... union U_m.      (GD.B1)

The local solutions agree on (-e,e) x (U_i intersect U_j) by A1-A2.
Define F(s,y) as the value of any local solution whose U_i contains y.
Pairwise equality proves that this definition is independent of the
index. On each open (-e,e) x U_i, F equals that local analytic solution;
thus F is analytic and satisfies the actual equation and prescribed data
on (-e,e) x O. These properties are local identities of finite derivatives.

If F' is another such construction, apply A2-A1 pointwise on the common
base region and the common normal interval. The resulting functions agree
there. All cover choices, CK radii and local representatives therefore
give the same germ along {0} x C. QED.

The only compactness use in this construction is the finite subcover
(ML20). Its finite minimum produces an actual positive radius. The
radius depends on the given local solutions and analytic data. No
uniform radius over a norm-bounded family follows from this argument.

B2. Normalized torus representation and translation transitions.

Let pi:R^3 -> T^3=(R/Z)^3 be the fixed marked quotient. For any open
time interval J, a torus field f:J x T^3 -> R^N pulls back to

  f_hat(t,x)=f(t,pi(x)),
  f_hat(t,x+n)=f_hat(t,x), n in Z^3.                 (GD.B2)

Conversely, a Z^3-periodic field f_hat defines f(t,[x])=f_hat(t,x), since
representatives of [x] differ by an integer vector. Every point of T^3
has a coordinate neighborhood obtained from a sufficiently small open
cube in R^3 on which pi is injective. Its inverse is a local affine
section of pi. Therefore f is jointly real analytic precisely when its
periodic pullback is jointly real analytic; finite coordinate derivatives
and pointwise tensor operations commute with both constructions.

Two lifted torus coordinate neighborhoods are related near each common
point by x |-> x+n for some fixed n in Z^3. The integer n is locally
constant and may differ between overlap components. On each such local
transition, its derivative is the identity and all higher derivatives
vanish. Hence the actual scalar equation, Gaussian radial equation,
eta, the marked tensor components and prescribed data transform by
ordinary translation. There are no additional connection terms.

Apply A2-A1 to every such transition. B1 then glues the solutions as
actual analytic torus fields. The resulting pullbacks satisfy (GD.B2)
on the entire common product collar. Equivalently, translation of the
periodic pullback has the same equation and data and is identified by
the same uniqueness argument. No connectedness assumption on chart
overlaps and no boundary-identification convention on a closed cube is
needed for analytic gluing.

The normalized spatial integral of a torus field is the integral of its
periodic pullback over a unit fundamental cube. The identified Fourier
coefficients use the same e^(2*pi*i*k.x) characters. Existing AF01-AF09
give the analytic Fourier interfaces. ME19 supplies concrete continuity,
surjectivity and quotient-map support for the unit-cube projection;
its statements alone do not supply the local analytic-section argument.
ME06 identifies periodic continuous fields from actual Fourier data.

=======================================================================
XIII.C. THE TWO COMPLETION DOMAINS, INCLUDING ALL MARGINS
=======================================================================

C1. Scalar completion on one full time cylinder.

Fix rho!=0 and an actual analytic periodic pair (u,p) on
J_delta0 x T^3, delta0>0. The normalized scalar traces are
psi(t0,x)=psi_t(t0,x)=0. At every x the normal coefficient is 2rho.
CK12 and the explicit scalar normal map give a local analytic solution
on a product neighborhood of (t0,x). Restrict each local product to the
given source interval. The translated normal coordinate s=t-t0 permits
B1 with C=T^3, while B2 proves compatibility of the periodic transitions.
Thus some delta1 with 0<delta1<=delta0 gives an actual analytic periodic
psi on J_delta1 x T^3, satisfying the actual scalar equation and both
zero Cauchy traces.

At t=t0, Delta_x psi=0 on T^3. The map (t,x) |-> Delta_x psi is continuous.
For every x choose a time-space neighborhood on which
|Delta_x psi|<|rho|/2. A finite spatial subcover and minimum time radius
give 0<delta2<=delta1 such that

  |rho+Delta_x psi(t,x)|>=|rho|/2>0
       for (t,x) in J_delta2 x T^3.                  (GD.C1)

The factor 2(rho+Delta psi) in the scalar normal equation therefore has
absolute value at least |rho|. No sign choice for rho is imposed.
The scalar solution is unique on every common cylinder on which two
such analytic solutions exist, by A2-A1. This proves the full global
periodic scalar-germ conclusion with all domain quantifiers explicit.

C2. Radial completion on a full marked product collar.

Let the analytic K constructed from the scalar solution exist on
J_delta2 x T^3 and satisfy the Hamiltonian and Codazzi identities. Choose

  0<a<delta2,
  C=[t0-a,t0+a] x T^3.                               (GD.C2)

Then C is compact and lies in the open tangential domain of K. At every
y=(t,x) in C, CK12 applied to the ten symmetric metric entries gives
a local solution of the radial normal equation with h(0,y)=eta and
h_r(0,y)=2K(y). B1-B2, with normal variable r, yield e>0 and one symmetric
analytic h on (-e,e) x O, where O is open and contains C. Restricting to

  (-e,e) x J_a x T^3

gives an actual product domain of the required marked form.

The Frobenius norm |h(r,y)-eta|_F is continuous and vanishes at r=0 for
every y in C. For each y in C, continuity gives a product neighborhood
on which this norm is <1/2. A finite subcover in C gives 0<e1<=e such
that throughout (-e1,e1) x C,

  ||h-eta||_op<=|h-eta|_F<1/2.                        (GD.C3)

CJ16 gives invertibility, ||h^(-1)||_op<=2, |det h|>=1/16 and inertia
(one negative, three positive). Thus g=dr^2+h has exactly one negative
direction on (-e1,e1) x J_a x T^3. The strict Frobenius bound is only a
sufficient Euclidean bound; it is not identified with the operator norm.

On this collar all inverse metric coefficients and actual curvature
expressions are analytic. GE07-GE11 identify E=G_rr and M_a=G_ra and show
that all their normal jets at r=0 vanish by the homogeneous subsidiary
system and the initial constraints. CJ14 gives local vanishing at each
(0,y). A1 applied to these already-existing analytic residuals gives
E=M=0 on the entire common normal interval for every y in J_a x T^3.
The finite-dimensional trace argument then gives Ric(g)=0 everywhere
on the stated collar. No additional subsidiary existence theorem is used.

Both a and e1 depend on the actual input domain and constructed analytic
fields. This result does not give a radial collar controlled solely by
a Sobolev norm, nor global time existence for the NSE solution.

=======================================================================
XIII.D. RESTRICTION NATURALITY AND THE EXACT GERM QUOTIENTS
=======================================================================

D1. Full-torus restriction commutes with every reconstruction operation.

Suppose two analytic periodic fields f,f' agree on J_b x T^3, b>0.
Equality on this open cylinder gives equality of every finite time and
spatial derivative there. Consequently their means agree at every time;
their Fourier coefficients agree for every k at every time; and their
actual mean-zero inverse Laplacians and Leray projections agree there.
The latter conclusions follow either from identical coefficient
multipliers and analytic synthesis or from the mean-zero Poisson
uniqueness identities already proved in AF05-AF09. Thus

  restriction o D = D o restriction,
  restriction o <.> = <.> o restriction,
  restriction o G = G o restriction,
  restriction o P = P o restriction,                 (GD.D1)

where G=Delta_0^(-1)Q0 on general scalar fields and is the original
Delta_0^(-1) on mean-zero fields. Finite sums, products, contractions,
trace removal and rational operations on their declared nonvanishing
domains have the same property. All equalities concern actual fields.

The full T^3 slice in the premise is essential. Equality of spatial germs
at one point does not determine a mean, G or P. These nonlocal operators
therefore descend from time germs of full spatial fields, or from germs
along the entire compact spatial slice; they are not asserted to act on
arbitrary pointwise spatial germs.

If metric representatives agree on a common open product collar, their
normal derivatives agree there and restriction to r=0 gives identical K
on the common full time cylinder. Applying (GD.D1) gives identical
beta,w,q,v,p_* and F_nu[K] on that cylinder. Consequently reconstruction
is independent of the chosen marked metric representative.

D2. Product collars form a genuine neighborhood basis for these germs.

For an open neighborhood W of {t0} x T^3, choose for each x a product
J_bx x U_x contained in W. Compactness gives finitely many U_x covering
T^3, and their positive minimum b gives J_b x T^3 subset W. Similarly,
for an open neighborhood of {0} x {t0} x T^3, choose finitely many
rectangles (-e_x,e_x) x J_bx x U_x and take positive minima of both
radii. Hence the product-collar germ relations used by the root equal
the usual neighborhood-of-compact-set germ relations.

An equivalent carrier uses time-family germs

  t |-> f(t,.) in (T^3 -> R^N)

at t0, and two-parameter family germs

  (r,t) |-> h(r,t,.)

at (0,t0). Eventual equality in these carriers means equality of full
spatial functions on a common neighborhood of the normal/time parameters.
The finite-radius observations above identify these models with the
original product-collar definitions. Analyticity is a property of an
actual representative on an open neighborhood, independent of arbitrary
values used to extend a partial representative outside that neighborhood.

D3. Explicit setoid proofs.

For analytic NSE representatives, define (u,p)~(u',p') iff there exist
b>0 and an analytic time function a on J_b, within both domains, with

  u'=u, p'=p+a(t) on J_b x T^3.                     (GD.D2)

Reflexivity uses a=0. Symmetry uses -a on the same domain. For transitivity,
restrict both witnesses to the positive minimum of their radii and add
their analytic gauge functions. This proves an equivalence relation.
Restriction of a representative to any smaller positive interval leaves
its class unchanged. The canonical pressure p-<p> is invariant under
(GD.D2), because <p+a>=<p>+a under normalized torus measure. Conversely,
equal velocities and equal normalized pressures on a common cylinder
give (GD.D2), with a=<p'>-<p>, analytic by AF09/CG04.

For marked metric representatives, define g~g' iff their actual h arrays
agree on some common positive product collar. Reflexivity and symmetry
are immediate; transitivity takes positive minima of the two normal
radii and the two time radii. No coordinate change or diffeomorphism is
included in this relation. Restriction leaves the class unchanged.

The same argument gives ordinary field germs without a pressure gauge.
ML22-ML23 and ML49 can package these already-proved invariance statements.
In particular, a generic Germ constructor or quotient eliminator does
not prove that a nonlocal spatial operator respects (GD.D2); D1 supplies
that exact body. For a field-family carrier, a fibrewise operator such
as P acts on the whole spatial field value, which correctly matches its
nonlocal spatial semantics.

=======================================================================
XIII.E. CHOICE INDEPENDENCE AND BOTH ACTUAL INVERSE IDENTITIES
=======================================================================

E1. Lift independence of every auxiliary local choice.

Fix an input representative. Any two scalar constructions use the same
actual equation and normalized data. A2-A1 identifies them on their
common full cylinder. The completed tensors B and K therefore agree
there. Any two radial constructions then have identical h(0)=eta,
h_r(0)=2K and radial equation; A2-A1 identifies their h arrays on the
common product collar. Thus charts, finite subcovers, complexification
domains, majorant bounds and CK radii do not alter the resulting metric
germ. Restricting the input representative changes none of these
comparisons, by D1 and the same two uniqueness applications.

Suppose now that (u',p')~(u,p), with u'=u and p'=p+a on a common cylinder.
Let f=-I2a, the analytic normalized double primitive, so f''=-a and
f(t0)=f'(t0)=0. The exact identity from CG07 gives

  S_rho[u,p+a]+C[psi+f]=S_rho[u,p]+C[psi].             (GD.E1)

The scalar psi+f has the prescribed zero traces and satisfies the new
scalar completion equation. Scalar uniqueness identifies it with the
scalar constructed for (u',p'). Hence the completed B and K are the
same. Radial uniqueness then gives equality of metric germs. This proves
that the original lift L is a function on the exact pressure-gauge
quotient, without any choice of pressure witness or extension radius
remaining in its value.

E2. Reconstruction and the first inverse.

For a representative in the fixed metric sector, D1 proves that the
actual formulas for v and p_* descend to its metric germ. The already-
proved boundary identities give NSE_nu(v,p_*) on a smaller common full
cylinder, so R has the exact required codomain. For the metric lifted
from (u,p), the actual reconstruction identities are

  q=psi-<psi>,
  v=P(u-grad psi_t)=u,
  p_*=p+partial_t^2<psi>.                             (GD.E2)

AF09 makes partial_t^2<psi> an analytic time function on a possibly
smaller common cylinder. Equations (GD.E2) satisfy the explicit witness
condition (GD.D2); therefore R(L([u,p]))=[u,p]. No pointwise choice of
pressure constants is needed.

E3. The second inverse on actual common domains.

Let g belong to the fixed sector. Actual reconstruction gives

  B=S_rho[v,p_*]+C[q], H(B)=0,
  q(t0,x)=q_t(t0,x)=0.                               (GD.E3)

The initial scalar coefficient is 2rho!=0. On a sufficiently small
common full cylinder, q and the scalar psi constructed from (v,p_*)
solve the same normal equation with the same Cauchy data. A2-A1 identifies
q=psi there. Consequently the original metric g and the metric obtained
by lifting its reconstruction have exactly the same actual h|0=eta and
h_r|0=2K. They both satisfy the radial normal equation. A2-A1, now in
the radial variable, identifies the actual h arrays on a common positive
product collar. This is precisely g~L(R(g)); hence L(R([g]))=[g].

The inverse proof uses only the original sector hypotheses and the
already-derived scalar/radial existence and uniqueness bodies. It does
not replace the sector by an image definition, weaken equality to a
diffeomorphism class, or require a separate global gauge choice.

=======================================================================
XIII.F. ATOMIC CONTRACTS AND SOURCE ASSIGNMENTS
=======================================================================

GD01 VERTICAL_ANALYTIC_IDENTITY
  Input: analytic maps on product domains with normal intervals centered
    at 0; equality of their germs at every common initial point.
  Output: equality on the full common normal interval over every overlap.
  Proof: A1; no connectedness of the spatial overlap.
  Support: ML26-ML27 for slice restriction; ML30 with actual eventual
    equality; scalar/vector analytic completeness hypotheses as recorded.

GD02 CK_PRODUCT_OVERLAP_AGREEMENT
  Input: the actual scalar or radial normal equation, equal analytic
    source coefficients and two Cauchy trace functions on the overlap.
  Output: the GD01 premise and therefore full product-overlap equality.
  Proof: A2, using CJ04/CJ08 and CJ14.
  Support: ML07-ML09, ML29; convergence comes from existing actual
    analytic solutions, not from a formal recurrence alone.

GD03 FINITE_COMMON_PRODUCT_COLLAR
  Input: a nonempty compact initial set and local product solutions with
    the GD02 overlap hypotheses in compatible coordinates.
  Output: one positive normal radius, an open neighborhood of the compact
    set, and a unique glued analytic actual solution there.
  Proof: B1; radius is the finite positive minimum, with no overlap
    shrink factors; analyticity and PDE identity are local.
  Support: ML20; ML21 is an alternative compact-neighborhood adapter.

GD04 MARKED_ANALYTIC_TORUS_DESCENT
  Input: periodic coordinate data, integer-translation transition maps,
    and actual analytic fields/equations in the marked coordinates.
  Output: mutually inverse torus/periodic representations, coordinate-
    derivative compatibility and translation-compatible solution gluing.
  Proof: B2; local affine sections plus GD02-GD03.
  Support: ME19 continuous quotient interfaces; ME06 Fourier injectivity;
    AF01-AF09 and ML01-ML09 for analytic Fourier semantics. ME19 alone
    proves no analytic local-section or nonlinear-PDE theorem.

GD05 UNIFORM_SCALAR_CYLINDER_AND_MARGIN
  Input: rho!=0 and an actual analytic periodic pair on J_delta0 x T^3.
  Output: some 0<delta<=delta0 and the unique actual normalized analytic
    scalar completion on J_delta x T^3, with |rho+Delta psi|>=|rho|/2.
  Proof: C1, CK12, GD02-GD04 and compact continuity.
  Support: ML20-ML21; scalar normal map CJ01-CJ04; no smallness premise.

GD06 UNIFORM_MARKED_VACUUM_COLLAR
  Input: analytic symmetric K on a time cylinder with the exact Hamiltonian and
    Codazzi constraints and the prescribed flat marked boundary data.
  Output: a common positive marked product collar carrying the actual
    analytic vacuum metric, inverse bound <=2, determinant magnitude
    >=1/16 and exactly one negative direction.
  Proof: C2, CK12, GD02-GD04, CJ16 and GE07-GE11.
  Support: ML20-ML21, ML31-ML33. Geometry and residual propagation remain
    the actual previously derived bodies, not quotient consequences.

GD07 FULL_SPATIAL_RESTRICTION_NATURALITY
  Input: equality on a common open cylinder with the entire T^3 slice,
    or equality of marked metrics on a common open product collar.
  Output: equality after every displayed derivative/mean/Fourier/G/P/
    tensor reconstruction operation; equality of the boundary K traces.
  Proof: D1 and the actual AF operator identities.
  Support: AF04-AF10, ML01-ML12, ME01-ME09, ME18. Mere equality near one
    spatial point does not satisfy the nonlocal operator premise.

GD08 EXACT_PRODUCT_GERM_SETOIDS
  Input: the analytic representative domains and marking of Section 0.
  Output: equivalence relations, product-neighborhood germ models,
    pressure normalization and restriction-invariant quotient carriers.
  Proof: D2-D3; finite minima; compact-neighborhood basis; analytic means.
  Support: ML20-ML25, ML49, CG04. Germ machinery packages the proved
    representative invariance and supplies no nonlocal semantic proof.

GD09 LIFT_CHOICE_AND_GAUGE_DESCENT
  Input: an NSE pressure-gauge germ with the original fixed parameters.
  Output: the exact lift is independent of charts, covers, radii,
    local CK constructions, representative restrictions and time gauge.
  Proof: E1; GD02-GD08 and CG06-CG09.
  Support: ML22-ML23, ML34-ML39, ML49; complete scalar and radial
    uniqueness bodies remain explicit dependencies.

GD10 RECONSTRUCTION_AND_TWO_INVERSE_GERM_BODIES
  Input: the exact normalized metric sector and the original lift.
  Output: well-defined R, R o L=id and L o R=id in the exact quotient
    carriers, hence the unchanged root equivalence.
  Proof: E2-E3; GD07-GD09 and the original actual boundary identities.
  Support: ML22-ML25, ML49. Equiv packaging occurs only after both
    actual inverse laws and all quotient invariance bodies are proved.

DETERMINISTIC_DOMAIN_SPINE:
  CJ04/CJ08 + CJ14 -> GD02; ML30 -> GD01;
  GD01-GD02 + finite compact cover -> GD03;
  marked translation semantics -> GD04;
  CK12 + GD01-GD04 -> GD05 -> actual completed K;
  CK12 + GD01-GD04 + CJ16 + GE07-GE11 -> GD06;
  AF operator identities -> GD07 -> GD08;
  GD05-GD08 + CG06-CG09 -> GD09;
  actual reconstruction identities + GD07-GD09 -> GD10 -> exact T_EQ.

PLACEMENT_REFINEMENTS:
  CP00 / NP01: GD04, GD07-GD08; ML20-ML23,ML49; ME06,ME18-ME19.
  CP01 / NP02: GD07; nonlocal spatial semantics require full-slice germs.
  CP07 / NP05 / CJ15: GD01-GD05; ML20-ML21,ML29-ML30.
  CP09 / NP07 / CJ15-CJ16: GD01-GD04,GD06; ML20-ML21,ML29-ML33.
  CP10 / NP08: GD07-GD09; ML22-ML23,ML34-ML39,ML49.
  CP11 / NP09: GD07-GD10; ML22-ML25,ML49.
  CP28 / CJ15: GD01-GD06 replace compact-overlap bookkeeping by
    vertical analytic continuation plus the finite local-radius minimum.
  CP35 / CK12: GD05-GD06 supply the exact compact global domains.

SECTION_MATHEMATICAL_REGISTER:
  VERTICAL_ANALYTIC_OVERLAP_IDENTITY                 GD01-GD02 / A1-A2
  FINITE_COMMON_COLLAR_WITH_MARKED_TORUS_DESCENT      GD03-GD04 / B1-B2
  UNIFORM_NORMALIZED_COMPLETION_DOMAINS              GD05-GD06 / C1-C2
  EXACT_NONLOCAL_OPERATOR_AND_GERM_DESCENT           GD07-GD08 / D1-D3
  CHOICE_FREE_LIFT_AND_TWO_INVERSE_GERM_IDENTITIES   GD09-GD10 / E1-E3

A. MATHLIB: COMPLETE-SUBSET CONTRACTION AND CERTIFIED ERROR

COMMON_CONTRACTING_SOURCE:
  IMPORT = Mathlib.Topology.MetricSpace.Contracting
  REVISION = db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d
  BLOB_SHA = c4bd6fe2e61b904dfa1144ab1b7162550c99d1c3
  SOURCE = https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/db584cd6d46c92f209a44c0f1c829460d327499d/Mathlib/Topology/MetricSpace/Contracting.lean
  DIRECT_IMPORTS:
    Mathlib.Analysis.SpecificLimits.Basic
    Mathlib.Data.Setoid.Basic
    Mathlib.Dynamics.FixedPoints.Topology
    Mathlib.Topology.MetricSpace.Lipschitz

SOURCE_DEFINITION:
  def ContractingWith [EMetricSpace α] (K : ℝ≥0) (f : α → α) :=
    K < 1 ∧ LipschitzWith K f

ADAPTER_REQUIRED_FOR_ALL_CONTRACTION_RECORDS:
  For each fixed parameter (s,z) in the CK.B6 parameter polydisc, let
    D := {w : C : |w| <= W}.
  W>0, so D is nonempty; it is closed and complete in C.
  Prove T_(s,z)(D) subset D from the explicit |T(w)|<=W/2 bound.
  Prove the Lipschitz estimate on D from the actual complex derivative
  bound and integration on the convex line segment between w,w' in D.
  This yields ContractingWith (1/4) for the subtype self-map D->D.
  The derivative estimate on D does not prove that T is a contraction
  on all of C. No global extension is required to be contracting.
  Pointwise metric estimates can then be bounded uniformly over (s,z).
  Joint holomorphy, complex derivatives, coefficient positivity and PDE
  compatibility remain the actual CK/FC proofs, not fixed-point corollaries.

SOURCE_STATEMENT_BEGIN
theorem exists_fixedPoint' {s : Set α} (hsc : IsComplete s) (hsf : MapsTo f s s)
    (hf : ContractingWith K <| hsf.restrict f s s) {x : α} (hxs : x ∈ s) (hx : edist x (f x) ≠ ∞) :
    ∃ y ∈ s, IsFixedPt f y ∧ Tendsto (fun n ↦ f^[n] x) atTop (𝓝 y) ∧
      ∀ n : ℕ, edist (f^[n] x) y ≤ edist x (f x) * (K : ℝ≥0∞) ^ n / (1 - K)
SOURCE_STATEMENT_END
PLACEMENT = CK.B7/CK07 and Section 40 FC, complete-disc scalar majorant existence and a priori geometric convergence.
REMAINING_OBLIGATION = Supply actual complete D, MapsTo T D D, restricted contraction, x=0 in D and finite edist. Convert the ENNReal bound to the actual real complex-norm bound. Identify this fixed point with the independently derived holomorphic majorant using uniqueness. No joint parameter holomorphy is contained in the endpoint.

[ML45]

with forced boundary/vacuum factorization, complete fibres and actual
linearized equivalence; the original canonical root is retained.

  Existing parent contracts: NP01-NP16 = 16.
  Existing atoms: AF11 + CJ16 + ST8 + CG10 + GE12 + CK12 = 69.
  Additional atoms: HD10 + FC10 + GD10 = 30.

The atom identifiers are mathematical proof-body contracts, not names of
pre-existing Lean declarations. Each contract's exact scope and complete
mathematical proof occur at its indexed section. The new stress and finite
approximation theorems are additional corollaries/implementation interfaces;
their hypotheses are not inserted into the root theorem. GD refines the
previously required compact-domain and germ bodies without changing them.

43.2. Stress refinement branch.

  fixed real unit e + actual complex STF matrix
    -> HD01 -> HD02 -> HD03;
  HD03 -> HD04 (sharp constants);
  HD01-HD03 -> HD05 -> HD06 (inverse/minimum/distance identities);
  actual pressure gradient and residual definitions + HD05
    -> HD07 -> HD08;
  actual W_sigma norms + HD01-HD02 + AF02-AF08
    -> HD09 -> HD10 with AF01/AF09 and actual synthesis.

The actual canonical condition remains F_nu[K]=0. Its four-component
description is equivalent only after all four components vanish. Projected
forcing zero and full divergence zero retain their distinct weaker scopes.
No arbitrary tensor is promoted to an Einstein-boundary witness.

43.3. Finite certificate branch.

  CK06-CK10 + actual restricted contraction
    -> FC01 -> FC02;
  CK08 positivity + CK09 domination + CK10 actual convergence
    -> FC03 -> FC04;
  actual zero-data quasilinear equation + finite normal recursion
    -> FC05; FC03-FC05 -> certified Taylor approximation;
  FC04-FC05 + actual shifted coefficient Cauchy bounds
    -> FC06;
  actual CJ13 normal equation -> FC07 -> FC08;
  actual matrix inverse and curvature definitions -> FC09;
  CK12 + FC08-FC09 + actual source K + GE11 -> FC10.

FC05 and FC08 compare finite polynomials with the actual unique analytic
solution already established by CK. They do not use finite residual
vanishing to construct that solution. The coefficient induction decreases
normal order, so there is no recursion cycle caused by mixed derivatives.
The finite checker may later be implemented and proved correct independently.
Its output has no formal status until that exact checker-to-coefficient
interface and the actual output have passed the matching external kernel.

A finite Ricci jet vanishing through N-2 is precisely that conclusion.
It does not imply a vacuum collar, global geometric admission, or equality
of analytic germs from finitely many derivatives. Exact rational scalar
iteration likewise supplies its displayed error bound, not an exact
solution predicate for the iterate.

43.4. Compact-domain and actual germ branch.

  CK/CJ normal uniqueness + actual analytic Taylor identity -> GD02;
  one-variable analytic identity -> GD01;
  GD01-GD02 + finite compact cover -> GD03;
  marked integer translations + actual local affine charts -> GD04;
  scalar CK12 + GD01-GD04 + rho!=0 -> GD05;
  actual analytic symmetric completed K + radial CK12
    + GD01-GD04 + CJ16 + GE07-GE11 -> GD06;
  actual full-torus operator identities -> GD07 -> GD08;
  GD05-GD08 + exact CG normalized pressure gauge -> GD09;
  actual original reconstruction identities + GD07-GD09 -> GD10.

The vertical identity argument uses equality on a genuine initial
neighborhood and continuation on a connected normal interval. It does
not assume spatial overlap connectedness or a uniform initial Taylor
radius across every overlap point. Compactness supplies only finitely
many local product radii and their positive minimum. The source-data
dependence of all radii remains explicit.

43.5. Deterministic final root composition.

  1. Actual jointly analytic periodic NSE representative, nu>0,rho!=0,t0.
  2. AF/CG: actual canonical pressure and velocity-germ representation.
  3. CJ03 + CK12 + GD05: actual normalized periodic scalar completion.
  4. Actual S+C conservation/Hamiltonian algebra: analytic symmetric K
     with the exact Codazzi and Hamiltonian constraints.
  5. CJ07 + CK12 + GD06: actual analytic Lorentzian Gaussian vacuum collar.
  6. GD09: exact well-defined lift on the original pressure-gauge quotient.
  7. Actual boundary residual algebra + GD07: exact reconstruction.
  8. GD10: R o L=id and L o R=id in the original marked germ carriers.
  9. ML24-ML25: package only these already-proved maps and inverse bodies.

The root conclusion is exactly

  forall nu>0, forall rho!=0, forall t0:
    N^omega_(nu,t0) ~= G^omega_(nu,rho,t0).

There is no additional input certificate, checker admission, arbitrary
pressure witness, new geometric equivalence relation or weakened G4 in
this mathematical theorem. Optional finite/quantitative corollaries may
be implemented as separate proof branches after their prerequisites.

A_TIER = NOT_ESTABLISHED.
